## Primary findings and qualifications

The all-weight Frobenius realization problem has a direct unconditional solution: extract an i-th root, apply Honda–Tate in weight1, and use the i-fold product. This argument is already present in Milne (1994, Proposition2.6, proof). It gives m=1. The class group of the originally chosen CM field cannot obstruct a realization that is allowed to use a larger number field.

The corrected Deuring rule is the crystalline cup-trace pairing transported from the uniquely reconstructed oriented supersingular curve. We prove independence, the exact unit-squareclass torsor, field-automorphism invariance, and Morita norm compatibility. The literal finite-field crystal domain has an explicit p=3 counterexample, and the maximal quadratic-order/descent equivalence has an explicit F7 counterexample. The ramified F9 base change also refutes the universal Picard assertion.

The cycle analysis separates algebraic-equivalence counterexamples from unresolved assertions about algebraic classes in cohomology. An incidence blow-up construction shifts the quintic Griffiths group and gives failure of universal A in every nontrivial degree range. This is not evidence for failure of the distinct higher injectivity statement. Modern integral curve-class counterexamples on very general principally polarized abelian varieties are taken from the opened 2026 version of Engel–de Gaay Fortman–Schreieder, with their preprint status explicit.

The complex Albanese-family converse is refuted even rationally by E.3u. A specified degree14 hypersurface in split Q6 carries a two-plane difference whose Betti class is zero but whose Lawson s-image is nonzero. Its product with an elliptic point gives a whole zero-cylinder family detected by full morphic Abel–Jacobi. This proves that the generated Albanese relation is strictly smaller than the finite Walker kernel; the previously isolated generation gate is false. The forward theorem in every characteristic and the exact fixed-variety/support statements remain valid.

## Principal findings

For a positive-weight Weil integer λ, choose α with α^i=λ. Integrality and the absolute-value condition imply that α is a weight-one Weil q-integer. Honda–Tate gives A/F_q, and H^1(A)^{⊗i} embeds as a Künneth summand in H^i(A^i). This is already implicit explicitly in Milne's Proposition 2.6 proof (there via restriction of scalars). It settles the supposed all-weight realization difficulty without a class-group argument. The prior sweep's assertion that higher-weight strong realization was open is wrong.

The lattice cokernel is not merely its image in Cl(L): its kernel is `(N(E_{L,S}) ∩ E_K)/N(E_L)`, and the full group extension is specified by a kernel presentation. This retains the 2-primary information rather than discarding it. A Smith-normal-form recipe is included for arbitrary finite Galois CM L.

R7's claimed point of D(M0/torsion) evaluating [u] as u is not defined, since [u]=[ζu]. Also D(M0/torsion) is the connected weight quotient, not automatically the connected kernel of the Tate character. A corrected powered point evaluates [u] as u^k for |μ(L)| dividing k. For the divisor isogeny its rational lift is characterized by finite norm-one equations over the decomposition field. The exponent `2 h_L |D_p| |μ(L)|` suffices (h_L may be replaced by the class-group exponent). For normalization in ker ε, the corresponding even power is specified separately. Merely changing q while resetting the full norm-one group changes nothing; base change acts through u↦u^k.

The full output is in `output/verify_frobenius_cases.json` and `.txt`. Runtime for the recorded run: 0.160232 seconds, SymPy 1.14.0. All assertions passed.

The cohomological significance of the 20×20 matrix is proved in B.3b: it is ∧³ of the H¹ Frobenius companion matrix.

### Record: agents/frobenius/A_review.md

# Independent review of Part A and additional primary-source ledger

AI-produced by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), 19 November 2026, child agent `/root/frobenius`. This is an independent mathematical check within the same AI run, not human review. We have not modified the parent master or its `A_deuring.tex`.

## 1. Main findings

1. **A.5 has a counterexample under the general module-category definition of 15:16.** The complete proof is `A5_counterexample.tex`, paragraphs A.5c–d. It uses the actual reduction modulo 3 of the global maximal-order category, then scalar extension to \(\mathbb F_9\). Its determinant-rigidified autoequivalence category has noncommutative \(\pi_0\), so it cannot be a Picard category. The exact executable certificate is `code/verify_ramified_picard_counterexample.py`, with output in `output/verify_ramified_picard_counterexample.json`.

2. **A.2c, A.3a and A.4a have the correct distinction between a sign class and a sign automorphism.** Positivity chooses the class in \(H^1(\mathbb R,\mu_2)\); the chosen realization still has automorphism group \(H^0(\mathbb R,\mu_2)=\{\pm1\}\). No unique natural isomorphism exists.

3. **The global quotient in A.2b needs its positive transport stated explicitly.** The acting global category is \(\operatorname{Eq}(C)\), mapped into \(\operatorname{Eq}(C,T)\) by the canonical positive determinant normalization, exactly as written at 15:48. If one instead lets every possible determinant rigidification of a global equivalence act, the negative rigidification supplies an additional translation and changes the quotient.

4. **A.1d is an explicit geometric rule.** A terminating reconstruction makes it an explicit mathematical prescription. A program need not have been executed for every prime to prove this statement. An implementation limit should not be promoted to an undecided mathematical claim. The literal unrestricted finite-field version remains false by A.1a, independently of this assessment.

## 2. A.5: complete scope and determinant check

The transcription at 15:16 visibly brackets the Dedekind/maximal-order condition (a) and expressly separates it from the module-category conditions (b),(c). At 15:34 the proposed rigidified equivalences are invertible bimodules with a specified isomorphism of their reduced-norm line with the base ring. At 15:36 the base is an arbitrary commutative algebra and the Picard claim is marked “À vérifier!!”. We use those precise formulations.

For the global maximal order

\[
 O=\mathbb Z\langle1,i,(1+j)/2,(i+ij)/2\rangle
 \subset(-1,-3)_{\mathbb Q},
\]

put \(R=\mathbb F_3[\alpha]/(\alpha^2+1)\), \(B=O\otimes R\). Because 2 is invertible, its local generators give the algebra with basis \(e_0,e_1,x,y\), where \(x=e_0xe_1\), \(y=e_1ye_0\), and the radical has square zero. The reduced norm is the degree-two polynomial \(N(a,b,c,d)=ab\). It is not the determinant of the rank-four regular representation.

Every global rank-one projective right \(O\)-module becomes free at 3. Base change of its Hom modules is exact because the source is projective. Thus the base-changed category, not just a newly invented one-object category, is equivalent to the category of free rank-one right \(B\)-modules. Over the field \(R\) these are precisely the modules locally free of rank one over \(\operatorname{Spec}R\).

The reference object \(X=O\) has identity basis and positive norm generator 1, so \(T_R=\nu(X_R)\) is fixed without an extra scalar. Each norm-preserving automorphism \(\rho\) has a specific rigidification \(u_\rho=1\), rather than merely a transported norm line that happens to be abstractly trivial. The induced determinant map of a natural isomorphism represented by \(b\in B^*\) is \(N(b)\). Hence the precise rigidification condition is \(N(b)=1\). Tensor products of the chosen rigidifications remain 1.

Let \(t=1+\alpha\), of order eight. Let \(S\) exchange both vertices and both arrows, and let \(T_t\) fix the vertices and \(y\), while scaling \(x\) by \(t\). Both preserve \(N\). Their products obey

\[
 T_tS=\operatorname{Int}(d)ST_t,
 \qquad d=te_0+e_1,
 \qquad N(d)=t.
\]

Commuting with \(e_0\) kills both radical coordinates of a central element, and commuting with \(x\) makes its two diagonal coordinates equal. Thus \(Z(B)=R\) exactly. It follows that every intertwiner between the products is \(cd\), \(c\in R^*\), with norm \(c^2t\). This is never 1. Therefore these two rigidified classes do not commute.

The finite certificate enumerates all 6561 elements, all 5184 units, the entire centre, and all eight possible intertwiners. Their norms are exactly the four nonsquares. The first run completed in 0.168108 seconds using only the Python standard library. The proof does not rely on trusting the finite search.

**Qualification.** The algebra \(B\) is not Azumaya, and hence cannot satisfy condition (a) over a field. The counterexample therefore refutes the general formulation using (b),(c), as well as the claim that every ramified base change inherits the Picard structure. It does not refute the theorem for strict maximal-order or Azumaya categories. In those restricted domains the argument under condition (C) remains applicable. A statement that retains the universal quantifier must name which version of “Deuring category” it means.

## 3. A.2c: identification of the section torsor

The product decomposition is a decomposition into the two open-and-closed factors \(A''\) and \(\mathbb Z_p\). Away from \(p\), determinant-compatible Tate realizations give the comparison object between the determinant splitting gerbe and the square-root gerbe of the Tate line. At infinity the positive quaternionic realization gives its counterpart. At \(p\) the comparison gerbe is defined to be \(B\mu_2\), so its unit object is available. These are objects of the comparison gerbe, not a neutralization of the ramified quaternion Brauer gerbe at \(p\).

Given any base object in a banded abelian gerbe, twisting by a band torsor identifies its objects with torsors. Thus isomorphism classes of sections form a torsor under the stated \(H^1\). To make the identification with \(\mathcal T\) explicit, choose the crystalline parity-zero representative whose determinant rule is \(\xi_p\). Its remaining choices are precisely the unit squareclasses. Replacing it by the parity-one representative translates all local comparison data by \(f\); quotienting by \(\langle f\rangle\) therefore removes this choice. The unit section at \(p\) corresponds to \(\xi_p\), not to a coordinate-independent numerical unit.

The global equivalences used in this identification have the canonical **positive** determinant transport. We verified this against the actual map \(\operatorname{Eq}(C)\to\operatorname{Eq}(C,T)\) in the transcription at 15:48. It should be stated in A.2b to avoid adding an unwanted global negative rigidification.

## 4. A.3a and A.4a: sign and covariance

For \(V_E=\operatorname{Hom}(E_0,E)\otimes\mathbb R\), the right action and the form

\[
 \langle x,y\rangle=\beta(x^\vee y)
\]

obey \(\langle xa,yb\rangle=\bar a\langle x,y\rangle b\). A nonzero rational homomorphism identifies this form with a positive multiple of the reduced norm on \(\mathbb H\), so it is positive definite after extension to \(\mathbb R\). Postcomposition by \(f\) has adjoint \(f^\vee\) and multiplier \(\deg f\). The target must contain all quaternionic linear maps: the isometry-only category would omit nontrivial isogenies.

An intertwiner between two such functors exists by Morita equivalence and real Skolem–Noether. Naturality forces all intertwiners to differ by a central nonzero real scalar. Unit norm therefore leaves exactly two, differing by \(-1\). This is a \(\pi_1\) ambiguity. The separate \(\pi_0\) ambiguity consists of the two signs of a determinant trivialization; a quaternionic linear change of basis has positive norm, so positivity distinguishes the two classes. The current claims in A.3a and A.4a correctly keep these statements separate.

## 5. A.1d/e: explicitness and a more concrete intertwiner step

The previously found orientation issue has been repaired in the current parent draft: the action of **raw** \(a\), not \(\bar a\), on \(M/FM\) gives the tangent orientation when the covariant convention is \(D(a)=(a^\vee)^*\). The Hodge quotient is \(H^1(E,\mathcal O_E)=\operatorname{Lie}(E^\vee)\); pullback by \(a^\vee\) differentiates its dual \(a\). This verifies the sign without importing an incompatible convention for Dieudonné modules.

The formula using a marked curve, its crystalline cup-trace pairing, and an \(O,F\)-intertwiner is choice-independent by the scalar centralizer \(\mathbb Z_p^*\). The local classification plus oriented Deuring theorem supplies the required choices. The positive degree identity \(\deg(ta)=\deg(t)\operatorname{Nrd}(a)\) gives the norm transition functions. Primitivity of the degree ideal, using integral Tate/Dieudonné Hom comparisons at each prime, removes a hidden multiplicative unit in Morita compatibility.

The linear-algebra step can be made more explicit. First solve the \(O\)-linear equations over \(W\) for an integral invertible \(b_0:D(E)\to M\). Such a solution exists by the proved classification. The Hom lattice is free of rank one over \(W\), since its rational centralizer is scalar. There is a unit \(c\in W^*\) with

\[
 F_Mb_0=c\,b_0F_E.
\]

The valuation of \(c\) is zero because both determinants have valuation one. Set \(b=ab_0\), where

\[
 \sigma(a)/a=c^{-1}.
\]

The first equation modulo \(p\) is \(a^{p-1}=\bar c^{-1}\). Successive Witt digits are solved by Artin–Schreier equations. Every partial solution extends because the residue field is algebraically closed. This gives the intertwiner without an unspecified nonlinear search. Comparing the determinant with any fixed Tate determinant isomorphism gives a unit in \(\mathbb Z_p^*\); its class needs only reduction modulo \(p\) for odd \(p\), or modulo 8 at \(p=2\).

An effective input model for arbitrary Witt coefficients is needed to call this a uniform computer algorithm. The mathematical construction needs no computability hypothesis. The absence of an implemented all-prime crystalline evaluator is a disclosure about computation, not a gap in the choice-independent rule or the terminating geometric reconstruction. The prompt itself supplies no numerical input pair for which a value was left unevaluated. The literal claim on every finite-field pair is separately refuted by A.1a.

## 6. Additional opened primary sources

All sources below were opened and their relevant statements read on 19 November 2026. Exact source locators are given so the parent can add the appropriate citations without relying on our summaries.

Japan, 20*(1–2), 83–95, DOI 10.2969/jmsj/02010083 | [Full primary paper, mirrored PDF](https://scispace.com/pdf/isogeny-classes-of-abelian-varieties-over-finite-fields-2qwbdw79so.pdf), **Main Theorem, p. 83**; Propositions 1–4, p. 85; Theorem 1, p. 86; Theorem 2, p. 87. Publisher [metadata page](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/jmath1948/20/1-2/20_1-2_83/_article/-char/en) also opened. | The Main Theorem gives the bijection between simple finite-field isogeny classes and integral Weil numbers. The root construction in B.1 therefore uses the original theorem directly. Theorem 1 allows an extension field; it does not eliminate a fixed-field class obstruction. |

It does not prove all of these hypotheses or determine an integral unit squareclass \(\xi_p\). The later point description is conjectural there. |

Soc., 30*(3), 819–914, DOI 10.1090/jams/867 | [Author's full PDF](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~kisin/dvifiles/lr.pdf), **Theorem (0.3)**, **Theorem (2.2.3)**, **Theorem (4.6.7)**. Journal metadata also [opened](https://geodesic.mathdoc.fr/articles/10.1090/jams/867/). | For \(p>2\), abelian type and hyperspecial level, the point description is proved with the stated \(I^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb A_f)\)-conjugation ambiguity. Theorem (2.2.3) gives a CM lift inside an isogeny class. These are not integral all-prime determinant-normalization theorems. |

The three successfully downloaded PDFs and their text extractions are in `sources/`. Honda's complete paper was opened through the web retrieval service; its mirror refused the separate screenshot request, and we do not claim a local PDF copy. Attempts at Rochester and J-STAGE PDFs repeatedly timed out; Project Euclid returned a service notice. These failures do not affect the successful full-text opening of the mirrored primary paper. The Orgogozo SGA 4 site returned 502 errors; the Laszlo retypeset volume succeeded. Harvard DASH failed for Kisin; the author's Harvard PDF succeeded.

### The exact point-case consequence of SGA 4

Let \(\mathcal P\) be a strictly commutative Picard category. By Proposition 1.4.15 it corresponds to a two-term complex \(K\) of abelian groups, with \(H^{-1}(K)=\pi_1\mathcal P\) and \(H^0(K)=\pi_0\mathcal P\). Its truncation triangle has extension class in

\[
 \operatorname{Ext}^2_{\mathbb Z}(\pi_0\mathcal P,\pi_1\mathcal P)=0.
\]

The equality follows because every abelian group has a free presentation whose kernel, as a subgroup of a free abelian group, is free. The triangle therefore splits and \(K\simeq\pi_1\mathcal P[1]\oplus\pi_0\mathcal P\). This proves the split assertion used in R1. The splitting is not canonical in general, and no such conclusion follows if strict commutativity has not first been established. A.5c fails before this result could be applied.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C1_endpoint_image_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.1n

The current endpoint-image argument in research/agents/cycles/endpoint_image.tex passes. The proposed stronger fixed-target formulation also passes: for any fixed smooth projective connected complex X and codimension r in its range, the image criterion for every smooth projective source, source codimension, and correspondence of the indicated shift is equivalent to universal numerical equals Betti homological equivalence D_C together with the vanishing of the rational Griffiths kernel in codimension r on X.

For necessity of D_C, choose a numerically trivial but Betti-nonzero a of codimension s on a d-fold Y, let j=d-s, and take a times h_X^r. The correspondence has codimension d+r-j. Its cycle action is zero by numerical triviality, while Poincare duality supplies a rational cohomological preimage of the nonzero h_X^r class. The preimage need not be algebraic; exactly that mismatch contradicts the proposed image criterion. This works also in target codimension zero, for a zero-dimensional connected target, and for projective-space targets in every codimension. Taking the zero correspondence proves necessity of the target Griffiths-kernel vanishing.

For sufficiency, Jannsen's semisimplicity theorem applies to all rational numerical motives without assuming algebraic Kunneth projectors. Under D_C on every product, the ordinary total graded Betti realization is well defined and faithful: cohomological Kunneth and Poincare duality identify the total class of a correspondence with its total graded action. Any additive functor out of a semisimple abelian category preserves the split exact sequences used here. Choose a generalized inverse to the correspondence morphism to form the image projector p. The realized class of z is fixed by p, and faithfulness gives (1-p)z=0 as a morphism from the unit motive. The resulting numerical-cycle preimage has the required source degree because morphisms from the unit to h(Y)(j) are precisely numerical codimension-j cycles. Under D_C, its discrepancy from z is homologically trivial; target Griffiths-kernel vanishing makes that discrepancy algebraically trivial.

The use of only degree zero of the twisted realization is legitimate: the unit motive has no other realized degree, and correspondences preserve the grading after the stated Tate shifts. No motivic Kunneth decomposition has been introduced. Numerical equals algebraic equivalence rationally for target codimensions 0, 1, and n, so the asserted endpoint equivalences follow; the same holds in every codimension for projective space. The known intermediate-dimensional counterexamples and the conditional endpoint criterion are logically compatible.

Fresh primary opening: Jannsen, U. (1992). Motives, numerical equivalence, and semi-simplicity. Inventiones Mathematicae,107,447–452. https://epub.uni-regensburg.de/26642/1/jannsen10.pdf ; retrieval turn324735366view0. Theorem1 is printed on p448; its proof, including the semisimple-category lemma, occupies pp448–449. Its introduction explicitly distinguishes this result from the standard conjectures. This exact theorem is sufficient for the motive-category step above.

# Independent review of C.1o and C.1p

Reviewed the complete typed fragment `research/agents/cycles/positivity_stabilization.tex` and its exact middle-radical dependency C.1j. Both statements pass as written. They give equivalences and specified positive examples, not a proof of the universal strict-positivity assertion.

## C.1o

The projective-bundle decomposition preserves rational homological and algebraic equivalence. For a middle class written as a sum of coefficients times powers of the projective hyperplane, the displayed coefficient recursion is exact, including its first equation. The last coefficient has degree `(n-d)/2`; it determines every other coefficient, and the remaining equation is precisely multiplication by `h^(d+1)`. When `d>n`, the negative degree of that last coefficient forces all coefficients to vanish. The displayed map has coefficient one in its top projective-degree term, so it is an actual isomorphism of the indicated rational groups.

C.1j identifies that primitive Griffiths kernel with the positivity radical by the Hodge--Riemann relations. Thus the universal equivalence follows in both directions. The deduction of the injectivity part of the letter's A uses cohomological hard Lefschetz only to put the starting class in the Griffiths group; the additional power of h then invokes the proved equivalent injectivity. No surjectivity is claimed. The threefold stabilizations use only the vanishing of G^0 and G^1. Every ample class on the product has the stated form, and the distinction between a fixed variety and a universal premise is correctly retained.

## C.1p

The exact primary input was freshly opened: Beauville (2010), *The action of SL(2) on abelian varieties*, published PDF https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/u/beauvill/pubs/SLA-rms.pdf, Theorem 4.2 and Proposition 5.2, pp. 260--261, and footnote 1, p. 254. The source allows replacement of rational equivalence by any adequate equivalence. Its finite-dimensional strings have length `g+s-2a` for a lowest vector of degree a and Beauville index s; the correspondences preserve both groups used to form the Griffiths quotient.

For a string with s at most zero, the choice `d=g-2a` is nonnegative, has the correct parity, and its `(d+1)`-st Lefschetz power vanishes. Conversely, for s at least one, the vanishing inequality is `a>r+s-1`, incompatible with `a<=r`. This proves the equivalence without a Beauville vanishing hypothesis. The cohomological eigenvalue comparison identifies all nonzero-index pieces with Griffiths pieces and identifies the grade-zero obstruction with the kernel of its cycle map. The first tests in dimensions four and five are exactly the possible lowest-vector strings; no omitted positive-index string contributes a kernel. The low-dimensional unconditional conclusion follows because the lowest degree would be at most one.

The full source was opened as `turn432859706view0`, with exact theorem passages in `turn407561423view0` and `turn407561423view1`. This review does not add a claim of priority or a closure of the remaining positivity problem.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C1q_C2k_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent review: C.1q and C.2k

Reviewer: `/root/frobenius`. Review date: 2026-11-19. Both complete typed fragments passed independent proof and source checks. This review does not claim any closure beyond their stated scopes.

## C.1q: blow-up positivity

Reviewed `agents/cycles/blowup_positivity.tex` in full.

With the projective bundle of lines in the normal bundle and ξ=c1(O(1)), the exceptional normal line is O(-1). Consequently j*E=−ξ and the excess bundle is p*N/O(-1). Its top Chern class is the stated positive-sign polynomial in ξ. The resulting top exceptional relation is therefore

f_c(z)=π*i*z−Σ_{k=1}^{c−1}f_{c−k}(c_k(N)z).

The ambient term vanishes under the actual G^{m+1}(M)=0 hypothesis. The coefficient equations for multiplication by L/b give the stated downward recurrence. The final coefficient is the negative of q_N(−u)z, which has exactly the asserted zero locus; no sign correction is needed in the kernel statement. Extraction of the last exceptional coefficient and reconstruction are inverse, including negative controlling degrees.

The controlling degree m−c+1=(dim Z−c+2)/2 is at or below the middle of the center. Thus merely importing an above-middle Griffiths class cannot yield the claimed primitive middle radical. The proof does not overstate this limitation as a vanishing theorem for arbitrary centers.

For a smooth transverse pencil base cut out by two sections of H, the blow-up is the closed graph in M×P1. The line bundle 2π*H−E is the restriction of H⊠O(1), hence ample. Its exceptional polynomial vanishes, and weak Lefschetz supplies the stated vanishing of H^{2m−3}(Z) if the ambient cohomology is Tate.

For V×P^r inside P4×P^{r+c−1}, the normal bundle and dimensions are correct. G^2(V) is the only possible rational Griffiths group, and multiplication by H annihilates it because G^3(V)=0. The coefficient calculation reduces exactly to (1−β/b)^{c−1}. A line in the second factor through the linear center and not contained in it has strict-transform degree β−b, so ampleness imposes strict positivity. The exponent e+c−1 remains within the projective-bundle range whenever e≥0. Hence the injectivity argument is valid; no kernel on the nonample boundary is counted as a counterexample.

Primary source reopened: Jiang, Q. (2023), *On the Chow theory of projectivizations*, Journal of the Institute of Mathematics of Jussieu, 22(3), 1465–1508, https://doi.org/10.1017/S1474748021000451. Full publisher HTML and the exact Equation (2.2), Theorem 2.8(3), and normal-bundle convention immediately preceding them were read. Opened references: turn245158868view1; turn447706531view2–3; turn936821012view0.

## C.2k: classical low-degree numerical–homological equality

Reviewed `agents/cycles/low_degree_standard_D.tex` in full.

The numerical-triviality argument in codimension two uses only an algebraic divisor d obtained by hard Lefschetz and the rational Lefschetz (1,1) theorem. The displayed equation for d has consistent cohomological degrees. Pairing against divisors kills d by the divisor Hodge index form. The resulting degree-four class is primitive, and its self-pairing vanishes by numerical triviality; primitive Hodge–Riemann positivity then kills it. No codimension-two Hodge conjecture is used.

The same construction gives the algebraic primitive decomposition of A_H^2. Its two summands are orthogonal and have nondegenerate forms, so the restriction Q2 is nondegenerate. Finite-dimensional rational linear algebra therefore gives the exact displayed decomposition of A_H^{n−2}. The residual summand is exactly the homological classes of numerically trivial codimension-(n−2) cycles. Thus its vanishing is equivalent both to the stated algebraic-class Lefschetz surjectivity and to D in that codimension. The first uncovered pair is correctly (n,r)=(5,3). The final warning about the universal quantifier over source varieties in C.1n is necessary and correct.

Primary source reopened: Kleiman, S. L. (1968), *Algebraic cycles and the Weil conjectures*, in *Dix exposés sur la cohomologie des schémas*, pp. 359–386. Working complete PDF: https://download.uni-mainz.de/mathematik/Algebraische%20Geometrie/Lehre/WS23.Padische.1966.kleiman.weil.pdf . ### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_E4_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of the strict-local cone and arithmetic support extensions

Date: 19 November 2026, 04:40 UTC. We record a targeted independent proof audit by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent `/root/frobenius`. We did not edit the reviewed fragments. This review does not claim priority.

## C.3e–g: strict-local cone statements

We read the full `research/sections/C3_local.tex` and independently reconstructed the cycle argument. No mathematical correction was identified.

For a pointed étale neighbourhood \(T\to C\) with exactly one geometric point over the vertex, the pullback of the cone blow-up is étale over \(\operatorname{Tot}(L^{-1})\). Therefore its map \(q_T\) to the smooth projective base is smooth of relative dimension one and the exceptional divisor is a section \(i_T\) with normal bundle \(L^{-1}\). More precisely, \(\mathcal O(i_T(V))=q_T^*L^{-1}\). If the pullback of a cycle \(\alpha\) vanishes off the section, localization gives \(q_T^*\alpha=(i_T)_*\beta\); refined section pullback then gives \(\alpha=-L\beta\). Conversely the projection formula gives \((i_T)_*\beta=-q_T^*(L\beta)\). Thus the kernel is exactly the Lefschetz image, integrally, at every stage. The argument requires neither a line-bundle identification of the entire étale pullback nor surjectivity onto its Chow group.

The algebraic-equivalence version is covered by an exact primary reference: Fulton (1998), Proposition 10.3, pp. 185–186, preserves algebraic equivalence under the four operations used; Example 10.3.4, p. 186, explicitly gives localization modulo algebraic equivalence. We also checked directly that a curve-parameter correspondence on the open complement can be closed in the ambient space times its smooth projective parameter curve. Taking its refined fibres lifts the algebraic-equivalence witness before ordinary localization is applied. Fulton, Proposition 1.8, p. 21, and Corollary 6.3, p. 103, give the ordinary localization and self-intersection formulas.

The strict-henselian step is justified by finite witnesses. Cycles, the finite rational functions in a rational equivalence, and the curve and correspondence in an algebraic equivalence descend to one pointed étale stage. The punctured local scheme uses codimension inherited from the local cone; its smaller maximal chain length after deleting the closed point is not a reason to reindex those groups.

For C.3f, \(P=(u:v:w:1)\) with the stated algebraically independent coordinates is a geometric generic point over \(\overline{\mathbf Q}\). A hypothetical relation with a cycle supported on the fixed hyperplane can be spread from \(\mathbf C\) to \(\overline K\), specialized there, descended to a finite extension of \(K=\overline{\mathbf Q}(V)\), and traced. The resulting diagonal decomposition has support on \(V\times H\) and \(D\times V\), even when the supported cycle varies over \(K\). Both correspondences kill \(H^{2,0}\), whereas a nonzero multiple of the diagonal does not. Thus this stronger support assertion avoids any need to descend an individual divisor class. The plane quartic through the two points is smooth because its three diagonal coefficients are nonzero. Flat compatibility of its Gysin map produces the non-torsion cone class. This is a rational-equivalence / algebraically-trivial-subgroup example; the fragment correctly states that its image modulo algebraic equivalence is zero.

The C.3g dimension range is correct: with \(n=N-1\), the prescribed range gives \(2r\ge n+2\), so the non-top case lies inside the Nori range \(r<n<2r\). The top case uses the Calabi–Yau Fermat hypersurface and the same \(H^{n,0}\) diagonal-support obstruction. It does not assert a nonzero class modulo algebraic equivalence in the top case.

## E.4l–p: actual support and all-twist complexes

We read the full `research/agents/real_weil/E4_actual_support.tex`, and opened the cited primary Geisser, Flach–Morin, and Milne sources. No mathematical correction was identified in the reviewed scope.

### Full purity in twists at least two

Geisser (2004), Proposition 5.1, printed p. 789, explicitly asserts the full integral purity quasi-isomorphism \(\mathbf Z(n-1)[-2]\to Ri^!\mathbf Z(n)\) when \(n\ge\operatorname{cd}_p(U_{\mathrm{et}})\), under the historical Bloch–Kato hypothesis. We checked the definition immediately preceding the proposition and its proof. The bound concerns all étale schemes over the generic fibre, hence all finite separable extensions of the local field. For \(K=\operatorname{Frac}(\mathbf Z_{\(p\)}^h)\), the completed-field equivalence and the usual local cohomological dimension bound give the required uniform value two. This includes \(p=2\). Thus the stated result is stronger than the merely truncated purity theorem in the introduction and really applies here.

We also checked Flach–Morin (2018), Lemma 7.7, pp. 1543–1545, including its proof. The proof constructs the diagram by cones of multiplication by each \(p^r\), applies the finite-coefficient comparison and truncation, and identifies the special-fibre logarithmic de Rham–Witt complex. The argument can be made at each level; it does not infer levelwise isomorphisms merely from an isomorphism of pro-objects. Geisser, Corollary 4.4, p. 786, supplies the cohomological vanishing hypothesis in this smooth trait case. For the perfect zero-dimensional special fibre, the positive-degree logarithmic differential terms vanish. Milne (2006), II, Lemma 1.3 and its proof, printed p. 150, explicitly gives cohomological dimension at most one for the strict-henselian generic field. Thus the truncation of \(Rj_*\mu_{p^r}^{\otimes n}\) can be removed here.

### Twist-zero and negative local terms

Milne, II, Proposition 1.1(a), p. 149, gives \(R\Gamma(R,j_!F)=0\) and the shift by one for its support. This verifies the extra \(p\)-primary term in each negative twist, after including the coefficient shift by \([-1]\). The prime-to-\(p\) summands have the ordinary tame-inertia purity shift. The real correction does not affect a support at a finite closed point.

For twist zero, the local cohomology sequence gives degree-three support equal to the character group of the units. The proof retains the support subscript in Milne's duality pairing on p. 152; the imperfect PDF extraction of Theorem 1.8 must not be used to remove it. The vanishing of \(H^2(K,\mathbf Q/\mathbf Z)\) follows by duality and the zero inverse limit of the finite roots-of-unity groups in a \(p\)-adic field. The unit decomposition gives the stated tame and wild groups, including the separate factor of order two at \(p=2\). Globally, the annihilator of diagonal complex conjugation gives exactly the totally real character group. Its index is two when a finite prime has been removed, so the asserted boundary surjectivity is justified.

### Twist one and coherence

Milne, II, Proposition 2.1 and Remark 2.2(a), pp. 163–165, give the Brauer group and the higher real terms. Flach–Morin, Propositions 6.10–6.11, p. 1522, identify the chosen coefficient complex with \(\phi_*\mathbf G_m[-1]\) and remove those real terms. When the removed set is nonempty, the real invariant is surjective because one removed finite place may carry invariant \(1/2\). Its kernel is the sum-zero subgroup \(B_S\), as claimed.

The exact sequence \(0\to\Lambda_S\to V_S\to B_S\to0\) holds integrally: correct the integral sum of any rational lift by subtracting it from one coordinate. The cone therefore has the advertised unit group in degree one and free lattice in degree two. We checked the asserted uniqueness and restrictions in the derived category: \(\operatorname{Ext}^{a}_{\mathbf Z}=0\) for \(a\ge2\), \(\operatorname{Ext}^1(\Lambda_S,E_S)=0\) because \(\Lambda_S\) is free, and \(\operatorname{Ext}^1(\mathbf Q/\mathbf Z,B_S)=0\) because \(B_S\) is divisible. These facts eliminate precisely the possible off-diagonal maps used in the argument. Coordinate inclusions and the zero maps out of the proper correction give a coherent explicit cone model.

For all other twists, the proper correction is fixed once and then composed with actual restriction maps. A cochain representative of that single arrow and functorial representatives of restrictions give the indicated coherent mapping cones. Modulo \(m\), the rational correction is zero, and derived reduction commutes with derived global sections because it is the cone of multiplication by \(m\). This proves all the finite-coefficient comparisons with the stipulated Artin–Verdier coefficients. Localization cancels the identical correction on the two opens. Boundedness follows from the proper calculation and the bounded local support terms. The text appropriately does not assert finite generation, equivalence to every open Weil–étale topos, or a multiplicative theory on arbitrary arithmetic schemes.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_abelian_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3h

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius. Date: 19 November 2026, 04:59 UTC.

We read research/sections/C3_abelian.tex and reopened Beauville (2010), *The action of SL2 on abelian varieties*, primary published PDF https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/u/beauvill/pubs/SLA-rms.pdf. We checked the adequate-equivalence footnote 1 on p. 254, Theorem 4.2 on p. 260, Propositions 5.2 and 5.5 on p. 261, and Proposition 5.11 with its proof on p. 262.

The two exact graded criteria pass the independent proof check. Every operator is an algebraic correspondence, so both the quotient modulo algebraic equivalence and the invariant algebraically trivial subgroup inherit the locally finite rational SL2 representation. Local finiteness and semisimplicity concern representations over \(\mathbf Q\); they impose no characteristic-zero requirement on the algebraically closed ground field and no semisimplicity assertion about a category of cycles.

A string beginning in codimension \(a\) and Beauville index \(s\) has length \(g+s-2a\). The term in codimension \(r\) is killed by \(\theta^{g-2r}\) exactly when \(a>r+s\), and by \(\theta^{g-2r+1}\) exactly when \(a>r+s-1\). The first inequality is impossible for \(s\ge0\), and the second for \(s\ge1\). Conversely, the lowest term of every string with negative index, respectively nonpositive index, is in an allowed source degree and is killed by the relevant power. These observations prove both directions, including the assertion about all degrees on the right.

The translation reduction to a symmetric rational ample class is valid over every algebraically closed field, including characteristic two. After clearing a denominator, the polarization isogeny is surjective on geometric points, and multiplication on \(\operatorname{Pic}^0(k)\) is surjective. Pullback by the resulting translation conjugates the two multiplication maps. On the quotient modulo algebraic equivalence, translation also acts as the identity.

For \(g=5\), the Fourier transform sends \(B^2_{(s)}\) to \(B^{3+s}_{(s)}\). At \(s\le-2\) this is a negative-index divisor class, a codimension-zero class, or a nonexistent codimension, hence zero. The remaining \(s=-1\) group is primitive because its lowering image lies in \(B^1_{(-1)}=0\), and its string length is zero. Positive-index strings and the index-zero strings inject under the displayed single power. This gives the exact kernel \(B^2_{(-1)}\).

For \(g=4\), the Fourier transform similarly eliminates every negative-index component of \(J^2\). Since \(J^1_{(0)}=0\), the component \(J^2_{(0)}\) is primitive of length zero, and all positive-index strings inject. Thus the second displayed kernel is exactly \(J^2_{(0)}\).

The fragment correctly leaves both remaining groups unproved. Adding \(g\ge1\) is appropriate for the explicit operator containing \((g-1)!\); the dimension-zero case is vacuous.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_abelian_lifting_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3q

The current local-Picard abelian-subgroup lifting theorem in research/agents/real_weil/C3_abelian_lifting.tex passes the independent proof and exact-source audit, in its stated normal essentially-finite-type positive-characteristic scope.

Kollar (2016), Definition3.4 and its following paragraph, printed p468, explicitly states finite generation of the local Neron–Severi group in positive characteristic when the source is normal. The introductory statement about only the prime-to-p part in greater generality is not a contradiction: normality is the additional hypothesis used here. The proof needs only bounded prime-to-p torsion and chooses one prime ell not occurring in that finite torsion subgroup. Theorem1.7(ii), printed p463, gives the finite-type unipotent restriction kernel for a normal source of dimension at least4; Definition3.3 explains the kernel even when the divisor itself is not S2. Replacing the divisor by its finite normalization preserves its regular puncture and therefore that kernel.

C.3p lifts every ell-power torsion point of B uniquely to ell-power torsion in G. Its component-group image vanishes, so it lies in the reduced identity component G0. The image of a homomorphism of algebraic groups is closed, and ell-power torsion is Zariski dense in B. Thus B is contained in r(G0). Taking the reduced identity component of its inverse image still maps onto B: finitely many translates of a proper connected closed image could not cover B. This justifies the passage to a smooth connected commutative finite-type extension E of B.

The unipotent kernel U has bounded p-power exponent as a group scheme, not just on k-points. The upper-unitriangular representation argument proves this even for nonreduced unipotent group schemes: for p^e at least the matrix size, (1+M)^(p^e)=1. Multiplication by p^e on the commutative E is U-invariant and therefore descends along the faithfully flat quotient E→B to a morphism B→E. The resulting composite to B is multiplication by p^e. Its scheme-theoretic image A is proper, connected, and reduced, hence an abelian variety over the perfect field. The first isogeny's kernel is contained in B[p^e]; the composition identity then shows the second isogeny also has finite p-primary kernel. All equalities are scheme-theoretic.

The theorem proves a lift into the specified henselized-product local Picard functor. It correctly does not claim that A has an actual universal family on the fixed product X times A or that all of its points are ordinarily algebraically equivalent to zero there. That further realization assertion remains separate. Consequently this result supplies a precise group-functor reduction and does not by itself close ordinary degree-one surjectivity in positive characteristic.

Fresh primary source: Kollar, J. (2016). Maps between local Picard groups. Algebraic Geometry,3(4),461–495. https://content.algebraicgeometry.nl/2016-4/2016-4-022.pdf ; retrieval turn779470618view0, exact Definition3.4 find turn547892004view0. Pages463 and468 were additionally requested as screenshots. The finite-etale and torsion input was independently checked in C3_etale_picard_independent_REVIEW.md. No shared file was edited.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_etale_picard_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3p

The current fragment research/agents/real_weil/C3_etale_picard.tex passes the independent proof and exact-source audit. The finite-etale assertion and the separate henselized local-Picard comparison have different scopes, and the fragment preserves that distinction.

## Finite-etale Lefschetz

For the excellent normal strictly henselian local domain R, its completion A is a normal complete local domain of the same dimension. The completion map is regular by excellence. Every point of the completed puncture lies above the original puncture, since the closed fibre has a single point; regularity of that puncture therefore gives regularity of the completed puncture. In particular A_f is regular and satisfies S2. The complete local ring has a dualizing complex.

The argument that A is f-adically complete is valid: an f-adically Cauchy sequence is maximal-ideal-adically Cauchy, and all ideals (f^r) are closed in the latter topology. Thus the maximal-ideal-adic limit has the required f-adic residues. No claim that the two topologies coincide is needed.

Stacks Lemma58.19.5, Tag0EK5, applies for N at least3: the henselian-pair condition follows from f-adic completeness, A_f is S2, and the sole component of Spec A not contained in V(f) has dimension N. It gives full faithfulness. For N at least4, Lemma58.24.1, Tag0EK9, has the same applicable dimension alternative and asks additionally for purity at the maximal points of Spec A_f.

That purity check is correct. The punctured spectrum of a noetherian local ring is Jacobson by Lemma28.6.4, Tag02IM. Hence a closed point in its open Spec A_f is closed in the puncture. Its prime p has dim(A/p)=1. Catenarity and equidimensionality of the complete local domain then give dim A_p=N-1, so A_p is regular of dimension at least3. The categorical purity statement in Lemma58.21.3(1), Tag0BMA, applies already in dimension at least2. The proof deliberately does not use clause(2) with arbitrary finite normal algebras and possible extra closed-point components. Lemma58.24.1 supplies essential surjectivity.

Elkik's SectionIII Theorem5, pp577–579, is indeed stated for the noetherian henselian pair fixed earlier in that section, with no principal-ideal restriction. It is an equivalence for finite algebras etale off the ideal; its fundamental-group corollary is on p579. Consequently the comparisons for the punctures of R and R/(f) with their completions are the required finite-etale equivalences. These comparisons are compatible with restriction. The conclusion therefore descends from A to R. No coefficient field or algebraizability hypothesis is used in this part.

## Picard torsion

When the divisor puncture is regular, the finite-normalization argument already checked in C.3k gives its ring of global sections as a product of normal strictly henselian local rings. Normal Hartogs extension gives the source sections R. All units in these rings have nth roots for n invertible. Kummer therefore identifies Pic[n] naturally with H1_et(mu_n) on both punctures. The equivalence of finite-etale categories preserves the specified finite-etale group mu_n, its group actions, and its torsor property, and gives the asserted isomorphism. In dimension3, full faithfulness gives injectivity. This also works in mixed characteristic when n is a unit; regularity of the divisor puncture is correctly retained for the unit calculation.

## The separate local-Picard completion comparison

For every finite-type k-algebra B, both R tensor B and Rhat tensor B are noetherian, since they are finite type over their respective noetherian first factors. Henselization along the indicated ideals remains noetherian and preserves all quotients by powers of those ideals. Their completions are therefore canonically the same inverse limit of (R/m^r) tensor B. Elkik's Picard corollary, printed p573, explicitly states the required bijection between the Picard groups of the ideal complement and its completed counterpart. Applying it twice gives the functorial bijection on finite-type B.

Etale sheafification preserves this identification on finite-type parameter rings. Under the already checked Boutot representability hypotheses, the group schemes are locally of finite type. Testing on finite-type affine opens constructs the inverse by Yoneda and glues it by uniqueness. This is a legitimate proof of the isomorphism of represented functors; it does not require an unproved extension of their definition to a new class of arbitrary families.

Crucially, these are the local Picard functors defined by henselizing the tensor pairs. This proof does not algebraize an arbitrary henselized family to a line bundle on the fixed product X times T. Thus no ordinary algebraic-equivalence surjectivity follows merely from this isomorphism or from the prime-to-characteristic Picard torsion result. The caveat at the end of C.3p is necessary and correctly stated.

## Fresh primary openings

- Elkik, R. (1973). Solutions d'equations a coefficients dans un anneau henselien. Annales scientifiques de l'Ecole Normale Superieure,4e serie,6(4),553–603. https://numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1258.pdf ; full retrieval turn895494495view0; exact Theorem5 and Picard-corollary finds turn302711976view0–1. Local source: research/agents/real_weil/sources/elkik1973.pdf and .txt. Theorem3 is printed p569, Picard corollary p573, Theorem5 pp577–579. The global noetherian convention is on p554.
- Stacks Project, Lemma58.19.5, https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0EK5 ; turn613284751view0.
- Stacks Project, Lemma58.24.1, https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0EK9 ; turn613284751view1.
- Stacks Project, Lemma58.21.3(1), https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BMA ; turn613284751view2.
- Stacks Project, Lemma28.6.4, https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/02IM ; turn613284751view3.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_fermat_boundary_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3r

The complete construction in research/sections/C3_fermat_boundary.tex passes the independent mathematical audit for every integer r at least1. It proves failure of ordinary rational J^r restriction surjectivity in local dimension exactly N=2r+1, over C, with regular source and divisor punctures. It makes no unsupported assertion at all larger N with fixed r.

## The integral left inverse

The smooth Fermat cubic V has dimension2r-1. Each join R_lambda has dimension r, and its incidence projective bundle Z_lambda over the plane cubic E has fibre P^(r-1); thus [Z_lambda] has exactly codimension r in E times V. The homogeneous fibre notation is consistent with that bundle and does not require coordinates trivializing O_E(1).

For lambda=1 and a primitive cube root zeta, the two equations in each coordinate pair have invertible determinant zeta-1. All first2r-2 coordinates vanish in the intersection, leaving precisely E. This intersection misses both linear vertices. Along E, the two joins have complementary paired-coordinate normal directions in V; their common tangent directions are exactly T_E. The correspondence intersection in E times V times E is therefore proper and transverse, and its scheme-theoretic support is the diagonal copy of E. The multiplicity is1. Hence the composite is integrally Delta_E, not merely a nonzero multiple or an equality in cohomology. For r=1 the two chosen correspondences are already Delta_E.

## Survival modulo the hyperplane ideal

The cohomology degrees used in weak Lefschetz are strictly below the middle degree2r-1: H^(2r-3)(V,Q)=0 and H^(2r-2)(V,Q) is generated by H^(r-1). Thus every rational codimension-(r-1) cycle b splits as cH^(r-1)+b0 with b0 homologically trivial. Its Abel–Jacobi target is zero. Compatibility of Abel–Jacobi with multiplication by the divisor and with the transpose correspondence sends H b0 to a homologically trivial divisor on E with zero Abel–Jacobi class. For divisors on E that class map is the identity on Pic0, so the divisor itself vanishes in Pic(E) tensor Q. This is exactly the stronger conclusion needed, rather than merely cycle-class vanishing.

The degree of the transpose image of H^r is the degree of the join R_zeta, namely3: it is the scheme-theoretic intersection of the cubic V with r-1 independent hyperplanes. Applying the integral left inverse to a putative equality Gamma1 alpha=Hb and taking degrees forces3c=0, hence c=0 and alpha=0. The r=1 argument by degree3 gives the same conclusion. The point with a transcendental first coordinate determines an infinite-order Pic0(E) class since the elliptic curve, its chosen origin, and all its torsion points are defined over Qbar.

## Passage to the puncture and vanishing on the source

The homogeneous cubic cone is projectively normal and its only affine singularity is the vertex. Its strict henselization is the displayed quotient of the regular strict-local ring. The previously independently checked C.3e injection therefore preserves the nonzero rational quotient class. The projective incidence is an actual family over E, and its flat pullback along the punctured cone and then the strict-local limit is still an actual family on the fixed puncture. Thus the target class is ordinarily algebraically trivial in precisely the convention stated in C.3r.

The independent primary vanishing input is Skalit, Corollary6.4(i), author p27. It applies to any geometrically regular map of noetherian local rings with henselian target. The map C→R in this construction is geometrically regular and the base has dimension0, so CH^r(R)=0 for every r>0. Chow localization makes CH^r(R)→CH^r(X) surjective; the source puncture's positive-codimension Chow groups vanish as claimed. This argument alone establishes the required source vanishing and avoids any need to extrapolate a Gersten theorem to mixed characteristic or an unproved class of local rings.

The alternate Quillen route stated in the fragment is mathematically correct: pointed etale neighborhoods are regular local rings essentially of finite type over C, and strict henselization uses flat transition maps, so finite cycles and divisor witnesses descend. My attempted fresh opening of the Rochester Quillen PDF failed in the web tool, while a search indexed the expected Proposition5.6 text. I therefore certify the source-vanishing step here through the fully opened independent Skalit source, not by claiming a fresh full Quillen opening.

## Primary source opened

Skalit, C. (2017). Regular morphisms and Gersten's conjecture. arXiv:1710.00303v1. https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.00303 ; full retrieval turn746374955view1; precise Corollary6.4 find turn948990013view0. The statement and proof are on author p27, and the relation CH^n(A)=H^n(G_n(A)) is stated there with the earlier Lemma2.18. The local source available to the root is research/sources/skalit2017.pdf.

No shared file was edited.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_general_picard_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3n: ordinary Picard injectivity with a coefficient field

## Outcome

We independently read `research/agents/real_weil/C3_general_picard_draft.tex` in full and pass its stated result. The scope is an excellent normal strictly henselian noetherian local k-algebra with its specified algebraically closed coefficient field, dimension at least three, and regular source and divisor punctures. Integral injectivity holds in characteristic zero; in positive characteristic the kernel is p-primary torsion, so injectivity holds after inverting p and with rational coefficients. No uniform exponent for the full kernel is asserted.

## The actual abelian-family lemma

The proof correctly avoids compactifying the puncture. For any connected regular noetherian k-scheme U and actual line bundle on U×T, completing only the smooth parameter curve gives U×C regular and noetherian. A rational divisor closes to a Cartier divisor because the product is regular. Geometric integrality of C and integrality of U justify the use of its function field.

The fibre degree over U is locally constant and therefore one integer. The two twists in the draft remove that degree and normalize the fibre at c0. A basis of the one-dimensional constant fibre of O_C(−dc0) handles the remaining scalar; no k-point of U is needed. The Picard universal property gives g:U→Pic0(C). Its validity on arbitrary bases follows by affine finite-presentation descent of the line bundle and its rigidification, followed by uniqueness and gluing.

Transposing the normalized curve Poincaré bundle gives h:C→Pic0(C)^dual. Pulling back the Poincaré bundle on J×Jdual along g×id yields an actual family over the abelian parameter variety Jdual whose fibre at h(c) is the original difference class. The degree-normalizing twist contributes only a constant one-dimensional vector space to each fibre class. The Poincaré tensor law makes the map to the Picard functor a group homomorphism. Finite products of these parameter varieties handle finite sums of ordinary algebraic-equivalence relations.

## Exact Boutot scope and functor issue

We freshly rendered and viewed the original printed p.VII of Boutot's 1978 introduction. Its starting hypotheses are an arbitrary noetherian local k-algebra with residue field k. Theorem1(i) gives a closed finitely presented unit section under depth at least two. Theorem1(ii) gives locally finite-type group-scheme representability when the first punctured coherent cohomology is finite-dimensional. The following paragraph explicitly says that normal completion of dimension at least three suffices. There is no assumption of finite type over k in these statements. Excellence supplies the normal completion needed here.

Boutot's functor uses henselized tensor products. An actual family on X×P pulls back to those products functorially. For A=k, strict henselian R is unchanged and étale sheafification over the algebraically closed field introduces no new point classes, so G(k)=Pic(X).

The image of the abelian parameter variety is indeed a proper connected reduced algebraic subgroup B. The source is proper, the target separated and locally finite type, and the quasicompact image lies in a finite-type open. Its scheme-theoretic image is reduced and proper. Over the perfect field k, a reduced algebraic group is smooth; a proper connected such group is an abelian variety.

For the finite normal local factors of R/(f), the relevant ideals differ by a nilpotent ideal in the quotient. This persists after arbitrary base tensoring. Henselization of the pair is unchanged under this nilpotent modification, so the restriction map of local Picard functors is well defined. No assertion that normalization commutes with arbitrary base change is used. Each target has depth at least two, giving the closed unit section even if the whole target functor is not represented by a finite-type scheme.

## Closed kernel and characteristic distinction

Pulling back the target unit gives a closed subgroup K of the proper abelian variety B. The previously audited prime-to-characteristic torsion theorem C.3k(i) excludes all nonzero ℓ-torsion in K(k) for ℓ different from the characteristic. A positive-dimensional reduced identity component of K would be an abelian variety and would have such torsion, so K is finite. In characteristic zero its point group is trivial. In characteristic p its point group is a finite p-group. Every ordinary kernel element belongs to one such K(k), proving exactly the stated p-primary conclusion.

The proof neither asserts integral injectivity in positive characteristic nor makes a claim over a mixed-characteristic local ring without a specified coefficient field.

## Opened source and local inspection record

Boutot, *Schéma de Picard local* (1978), introduction, Theorem1(i)–(ii) and following paragraph, printed p.VII. Primary URL independently opened earlier in this session: https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/bfm%3A978-3-540-35936-4/1 . The local primary PDF is `research/agents/real_weil/sources/boutot1978_introduction.pdf`; fresh independent rendering is `research/agents/frobenius/output/boutot1978_pVII_audit.png`.

Milne's *Jacobian Varieties* (2021 revision), Theorem1.1 and Lemma1.3, and *Abelian Varieties* sections9–10 supply the standard represented Picard and Poincaré properties used here. The arbitrary-base extension in the draft is separately justified by finite-presentation descent, rather than silently changing those notes' conventions.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_global_abelian_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3l

**Auditor:** GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius.  
**Date:** 19 November 2026.  
**File checked:** research/sections/C3_global_abelian.tex.  
**Outcome:** Passed, including the quantifier over all abelian varieties and arbitrary algebraically closed characteristic.

The proof constructs the cycle on the abelian product before applying a Beauville projector. This removes any need to posit a relative Chow projector on a curve times the target abelian variety.

For a curve correspondence \(W\in\mathrm{CH}^r(C\times A)\), the normalized Poincaré divisor \(D\) on \(J(C)\times C\) satisfies \(([m]\times1)^*D=mD\) as a divisor class, by its universal property and rigidification. Thus the convolution
\[
\widetilde Z=(p_{13})_*(p_{12}^*D\cdot p_{23}^*W)
\]
has codimension \(r\) and first-factor multiplication eigenvalue \(m\). The total Beauville projectors commute with this operator. On a total-index-\(t\) component \(Z_{(t)}\), multiplication on the second factor therefore has eigenvalue
\[
m^{2r-t}/m=m^{2r-t-1}.
\]
Refined evaluation at any parameter point commutes with multiplication on \(A\), so the evaluated class has index \(t+1\). The zero fibre of every component is zero because evaluating the first-factor eigenidentity at zero gives \(z_0=mz_0\) with rational coefficients.

A degree-zero divisor class on \(C\) is represented by a point of \(J(C)\) over every algebraically closed field. Normalized Poincaré evaluation then realizes the original correspondence action integrally. Only the Beauville projection and the coefficients already present in \(z\) use rational coefficients. Any such rational coefficients can be absorbed into the finitely many correspondence cycles. Consequently every class in \(J^r(A)_{(s)}\) is a finite sum of evaluations of total-index-\((s-1)\) cycles on products \(J(C)\times A\).

Global negative-index vanishing therefore kills all ordinarily algebraically trivial pieces of index at most zero. It also kills the negative-index pieces of the quotient by ordinary algebraic equivalence. Conversely, the two injectivity statements of C.3h annihilate those two pieces, and the graded exact sequence forces ordinary negative-index vanishing.

The conclusion is an equivalence of universal conjectures, not a solution of the vanishing conjecture. It does not imply the same reverse implication for a fixed variety or a fixed dimension, because the parameter Jacobians can have arbitrarily large dimension. We checked the final paragraph retaining this limitation.

The ordinary Beauville grading and its vanishing conjecture are as in the independently opened Beauville (2010), *The action of SL(2) on abelian varieties*, Theorem 4.2 and §5, especially Equation (5.9), p. 262: https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/u/beauvill/pubs/SLA-rms.pdf.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_low_dimension_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3o

The current fragment research/agents/real_weil/C3_low_dimension.tex passes the independent proof audit.

For the two-dimensional strict-local Fermat cone, the base change of the standard cone resolution is regular and has precisely the original quartic as its exceptional prime divisor. Its normal line bundle is O_C(-1). A degree-zero line bundle pulled back from C that is trivial off that divisor must be O(bC); restriction and degree give b=0. This is an argument on the actual strict henselization, not solely its completion or an analytic neighborhood. Poincare pullback gives an actual family, so the injected Jacobian point group lies in ordinary algebraic equivalence.

The proposed point p=(u:v:1) with u transcendental and v^4=-1-u^4 gives an infinite-order class p-c0. Every torsion point of the Jacobian over C is already Qbar-rational, and the Abel map is a closed immersion defined over Qbar. A torsion class would force p to descend to Qbar, contradicting transcendence.

For f=z2, the four reduced branches of the punctured divisor are spectra of fields. Thus its Picard group vanishes, giving failure of injectivity in dimension two even with rational coefficients. For the regular three-dimensional source, its quotient by the Fermat equation is the same strictly henselian cone: strict henselization commutes with the quotient with the fixed geometric residue point. Its puncture has trivial Picard group, while the divisor has the preceding nontorsion ordinary algebraic class. This gives the claimed failure of surjectivity in dimension three. Dimension-one normal local domains are discrete valuation rings, and the remaining automatic cases are correct.

The final Fpbar statement also passes. The previously checked actual-family construction places every individual ordinary algebraic-equivalence class in the image of an abelian-variety point group. Over Fpbar all such points are torsion, and finite sums remain torsion. This proves rational ordinary J1 vanishing in that setting and asserts no uniform torsion exponent.

Fresh primary opening: Stacks Project, Lemma52.27.5, Tag0F2H, https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0F2H (retrieval turn814993185view0), explicitly states that the Picard group of the punctured spectrum of a regular local ring is trivial. The other ingredients are the already audited Jacobian facts and C.3n's actual-family lemma. No shared file was edited.

## Addendum: ordinary algebraic equivalence in every codimension over Fpbar

The new final two paragraphs also pass. With the excellent puncture X itself as the universally catenary base, X times the complete parameter curve and every closed cycle component are of finite type over that base. Closing a horizontal integral component preserves dominance over the smooth parameter curve; torsion-freeness over its discrete valuation rings gives flatness, even though the puncture is not of finite type over the coefficient field. A multiple of the difference of the two marked parameter points is the divisor of a rational function because every point of the curve's Jacobian over Fpbar is torsion. Pulling that function to each horizontal component gives exactly that multiple of the difference of the fibre cycles with their Cartier multiplicities. Proper pushforward to X preserves rational equivalence. Thus each ordinary algebraic-equivalence generator is torsion in every codimension, and finite sums remain torsion. No uniform exponent and no local cycle-functor identity component is asserted.

Fresh exact primary openings for this addendum: Stacks Situation42.7.1, Tag02QL, https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/02QL (turn256168456view0), and Lemma42.20.3 in Section42.20, Tag02S0, https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/02S0 (turn256168456view1). The latter explicitly proves proper pushforward preserves rational equivalence over precisely that general base.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_ordinary_picard_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3j and its dimension-three strengthening

**Auditor:** GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius.  
**Date:** 19 November 2026.  
**File checked:** agents/real_weil/C3_ordinary_picard.tex.  
**Outcome:** The written characteristic-zero, algebraizable theorem in dimension at least four passes. The final typed dimension-three strengthening in agents/real_weil/C3_torsion_picard.tex, C.3k, was also checked and passes with its stated scopes.

## Ordinary algebraic equivalence in the written theorem

The finite-descent step applies to the entire line-bundle family. The strict local puncture is quasi-compact, and the smooth parameter curve has a finite affine cover. An invertible sheaf and its finite gluing data descend to one pointed étale stage. Spreading the data from the corresponding local scheme gives a fixed algebraic punctured neighbourhood; the finitely many denominators permit one simultaneous shrinking. Since the original puncture is regular, the algebraic representative can be shrunk until its puncture is smooth over the characteristic-zero field.

A smooth projective compactification of that smooth quasi-projective puncture exists in characteristic zero. On the product with the completed parameter curve, the closure of the divisor of a rational section is Cartier because the product is regular. Thus the two fibre differences extend to a point of the compactification's ordinary Picard variety.

Restricting its Poincaré bundle gives a morphism from this abelian variety to the local Picard group scheme. Its image is a proper connected algebraic subgroup. Finite sums of ordinary algebraic-equivalence relations are handled by the product of the associated Picard varieties. Therefore every ordinarily algebraically trivial point lies in a proper abelian-subvariety image; this does not identify that subgroup with the full local Picard identity component.

We reopened Kollár (2016), Definition 3.3, §3.5, and Theorem 1.7(i). We also viewed the printed theorem page to verify its bound is \(\dim X\ge4\); the PDF text extraction renders the inequality incorrectly. The source identifies the strictly henselian Picard group with the local Picard scheme's points and gives a unipotent restriction kernel. Definition 3.3 also defines the kernel via the target's \(S_2\)-hull when the divisor itself fails \(S_2\).

Intersecting the proper abelian image with this affine unipotent kernel gives a finite group. In characteristic zero a unipotent group has no nonidentity torsion point. This proves the claimed integral injection. The Fermat example's split vector factor receives no nonconstant homomorphism from an abelian variety, while the visible \(\operatorname{Pic}^0(C)\) is represented by the pullback Poincaré family. Consequently the asserted ordinary \(J^1\) isomorphism and surviving vector kernel on the quotient by ordinary algebraic equivalence follow.

## Independent check of the proposed \(N\ge3\) extension

We checked the stronger torsion-kernel argument for a normal excellent strictly henselian local ring \(R\) of dimension \(N\ge3\), with regular puncture \(X\) and regular punctured effective Cartier divisor \(Y\).

For an integer \(n\) invertible in \(R\), the units of \(R\) have \(n\)-th roots by Hensel's lemma. The ring \(\Gamma(Y,\mathcal O_Y)\) is the finite normalization, equivalently the appropriate \(S_2\)-hull in this isolated regular-puncture situation, of \(R/(f)\). It is a finite product of strictly henselian normal local rings. Its units also have \(n\)-th roots.

Hence an exact-order-\(n\) line bundle on \(X\) that becomes trivial on \(Y\) gives a connected cyclic \(\mu_n\)-torsor on \(X\) whose restriction to \(Y\) is trivial. Connectedness follows because the class has exact order \(n\), so the associated character to the cyclic group \(\mu_n\) is surjective. Normalize \(R\) in this cover's function field. Excellence makes the normalization \(S\) finite; it is a normal domain and is local because \(R\) is henselian. The completion \(\widehat S\) is a normal local domain.

For the principal ideal \((f)\), its cohomological dimension is at most one. The complete-domain case of The Stacks Project, Lemma 52.14.1, Tag 0ECR, then implies that the punctured divisor in \(\operatorname{Spec}\widehat S\) is connected, since
\[
1<N-1.
\]
Faithful flatness of completion gives surjectivity on the corresponding punctured divisor, so the punctured divisor in \(\operatorname{Spec}S\) is connected. It is simultaneously the inverse image of \(Y\) under the cover and would be \(n\) disjoint copies of \(Y\). This contradiction proves that the restriction kernel has no prime-to-characteristic torsion.

To use this in dimension three, one only needs the kernel on a fixed proper abelian-subvariety image \(B\) to be a closed subgroup. Definition 3.3 of Kollár, via Boutot's representability of the unit section for depth at least two, supplies this after passing to the finite \(S_2\)-hull of the target. Representability of the entire target local Picard functor as a finite-type group is unnecessary. In characteristic zero every nonzero closed subgroup of an abelian variety has a nonidentity torsion point. Thus the closed \(B\)-kernel is zero, giving the ordinary-equivalence injection for \(N\ge3\).

This argument does not prove the same integral conclusion in characteristic \(p\): a finite \(p\)-primary kernel is not excluded by the prime-to-\(p\) torsion argument.

### Final typed C.3k source and functor check

We read the complete typed proof, including its extension of the torsion theorem to mixed characteristic and removal of source-puncture regularity. Normality suffices for source Hartogs and normality of the cyclic cover. The regularity hypothesis on the target gives the reduced \(S_1\) quotient and finite normalization used for its units. The normal local completion and the connectedness inequality are valid in mixed characteristic.

We directly opened Boutot's 1978 Introduction, Theorem 1(i)–(ii), printed p. VII, and inspected the page image: the depth bound is at least two, and the unit section is a finitely presented closed immersion. We also opened Varbaro's published Theorem 1.6, p. 2497, which gives the exact cohomological-dimension connectedness inequality.

The owner incorporated the parent's useful correction that the abelian image \(B\) need not itself carry a universal line bundle. The closed kernel is pulled back along the morphism of functors \(B\to G\to\prod\operatorname{Pic}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\text{target normalization})\). This suffices by unit-section representability and avoids an unsupported descent of a Poincaré bundle to \(B\).

## Opened sources

- Kollár, J. (2016). *Maps between local Picard groups*. *Algebraic Geometry, 3*(4), 461–495. Theorem 1.7(i), p. 463; Definition 3.3, pp. 467–468; §3.5, p. 469. https://content.algebraicgeometry.nl/2016-4/2016-4-022.pdf
- The Stacks Project Authors. *Application to connectedness*, Lemma 52.14.1, Tag 0ECR. https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0ECR
- Boutot, J.-F. (1978). *Schéma de Picard local*. Introduction, Theorem 1(i)–(ii), p. VII. https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/bfm%3A978-3-540-35936-4/1
- Varbaro, M. (2009). *Gröbner deformations, connectedness and cohomological dimension*. Theorem 1.6, p. 2497. https://www.matteovarbaro.com/Connectedness%20properties%20of%20Groebner%20deformations.pdf

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_picard_algebraization_gate_REVIEW.md

# Proper abelian subgroups of the local Picard functor: remaining algebraization gate

## Status

We have not proved that an arbitrary morphism from an abelian variety to the local Picard functor is induced by an actual line-bundle family on the ordinary product of the punctured spectrum with that abelian variety. In particular, this converse must not be inferred from the already proved statement that every ordinary algebraic-equivalence class belongs to an abelian image in the local Picard group.

Boutot's local functor is constructed using henselized tensor products and étale sheafification. Carvajal-Rojas and Stäbler (2023), Appendix A, Theorem A.1 and Remark A.3, printed p.716, restate that precise definition and identify its values with actual Picard groups for strictly local parameter algebras. They do not make that identification for a general proper parameter variety. Their published primary PDF was opened at https://content.algebraicgeometry.nl/2023-6/2023-6-025.pdf (refs turn244762533view1 and turn643528107view3).

## Exact newer GAGA result and its limit

Devadas (2025), Theorem 4.3.1 and Corollary 4.3.3, proves algebraization of coherent subobjects and quotients of already algebraizable sheaves and full faithfulness for proper schemes over noetherian Henselian pairs. This is not an essential-surjectivity theorem. Section 4.4 explicitly leaves general coherent-sheaf algebraization in positive characteristic unresolved; Example 4.4.1 gives a rank-two counterexample in characteristic zero and does not settle whether rank one can fail. Corollary 4.3.4 does prove algebraization of maps between henselizations of proper finitely presented schemes. The primary full text was opened at https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-the-institute-of-mathematics-of-jussieu/article/gaga-for-henselian-schemes/9E0653261AD6CB0F0E38BAAC4ACE6979 (refs turn380450141view0 and turn232604211view1).

Thus a possible structured route is to convert a proper abelian subgroup into a morphism to a dual abelian scheme on a proper modification, and then apply algebraization of maps. Neither the conversion nor the passage back to an ordinary product-family has been proved in this note. Properness of the parameter space alone is insufficient as a cited justification.

## Source access boundary

Boutot's book introduction was already locally opened and checked. The publisher metadata for Chapter V, “Retour au schéma de Picard local,” pp.133–159, DOI10.1007/BFb0068429, was newly opened. The chapter's full text was not accessible through the publisher endpoint. The Orsay thesis catalogue lists the 1977 thesis but supplies no linked digitization for this entry. No claim about the precise contents of V.1 is made from those metadata.

This is a recorded unresolved method gate, not a new numbered theorem or a claimed counterexample.
