### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_picard_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of the strict local Picard counterexample C.3i

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius. Date: 19 November 2026.

We read research/agents/real_weil/C3_picard.tex and independently opened Kollár (2016), *Maps between local Picard groups*, full primary published PDF https://content.algebraicgeometry.nl/2016-4/2016-4-022.pdf. We checked Definition 3.3, pp. 467–468; §3.5, p. 469; and Example 4.2, particularly equations (4.2.3)–(4.2.4), p. 470.

## Actual Picard groups, not tangent spaces

The primary §3.5 explicitly identifies the rational points of the local Picard scheme over an algebraically closed field with the étale-local Picard group. For a henselian local ring, this is its actual punctured-spectrum Picard group. Example 4.2 gives the characteristic-zero exact sequence with the additive vector group \(\bigoplus_{a>0}H^1(V,M^a)\), not only its tangent space. The fragment therefore has the required algebraization input for actual line bundles. It does not improperly substitute completion for strict henselization.

## Geometry and cohomology

For \(m=N-2\ge2\), the kernel of the surjection \(\mathcal O_C^{m+1}\to K_C\) has rank \(m\), so its projective bundle of lines is smooth of dimension \(m\). The three coordinate sections of the plane quartic span \(H^0(C,K_C)\). The map
\[
H^1(W,\mathcal O_W)\longrightarrow H^1(W,L)
\]
is therefore exactly the injective contraction with image
\(\langle x_0,x_1,x_2\rangle\).
Künneth and Serre duality give \(H^1(W,L)=\mathbf C^{m+1}\), vanishing of \(H^1(W,L^a)\) for \(a\ge2\), and vanishing of all the \(H^2(W,L^a)\) used in the hyperplane exact sequence. Thus the target positive-degree vector group is \(\mathbf C^{m-2}\), and the restriction map is the indicated quotient with kernel \(\mathbf C^3\). At \(m=2\) the target vector group is zero, which is consistent.

The projective-bundle Picard formula applies because the fibres have dimension \(m-1\ge1\). It identifies \(\operatorname{Pic}(W)\to\operatorname{Pic}(H)\) as an isomorphism carrying \(L\) to \(L_H\). In the diagram of Kollár's exact sequences, the right vertical arrow is therefore an isomorphism and the left arrow is surjective with kernel \(\mathbf G_a^3\). The snake lemma gives precisely the asserted kernel and surjectivity on the middle groups. This conclusion does not actually require choosing splittings, although the projection splittings are compatible here.

## Normalization and henselization

The injection \(B/(f)\hookrightarrow B_H\) follows degree by degree from the hyperplane sequence. Its cokernel vanishes in all sufficiently large degrees, so it is finite-dimensional over \(\mathbf C\), and \(B_H\) is finite over \(B/(f)\). The map is an isomorphism on the puncture, and the full section ring is normal. Every positive-degree element above the vertex is nilpotent modulo the original positive-degree ideal, by finite graded integrality; hence there is exactly one point above the vertex, with residue field \(\mathbf C\). Finite algebras over a henselian base decompose into the henselizations at their points over the closed point. Here there is one point, so the normalization and henselization comparison used in the proof is valid. Quotient by \(f\) commutes with henselization as stated.

One explanatory clarification was sent to the owning agent. The normalized hyperplane section ring \(B_H\) need not be generated in degree one: in this example its additional sections occur in degree two. Consequently \(\operatorname{Tot}(L_H^{-1})\to\operatorname{Spec}B_H\) should be described as the proper normalized cone contraction, rather than necessarily the blow-up of the maximal ideal. It is obtained by factoring the proper contraction over the original hyperplane cone through its finite normalization. It is an isomorphism away from the zero section and has the same formal neighbourhood and normal line bundle used in Kollár's calculation. The fragment did not explicitly assert degree-one generation for \(B_H\); this clarification does not change the result.

The depth check is also correct. Normality gives depth at least two, and nonzero \(H^1(W,\mathcal O_W)\) gives nonzero degree-zero local cohomology in degree two, so the depth is exactly two. Quotient by the nonzerodivisor \(f\) lowers it to one. The example therefore does not meet the depth hypothesis of the cited local Lefschetz theorem.

## Scope of the conclusion

Every nonzero element of \((\mathbf C,+)^3\) is nontorsion, so the full Picard restriction fails to be injective after rational coefficients or removal of finite groups. The source and target punctures are regular, hence their \(\mathrm{CH}^1\) groups agree with their Picard groups.

Definition 3.3 permits an étale neighbourhood of the product with a parameter scheme that varies with that parameter. Ordinary algebraic equivalence of cycles on a fixed punctured scheme does not automatically allow this variation. Thus the fragment correctly distinguishes the identity component of the local Picard scheme from \(\mathrm{CH}^1_{\mathrm{alg}}\), and does not claim to refute the latter's restriction map without a further argument.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_segre_nonvanishing_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3s

The full typed fragment research/sections/C3_segre_nonvanishing.tex passes for every N>=2 and1<=r<=N-1. It establishes the exact universal positive-codimension finiteness/vanishing threshold for ordinary Chow groups and their ordinary algebraically trivial subgroups: examples are nonzero after tensoring with Q in every such r<N; for r>=N there are no points of the relevant codimension on a punctured local N-dimensional spectrum, so the groups vanish.

The Fermat hypersurface of degree r+2 in P^(r+1) is smooth and has trivial canonical bundle. The specified r independent transcendental coordinates and final algebraic root give a geometric generic point over Qbar. The fixed point lies on the chosen hyperplane. The earlier C.3f spread, specialization to the algebraic closure of the function field, finite descent, norm, and diagonal-localization argument applies in dimension r: if a nonzero multiple of the degree-zero generic-point cycle were hyperplane-supported, the resulting nonzero multiple of the diagonal would be supported on the union of the two forbidden product supports. Those supports annihilate the nonzero H^(r,0), a contradiction. For r=1 this is also the familiar degree and elliptic-Picard obstruction. All degree-zero zero-cycles on T are ordinarily algebraically trivial, with an actual curve family through the two points.

The projective-bundle identity is integral. Writing CH*(P^m times T)=CH*(T)[h]/(h^(m+1)) and quotienting by h+H sets h=-H and gives CH*(T)/(H^(m+1)). Its degree-r denominator is exactly H^(m+1)CH^(r-m-1)(T), interpreted as zero for negative degree. This is contained in H CH^(r-1)(T), so the previously nonzero class survives. No reversal of the quotient map is involved.

The Segre product embedding is very ample and projectively normal: both factor section rings are generated in degree1, and the full section ring of the product for O(1) external H is their diagonal Segre product. It is the normal full section ring of an ample line bundle on a smooth integral variety. Thus the cone and the C.3e strict-local injection have exactly the needed hypotheses. The cone's puncture is a nonzero line bundle over the smooth product, hence regular. The actual algebraic-equivalence family survives its flat pullback and the strict-local limit.

The theorem does not assert that the displayed class is nonzero modulo algebraic equivalence; it is constructed inside that equivalence's kernel. The separate B-group range is correctly left to C.3g. No shared file was edited, and no additional literature theorem beyond the already audited cone, projective-bundle, and diagonal-support inputs is needed.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_smooth_cone_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3u: ordinary Picard classes on a smooth cone

**Conclusion:** the complete typed proof in ../real_weil/C3_smooth_cone_draft.tex passes. We checked ordinary algebraic equivalence, integral coefficients, arbitrary characteristic, nonhomogeneous divisors, and each new external input. The result is restricted to the stated smooth cone source; it does not extend to a deformation or an arbitrary local germ by this argument.

## Exact statement checked

Let \(V/k\) be smooth projective integral of dimension at least three over an algebraically closed field, with \(L\) very ample and its full section ring generated in degree one. On the strict cone puncture \(X\), pullback identifies the **point group** of \(\operatorname{Pic}^0_{\mathrm{red}}(V)\) with line bundles ordinarily algebraically equivalent to zero. For any nonzero local element \(f\) with regular divisor puncture \(Y\), ordinary restriction is integrally surjective. The kernel is zero in characteristic zero and a finite \(p\)-primary point group in characteristic \(p\). Ordinary rational \(J^1\) therefore restricts isomorphically.

## Proof checks

1. **Resolution and boundary.** Degree-one generation identifies the vertex blow-up with the total space of \(L^{-1}\). Localization and ind-etale strict henselization preserve regularity. The resulting integral noetherian \(Z\) is proper over \(R\), its closed fibre is the single reduced prime divisor \(V\), and its normal bundle is \(L^{-1}\). The actual projection \(\pi:Z\to V\) survives. Closing a rational divisor extends each line bundle on \(X\), and the kernel of \(\operatorname{Pic}(Z)\to\operatorname{Pic}(X)\) consists of powers of \(\mathcal O_Z(V)\).

2. **Actual family and pointwise injectivity.** The normalized Poincare bundle on \(V\times A\), with \(A=\operatorname{Pic}^0_{\mathrm{red}}(V)\), pulls back to an actual bundle on \(X\times A\). If \(\pi^*Q=\mathcal O_Z(bV)\), restriction gives \(Q=L^{-b}\); algebraic triviality and ampleness force \(b=0\). The proof correctly asserts injectivity on points without assuming a trivial infinitesimal kernel. Restricting to chains of curves in \(A\) proves ordinary algebraic triviality.

3. **All torsion at one suitable prime.** Choose \(\ell\ne\operatorname{char}k\) avoiding torsion in \(\operatorname{NS}(V)/\mathbf Z[L]\). An \(\ell^n\)-torsion bundle \(M\) extends to \(\widetilde M\) with \(\widetilde M^{\ell^n}=\mathcal O_Z(bV)\). Its restriction \(Q\) has class in \(\mathbf Z[L]\). If \([Q]=a[L]\), twisting by \(\mathcal O_Z(aV)\) has the correct sign and makes the restriction algebraically trivial. Restriction of the remaining power identity and ampleness force its boundary coefficient to vanish. Both the new extension and \(Q\) are torsion. The quotient \(T=\widetilde M\otimes\pi^*Q^{-1}\) defines a finite etale Kummer torsor trivial on \(V\), because its defining unit lies in the divisible group \(k^\times\). Proper henselian finite-etale equivalence lifts its section to \(Z\). Thus \(M=\rho^*Q\), giving the claimed equality of all \(\ell^n\)-torsion groups.

4. **Density without arbitrary family algebraization.** C.3n places an ordinary class in a closed abelian image \(B\) of the local Picard scheme. Its dense prime-to-characteristic torsion lies in the closed proper image \(P\) of \(A\); therefore \(B\subset P\). For the target, the proof correctly uses the product of local Picard schemes of finite normalizations. C.3p lifts all target \(\ell^n\)-torsion to the source. Thus each target ordinary abelian image is contained in the closed proper image \(r(P)\). Every point of this image lifts to a point of \(P\) over the algebraically closed field, and that point already comes from the actual Poincare family. This gives integral surjectivity without inferring that arbitrary henselized-product families algebraize.

5. **Kernel.** The closed kernel in \(A\) has no nonzero prime-to-characteristic torsion by C.3p and pointwise injectivity. A positive-dimensional reduced connected subgroup would be an abelian variety with such torsion. The kernel scheme is therefore finite; its finite point group is zero in characteristic zero and \(p\)-primary in characteristic \(p\). This is precisely the claimed conclusion.

## Independently opened primary inputs

- **Stacks Project, Lemma 58.9.1, Tag 0A48:** https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0A48 . Fresh full opening confirmed the exact proper-scheme hypothesis over a henselian local ring. It is not an arbitrary-family algebraization statement.
- **Cheng, Ji, Larson, and Olander, Theorem of the Base, author version dated July 1, 2021:** https://chngr.github.io/assets/theorem-of-the-base.pdf . Fresh full opening and local text check of **Theorem 7.3, p. 18**, confirm finite generation of the Neron–Severi group in the required all-characteristic smooth projective case. Local PDF: ../real_weil/sources/cheng_ji_larson_olander2021.pdf.
- **Kleiman, The Picard scheme, arXiv:math/0504020v1 (April 1, 2005):** https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0504020 . Fresh full opening and direct local PDF reading checked **Theorem 2.5(2), p. 18; Exercise 4.3, p. 27, with Answer 4.3, p. 65; Theorem 5.4, pp. 37–38; Proposition 5.10, pp. 41–42**. These supply the universal bundle with a section, properness of the identity component, and ordinary algebraic equivalence. The version-qualified web URL initially failed; the unqualified PDF successfully opened the same unique v1. Local PDF: ../real_weil/sources/kleiman_picard2005v1.pdf.

The independently passed C.3n and C.3p remain explicit dependencies. No source is used for a stronger claim than its statement. No computational test is needed for this argument.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_surjectivity_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit: the deformed-cone ordinary Picard surjectivity counterexample

## Scope and outcome

We independently read the entire draft `research/agents/real_weil/C3_surjectivity_draft.tex` on 19 November 2026. The construction and proof pass this audit. The result concerns ordinary algebraic equivalence defined by actual families on the fixed punctured scheme. It does not rely on identifying that relation with the connected component of a local Picard functor.

The claimed range is every complex local dimension N at least four. The source ordinary algebraically trivial Picard group is zero, whereas the target contains an injected copy of the Fermat quartic Jacobian, including a specified point of infinite order. Thus the ordinary J1 restriction map fails surjectivity integrally and rationally in this range.

## Geometry and first neighbourhood

1. In the total space of O(-1,-1) over P^m × P², the equation F+tℓH is globally typed as a section of O(0,4). The codimension-two locus F=ℓH=0 cannot contribute an additional irreducible component to its hypersurface. The Segre contraction is proper, is an isomorphism away from the zero section, and has the asserted radical equations on its reduced image.
2. Smoothness near the exceptional V=P^m × C follows from dF. Properness permits removal of the image of the nonsmooth complement. The map factors through the finite normalization, whose fibre above the vertex consists of one point: the proper surjection from the connected exceptional V cannot cover two points of a finite discrete fibre. Strict henselization preserves the relevant regularity and exceptional-divisor statements.
3. The principal function f=tℓ has smooth strict transform D equal to the total line bundle over the incidence projective bundle H0→C. This supplies a regular punctured target without a projective-normality assumption.
4. The first-neighbourhood deformation class is the boundary of ℓH in the normal sequence twisted by L. Its curve components δ(z_i H) are exactly the three surviving degree-five Fermat Jacobian-ring monomials. The dimensions used are consistent: h0(O_C(5))=18, the derivative image has dimension15, and its cokernel H1(O_C) has dimension3. The Euler-sequence computation gives H1(T_P²|C⊗K_C)=0.
5. Serre duality turns these three independent boundary classes into an injective contraction cκ:H0(K_C)→H1(V,L). The same argument has rank3 for every m≥2.

## The integral family argument

The key improvement over the earlier possible route is that the proof uses the obstruction for an actual Poincaré family. It does not infer a global vector-group extension from a tangent calculation.

The square-zero extension obstruction for a line bundle on V×P is κ contracted with its V-Atiyah class. Its mixed component is the image of the normalized Poincaré tensor under cκ. Since cκ is injective, an extension to V2×P forces ψ*:H1(J,O_J)→H1(P,O_P) to vanish. For a homomorphism of complex abelian varieties this forces ψ=0. Normalization by the Abel-map diagonal identifies the mixed tensor correctly; only an irrelevant overall sign depends on conventions.

The previously audited finite-descent and smooth-compactification argument supplies an actual line bundle on X×P with P an abelian variety and fibre0 trivial. Its divisor closure on the regular noetherian product Z×P is Cartier. The fibre0 correction is O(bV), since V is the sole boundary prime divisor. Restriction to V×P is therefore a family in Pic0(V), and the obstruction proves that all its fibres there are trivial after a harmless pullback twist from P.

The square-zero unit sequence then expresses its class on V2×P by H1(V×P,L). The Künneth decomposition shows that restriction to any point of the proper connected P is the same map on the first summand and zero on the second. Hence all fibre classes on V2 agree, and the normalized fibre0 makes them trivial. For r≥2, both H1(V,L^r) and H2(V,L^r) vanish. The resulting Picard isomorphisms and surjective unit maps provide compatible trivializations on every infinitesimal neighbourhood.

## Completion and target injection

The equality m_R O_Z=I_V is justified in both directions: the old Segre coordinates generate the local ideal (t), and every additional normalization function in the maximal ideal vanishes on the reduced divisor V. Thus the V-adic formal neighbourhood is exactly the one to which proper formal full faithfulness applies after completing R.

Proper formal full faithfulness gives a trivialization on the completed resolution and hence on the completed puncture. A rank-one reflexive extension Q=j_*M is finite. Its local cohomology in degrees0 and1 vanishes; flat base change and the fact that the completion has a one-point closed fibre identify Q⊗Rhat with the extension of the now trivial punctured line bundle. It is Rhat. Faithfully flat descent makes Q a finite projective rank-one module over the local ring R, hence free. This closes the passage from formal triviality to the original strict local puncture.

The target injection Pic0(C)→Pic(Y) is also integral. A trivial target restriction would differ on the regular D by O(bH0). Restriction to a line in a P^{m−1} fibre gives b=0, and the projective-bundle Picard formula then gives the trivial source line bundle. This is where m≥2 is used essentially. The Poincaré family gives ordinary algebraic equivalence of its image. The point involving a transcendental coordinate has infinite order because all torsion points of the Qbar-defined Jacobian are Qbar-rational and the Abel map is a Qbar-defined closed immersion.

## Independently opened sources

- Illusie, *Cotangent complex and deformations of torsors and group schemes* (1972), Theorem2.6, printed p.166, and formula(2.7.2), printed p.167. Primary reprint opened at https://bibliotheque.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/media/filer_public/ce/24/ce243c31-2953-498e-92cb-c8b2e70d207d/i_illusie-64.pdf . The torsor obstruction is exactly the extension class composed with the Atiyah class. For G_m the co-Lie term is the trivial line, giving the displayed H2 obstruction. The draft also provides a direct Čech proof.
- Illusie's author errata, opened at https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~luc.illusie/ErrHalifax.pdf . Its corrections concern printed pp.177,185,189 and do not change the used theorem or formula.
- Stacks Project, Lemma30.24.1, Tag0883, opened at https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0883 . Its assumptions are precisely a noetherian complete base and a proper morphism; its conclusion is full faithfulness of formal completion for coherent sheaves. The proof uses it only after completing the base.

## Status limitation

This audit proves no assertion in dimension3 and makes no identification between ordinary Chow algebraic equivalence and all connected local Picard families. The construction establishes the stated failure of surjectivity for N≥4; previously proved injectivity results are compatible with a zero source.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3ab_surface_cone_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3ab: integral surface-cone top cycles

**Reviewer:** `/root/frobenius`. **Tool-clock date:** 2026-11-19 08:11:29 UTC.  
**Draft reviewed:** `research/agents/real_weil/C3_surface_cone_top_draft.tex`.  
**Outcome:** full typed proof and targeted primary-source applicability check pass, subject only to the harmless displayed-relation comma typo reported to the owner. This is a proof audit of the stated theorem and its use of the cited results, not an independent reproof of all cited K-theory theorems.

## Exact scope

The theorem concerns the strict henselization at the vertex of the normal, degree-one-generated full section ring of a smooth projective surface over an algebraically closed characteristic-zero field. Its extra condition is the vanishing of H²(V, Ω¹ over Q tensor L^j) for every j≥1. It does not silently replace absolute differentials by relative differentials. Under that condition the conclusion is integral: CH² of the puncture is CH₀(V)/(L CH¹(V)), and the ordinary algebraic-equivalence quotient is Z/gZ, where g is the positive gcd of intersection degrees of L with integral curves. The stronger assertion for arbitrary three-dimensional local germs is not made.

## Proof checks

1. **Resolution and infinitesimal neighbourhoods.** The section-ring assumptions identify the blow-up with the total space of L inverse. Strict henselization preserves the Artinian quotients because the residue field is already algebraically closed. The closed-fibre thickenings are therefore the actual split truncated graded algebras O⊕L⊕…⊕L^(a−1), with their projections to V. This splitting is used, correctly, at both the relative K-group and determinant steps.

2. **Finite detection, not an unjustified formal limit.** Morrow's Theorem 3.7(i) applies to the noetherian quasi-excellent Q-schemes in this square. Its relative pro-isomorphism supplies the displayed pro Mayer–Vietoris exact sequence. Since K₀(R) and each K₀(R/m^a) are Z with identity transition maps, quotienting the graph n↦(n,n[O_Z]) identifies the cokernel with constant K₀(Z). Thus K₀(Z) maps monomorphically to the pro-system K₀(V_a). The kernel system consists of decreasing subgroups of one fixed group and inclusion transition maps. Pro-zero therefore forces one of these subgroups to be zero. The claim of injectivity at all sufficiently large finite thickenings is justified and stronger than pointwise separation by an inverse limit.

3. **Relative K₂ retains integral information.** Weibel's Consequence 1.4 explicitly says that the relative K-groups of a nilpotent ideal in a Q-algebra are Q-vector spaces. Consequently Goodwillie's rational comparison is an isomorphism of the underlying integral abelian groups here. For A[t]/(t^a), in positive degree j<a the quotient of t^jΩ¹_A plus A t^(j−1)dt by d(b t^j) has the unique horizontal representative t^j(ω−db/j). Horizontal inclusion is injective and surjective. Under t′=u t it transforms by u^j, so the global summand is Ω¹ over Q tensor L^j. The relation a t^(a−1)dt=0 removes degree a. No extra conormal summand remains. The displayed draft had a literal comma in `t^j,db`; the owner was asked to replace it by a space.

4. **Descent spectral sequence and determinant.** Thomason–Trobaugh's Zariski descent theorem applies to both K-theory spectra and hence their fibre. On a surface, only H¹ of relative K₁ and H² of relative K₂ can contribute to relative K₀, since relative K₀ sheaf is zero. The latter term vanishes by hypothesis, and the H¹ term has no incoming or outgoing differential. The determinant identifies the surviving piece with relative Picard. The split restriction then identifies SK₀(V_a) with SK₀(V), and finite detection plus the section p* identifies SK₀(Z) with SK₀(V).

5. **Integral vanishing of CH₀(Z).** At each closed point z of the proper regular resolution, a regular system of parameters with first parameter the equation of V produces a one-dimensional integral transverse germ. Its closure D has zero-dimensional closed fibre. The proper image of a non-quasi-finite locus would meet the closed point of the local base, so D is quasi-finite and proper, hence finite. Its normalization is finite by excellence and local because it is an integral finite algebra over a henselian local ring. It is a DVR with residue field k. A uniformizer has the unique closed point as divisor; proper pushforward kills [z] with coefficient one. This is an integral argument.

6. **Codimension-two Riemann–Roch.** Fulton's Example 15.3.6 gives the two composites with multiplier (−1)^(2−1)(2−1)!=−1, so CH² and F²K₀/F³K₀ are integrally isomorphic. The limiting argument through smooth pointed étale blow-up neighbourhoods carries vector bundles, support filtrations, cycles, and finite rational-equivalence witnesses to the strict-local Z. Its F³ group is generated by point classes and vanishes by the previous step. Thus the Chern-class comparison and pullback really give CH²(Z)≅CH₀(V).

7. **Localization and ordinary algebraic equivalence.** Self-intersection sends the boundary map to multiplication by −L. Actual flat pullback on the fixed products with parameter curves preserves ordinary algebraic equivalence. The already audited C.3e injection modulo that equivalence is the same map as the resulting surjection, so it is an isomorphism. Zero-cycles on V modulo ordinary algebraic equivalence are classified by degree, yielding Z/gZ. Positivity of g follows from ampleness.

8. **Vanishing examples.** Akizuki–Kodaira–Nakano gives H²(Ω¹ over k tensor L^j)=0 on every such characteristic-zero surface. For k=Qbar the absolute and relative sheaves agree. For arbitrary k, the exact differential sequence and H²(L^j)=0 give the absolute condition; the possibly infinite constant vector space Ω¹_(k/Q) causes no problem because coherent cohomology on this finite affine cover commutes with direct sums. Serre duality proves the additional H²(L^j) vanishing for K3 surfaces and for surfaces with p_g=0, exactly as claimed.

## Independently opened or read primary locators

- Morrow, *Pro cdh-descent for cyclic homology and K-theory*, Theorem 3.7(i), author pp.21–22. Opened full author PDF and read the theorem and characteristic-zero proof: https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~matthew.morrow/Morrow,%20M.,%20Pro%20cdh-descent%20for%20cyclic%20homology%20and%20K-theory.pdf . Tool references `turn151369822view0`, `turn295699166view0`.
- Weibel, *Mayer–Vietoris sequences and mod p K-theory*, Consequence 1.4, printed p.392. Opened https://sites.math.rutgers.edu/~weibel/archive/papers-dir/MVmodpK-966.pdf (`turn151369822view1`) and independently viewed the full primary page image at `research/agents/real_weil/sources/weibel1984_p392.png`. The owner's source bibliography records the publisher/author-webpage year discrepancy; the old local filename is not a date claim.
- Goodwillie, *Relative algebraic K-theory and cyclic homology*, Main Theorem, printed p.348. Read directly from the primary local PDF `research/agents/real_weil/sources/goodwillie1986.pdf`, extracted text `/tmp/frob_goodwillie.txt`. A guessed Rochester filename failed and was not used as a retrieved source.
- Thomason–Trobaugh, *Higher algebraic K-theory of schemes and of derived categories*, Theorem 10.3, printed pp.383–386. Opened https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/Thomason-Trobaugh.pdf ; exact source references `turn151369822view2`, `turn295699166view1`.
- Fulton, *Intersection Theory*, second edition, Example 15.3.6, printed p.298. Opened the full-book primary-text reproduction https://djvu.online/file/Pl4avuZzLcI6I and found the exact example and its adjacent formulas (`turn394834927view0`, `turn788743614view0–1`).
- Esnault–Viehweg, *Lectures on Vanishing Theorems*, Theorem 1.3(a), printed p.4. Read directly from `research/agents/real_weil/sources/esnault_viehweg1992.pdf`, PDF page8, including the preceding characteristic-zero base-change explanation.

The audit adds no new assertion about unrestricted local top-cycle finiteness.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3ac_finite_field_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3ac

Reviewer: `/root/frobenius`. Date: 19 November 2026. Result: **passed**, with the stated 2026 source dependency retained.

## Scope checked

We independently read the complete `cycles/finite_field_abelian.tex` fragment. Its unconditional conclusions are the vanishing of every nonzero Beauville grade, the vanishing of ordinary algebraically trivial Chow groups after tensoring with Q, and Chow hard Lefschetz for abelian varieties over the algebraic closure of a finite field. The rational–numerical comparison, strict positivity, and equivalence with the corresponding numerical category remain explicitly conditional on the source-qualified 2026 CM Hodge input already audited in D.4c–e. Nothing here independently verifies that preprint's analytical proof.

## Primary source inspection

1. Künnemann, *A Lefschetz decomposition for Chow motives of abelian schemes*, Inventiones Mathematicae 113 (1993): we independently viewed the primary scans of printed pp. 99–100. Theorem 7.1 is exactly `CH^p(h^i(A),Q)=0` for `i != 2p`, over a finite field. Its proof on p. 100 uses purity of `h^1` and tensor powers. Theorem 7.3 on p. 100 is exactly the ample-divisor Chow hard Lefschetz isomorphism. Item 7.2 is a definition, not this theorem. The independent primary URL opened in this audit chain was <https://gdz.sub.uni-goettingen.de/download/pdf/PPN356556735_0113/LOG_0011.pdf>; local decisive scans are `research/agents/cycles/output/Kunnemann1993-16.png` and `Kunnemann1993-17.png`.
2. Kahn, *Équivalences rationnelle et numérique sur certaines variétés de type abélien sur un corps fini*, Annales scientifiques de l'École Normale Supérieure 36 (2003): we opened the primary PDF <https://www.numdam.org/item/ASENS_2003_4_36_6_977_0.pdf>, read Definition 1, printed p. 978, and independently viewed Theorem 1.10 and its complete proof, printed p. 982. The theorem concerns the actual rational-equivalence Chow groups and requires the product to lie in `B_tate`, not merely that the Frobenius grading be pure. Its proof explicitly invokes Kimura finiteness, Frobenius semisimplicity, and Proposition 1.7. The category in Definition 1 includes Artin motives and abelian motives. The decisive scan is `research/agents/cycles/output/Kahn2003-06.png`.
3. Soulé, *Groupes de Chow et K-théorie de variétés sur un corps fini*, Mathematische Annalen 268 (1984): we independently viewed printed p. 343, §3.5.5, Corollary 3, in the local primary scan `research/agents/cycles/output/Soule1984_pages/page-28.png`. The hypothesis is explicitly `Y_j = X_j tensor K` with `X_j` defined over the finite field. It does not cover arbitrary nonconstant varieties over a function field. The direct web retrieval of <https://gdz.sub.uni-goettingen.de/download/pdf/PPN235181684_0268/LOG_0047.pdf> failed during this check; the local primary page was successfully inspected. No failed retrieval is represented as opened.

## Mathematical checks

* Cycles and rational-equivalence witnesses descend to finite extensions. This justifies taking the filtered union of finite-field Chow groups. The canonical multiplication projectors commute with base change; the grading convention `i = 2r-s` is correct.
* The argument works for every ample class. Over the algebraic closure of a finite field its antisymmetric Picard component is torsion, so the rational symmetric-class convention introduces no restriction.
* For the asserted Gysin surjectivity, the range `2r >= g-1` makes both exponents in the displayed factorization nonnegative. Hard Lefschetz supplies `z = h^(2r+2-g) a`; then `z = i_* i^*(h^(2r+1-g) a)`. The dimensions and Chow codimensions agree. Ordinary Chow localization, rather than a nonexistent homological-equivalence localization sequence, supplies the complement assertion.
* Under the qualified Tate input, Tate surjectivity passes through products, algebraic projectors, direct sums, Tate twists, and Artin factors. Kahn's `B_tate` hypothesis is therefore available for the claimed abelian-type Chow motives.
* A geometric numerically trivial cycle descends to a finite field and is numerically trivial there: every finite-field complementary test cycle is among the geometric test cycles. Kahn then makes the descended representative rationally zero. This proves the geometric injectivity without an unsupported descent of every geometric numerical test cycle.
* Applying the same argument to products proves full faithfulness on correspondences. Idempotent splitting gives the stated equivalence only onto the corresponding generated numerical category. The manuscript correctly excludes arbitrary source varieties outside this category.
* Strict positivity uses the separate qualified Hodge-standard-conjecture input. Chow hard Lefschetz alone is not used to infer positivity or injectivity of grade-zero cycle classes.

No correction to the typed fragment is required by this audit. The constant-variety warning and the qualification of the recent CM Hodge theorem are mathematically material and should remain.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3ad_rational_top_cones_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3ad

Reviewer: `/root/frobenius`. Date: 19 November 2026; last tool clock 08:38:15 UTC. Result: **passed in the stated rational, smooth-cone, algebraic-number-field scope**.

We read the entire typed `real_weil/C3_rational_top_cones_draft.tex` and independently checked its new source inputs. The claimed statement is `CH^d(X)_Q = CH_0(V)_Q / (L CH^(d-1)(V)_Q)` and ordinary `B^d(X)_Q=0`, for a normal degree-one cone over a smooth projective d-fold over Qbar, d at least two. No unrestricted-germ, integral higher-dimensional, or higher-restriction conclusion is certified.

## Primary source checks

* Loday, *Cyclic Homology*, Theorems 4.6.7–4.6.8, printed pp. 151–152: we independently rendered and viewed both complete primary pages from `real_weil/sources/loday1992_cyclic_homology.pdf`. Its acquisition manifest records the URL <https://www.math.univ-paris13.fr/~vallette/GdT/Cyclic%20Homology%20-%20Loday.pdf>, 20,583,505 bytes, SHA256 `9161a46e6a59753156137364d4926ca66085bc6d9c1375d9b86179868d810eea`. Our separate browser open hit the size limit; successful source inspection was local. The decisive renders are `frobenius/output/Loday1992_page_166.png` and `Loday1992_page_167.png`. The hypotheses are exactly a commutative unital algebra over a ring containing Q. The upper Hodge-index vanishing and the top component `HC_n^(n)=Omega^n/dOmega^(n-1)` do not require smoothness. We read the proofs, including the use of the cokernel of the Hochschild boundary and of Connes's operator.
* Cortiñas–Haesemeyer–Weibel, *Infinitesimal cohomology and the Chern character to negative cyclic homology*: opened the complete primary PDF <https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0703133> and read Theorem 6.1 and Corollary 6.2, author p. 15, together with Theorem B.10, author p. 25. They give the compatible scheme-level relative comparison and the required shift `K^(d) -> HC^(d-1)[1]`. The opened PDF has an arXiv v2 header dated July 2008 and an internal August 2018 date; this discrepancy is recorded rather than silently normalized.
* Fulton, *Intersection Theory*, Example 15.2.16, printed pp. 294–295: independently reopened and read the primary book text at <https://djvu.online/file/Pl4avuZzLcI6I>. The rational Chern-character isomorphism for nonsingular varieties is exactly the input used before passing through pointed étale models.
* The Morrow, Thomason–Trobaugh, and Esnault–Viehweg inputs are the same exact primary statements independently checked in the immediately preceding C.3ab audit. The proof uses no additional smooth-HKR assertion for the nonregular infinitesimal thickenings.

## Proof audit

1. The pro cdh square and the graph quotient of the local `K_0=Z` terms give finite detection exactly as in C.3ab. Passing to a rational Adams summand does not weaken the asserted injectivity.
2. In internal degree j of `A[t]/t^a`, the formula `t^j omega + t^(j-1) dt wedge eta -> t^j(omega - d eta/j)` has the correct sign. Subtracting `d(t^j eta)/j` removes the vertical term. The remaining exact relations map to zero. The relation `a t^(a-1)dt=0` removes degree a and there are no larger internal degrees. Because the splitting supplies the pullback of base forms, the horizontal pieces glue as `Omega^(d-1) tensor L^j` with no additional conormal summand.
3. The relative weight-d sheaves vanish for q below d. In the descent spectral sequence the K_0 diagonal has q=b and b at most d; the only possible term is `H^d(Omega^(d-1) tensor L^j)`. Over Qbar, absolute differentials over Q are relative differentials over the field. Kodaira–Akizuki–Nakano kills that term since `(d-1)+d>d` for d at least two. Thus the full relative weight-d K_0 group vanishes.
4. Finite detection and the split projection give the stated weight-d isomorphism on the resolution. The smooth pointed étale models are adequate for rational Chern character and its compatibility with restriction and flat pullback; finite complexes and rational-equivalence data descend.
5. Localization and the self-intersection identity identify the quotient by `-c_1(L)`. All maps used to compare the fixed product families preserve ordinary algebraic equivalence. The second injection in C.3e is exactly the needed converse on ordinary quotients. The degree of an ample class on a curve is nonzero, so the top degree quotient vanishes over Q.

No mathematical correction to the typed draft is required. The rational-coefficient and Qbar qualifications are essential to this proof and should remain.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3ae_higher_local_surjectivity_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent complete audit of C.3ae

Reviewer: `/root/frobenius`. Tool clock at certification: **2026-11-19 08:53:01 UTC**. Result: **passed**.

We read the complete typed `cycles/higher_local_surjectivity.tex` after it appeared, including the integral cokernel statement, explicit equations, normalization, specialization invariant, conormal obstruction, Hodge projection, and actual-family argument. The conclusion is an integral surjection from the cokernel of **ordinary algebraically trivial codimension-two restriction** to `Pic^0(C)(C)`, for every local dimension at least seven. This is stronger than a single rational witness, but it is not a theorem about the image of arbitrary `CH^2(X)`.

## 1. The algebraic local scheme

The four displayed matrices agree entry for entry with the first tuple in the retained rank computation and with the tuple used in the basepoint computation. The coefficient of the linear fiber variable has no common divisor with the Fermat equation: `F=ell G=0` has codimension two in the smooth base. The graph description therefore gives an integral hypersurface with no extra vertical component. The differential of F makes it smooth near the entire exceptional divisor.

The Segre blowdown is proper and is an isomorphism away from its zero section. Removing the closed image of the singular locus is legitimate because that image misses the vertex. Normality of the resulting model supplies factorization through the finite normalization. Its connected exceptional divisor maps to one point of the finite fiber, and surjectivity shows there are no other points over the vertex. The dimension is `m+4=N`.

For complete explicitness, the scheme-theoretic fiber assertion can be justified in one sentence: every function in the normalized local maximal ideal vanishes on V, while the Segre coordinate functions `t x_i w_j z_k` generate `O(-V)` because their coefficient sections are basepoint free. Hence `m O_Z=O(-V)`. This is an immediate clarification of the written construction, not an additional existence hypothesis.

The strict transform of `f=t ell` is the split line bundle over H. The four-section surjection has kernel of rank m, so H is the smooth projective bundle of lines in that kernel. The normal line and the transversality of the two divisors are correct. Étale descent and nilpotent invariance of pointed étale neighborhoods preserve the specified first neighborhood.

## 2. Exact finite certificates

The earlier independent certificate audit was repeated with a distinct column-wise matrix construction and finite-field `DomainMatrix` operations. Rank is 57 for the 64 by 57 matrix, and the determinant of its first 57 rows is **47 modulo 101**. We also recomputed all twelve projective charts using lexicographic rather than grevlex Groebner bases; all are `[1]`. This is emptiness over the geometric fiber, not merely absence of F101-points. Properness of the common-zero scheme over Z promotes the empty fiber at 101 to an empty generic fiber.

The independently reproducible code is `code/audit_quartic_certificates.py`, with exact outputs in `output/audit_quartic_certificates.json` and `.out`. These computations certify only the linear algebra and basepoint freeness; the following steps are the separate mathematical audit.

## 3. The specialization invariant

Chow localization on the fixed proper model times a fixed proper parameter gives a lift of every class on the puncture. Two lifts differ by a boundary pushforward. Refined restriction changes that difference by multiplication by the normal Chern class `-(h+a)`, exactly the stated denominator. Thus sigma is well-defined on ordinary Chow groups, including all rational-equivalence witnesses.

The parameter-fiber embeddings and the exceptional-divisor embedding are independent regular embeddings. Their refined pullbacks commute. Hence sigma, projective-bundle evaluation, intersection with tau, and proper pushforward all commute with parameter fibers. There is no change of the parameter-dependent étale neighborhood in this argument.

For rank E equal to m at least three, the first projective-bundle relation has codimension at least three. Therefore in codimension two the quotient by `h+a` is exactly the base Chow group, with evaluation `b0+h b1+h^2 b2 -> b0-a b1+a^2 b2`. The source formula follows from the transverse refined restrictions to V and to the strict transform of the principal divisor. This argument remains integral.

## 4. The first-neighborhood obstruction

We independently opened the primary Stacks statements <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FEX> (Lemma 42.58.1) and <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FEZ> (especially Remark 42.59.9). They give the rational Chern-character realization on the regular scheme and its compatibility with refined pullback. The alternative argument through smooth finite-type pointed étale models is also valid, since the cycles, virtual bundles, and maps are finite data and the parameter is fixed and proper.

For a rank-one conormal kernel, the second exterior-power sequence has exactly the displayed kernel `L tensor Omega^1`. Its cohomological boundary is contraction with the first-neighborhood extension class. The local transition-matrix/Atiyah-class calculation in the draft supplies a lift of the degree-two Chern character through the middle term. Consequently every closure class satisfies the required necessary vanishing. No converse deformation criterion or unproved sufficiency of a Hodge condition is used.

The projection of the construction to `P^m × W` shows that the extension class has only a curve-tangent component. The three `delta(z_k G)` classes are the basis of the degree-five Fermat Jacobian quotient. Taking the source basis Serre-dual to them makes the obstruction coefficients precisely the displayed integers, without an unproved coordinate normalization.

## 5. The Hodge projection and boundary corrections

The Euler and normal sequences give the dual descriptions `H^1(Omega_W^1)/C H_W = R4^*` and `H^1(Omega_W^1(1))=R3^*`. Multiplication by a coordinate is dual to `R3 -> R4`. Thus the certificate matrix acts on exactly the claimed quotient; no unmentioned self-dual monomial basis is needed. The hyperplane class is killed by all these twisted multiplication maps.

In the selected target component, contraction can only act on the curve differential. Its source is exactly `H^1(W,Omega_W^1) tensor H^0(C,K_C) tensor H^1(P,O_P)`. Projective-space differential terms cannot enter that component. The injectivity of the 64 by 57 matrix makes the primitive W-component vanish. Pairing with tau then annihilates the remaining hyperplane component.

The correction terms are explicitly controlled. Since the K3 surface has no first cohomology of O and no global one-forms, a varying mixed divisor component on `W × C × P` comes from `C × P`. Multiplication by a adds `H_W`, which pairs to zero with tau. All other divisor terms, and the codimension-zero term, are constant in the parameter. Thus the boundary correction cannot restore a varying Picard component.

The intersection numbers `H_W^2=4`, `H_W D_W=1`, `D_W^2=-2`, `tau H_W=0`, and `tau D_W=-9` are correct.

## 6. Actual families and the conclusion

Every ordinary algebraic-equivalence generator can be compactified to a smooth proper parameter curve by closing its cycle in the fixed product; its specified open fibers do not change. The constructed line bundle on `C × P` has zero mixed Hodge component, so its classifying map to `Pic^0(C)` is constant. Indeed the induced homomorphism from the Jacobian of P has zero map on `H^1(O)`; over C it therefore has zero differential and is the zero homomorphism. This proves vanishing of rho on the source **ordinary** group.

The target classes obtained from `D_W` and a degree-zero divisor on C are actual ordinary algebraic families. Their rho image is exactly `-9` times the input. Divisibility of the complex points of `Pic^0(C)` gives the asserted integral cokernel surjection. The specified transcendental point yields a nontorsion Abel class because all torsion points of this Qbar-defined Jacobian are Qbar-defined and the Abel map is a closed immersion over Qbar. The resulting rational failure of surjectivity follows.

No remaining mathematical gap was found in this typed proof. The distinction between ordinary algebraically trivial cycles and arbitrary Chow classes is essential and is correctly retained.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3af_all_degree_local_surjectivity_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent complete-proof audit: C.3af

**Outcome: passed for the full-basis draft read at 09:03–09:04 UTC, 2026-11-19.** File audited: `research/agents/cycles/all_degree_local_surjectivity.tex`. This review covers the threshold N≥3·4^(r−1)+2r−1. A later proposed sharper coefficient construction is not included in this certification.

The construction has q=3·4^(r−1) explicit basis sections with coefficients0/1. Their basepoint freeness follows at every point by choosing one nonzero coordinate in each factor. The dimension count m=N−2r≥q−1 gives a rank-m incidence projective bundle whose first relation is above codimensionr. The projective-bundle evaluation h→−a therefore provides the stated exact Chow quotient, independently of any Hodge calculation.

The conormal exterior sequence has kernel L tensor Ω^(r−1). The Chern character of an actual virtual-bundle lift of the closure supplies the vanishing under contraction with the first-neighborhood class. Rational Chern characters suffice, since only a mixed Hodge component is being killed; the resulting morphism between complex abelian varieties is zero integrally once its differential is zero.

The projection of the obstruction is correctly isolated. The deformation contracts only a C differential. Multiplication by a section on each K3 factor preserves that factor's separate cohomology and differential degrees. Thus (2,0) and (0,2) factors, and all terms with positive projective-space degree, cannot feed the selected tensor of H1(Ω1) factors. On each Fermat K3, the joint multiplication map has kernel precisely C·H_i; its quotient is dual to the surjective degree1 times degree3 multiplication onto degree4 of the Jacobian ring. Tensoring these injections yields exactly the sum of kernels with an H_i factor. Pairing with all τ_i kills that sum.

Every correction term a^j b_j with j≥1 is also handled. A canonical class from C gives the wrong curve cohomology degree for the mixed Picard component. An H_i factor must pair with τ_i and a degree-zero residual factor, hence vanishes because τ_iH_i=0. Repeated H_i factors have excessive degree. This reasoning applies to arbitrary cycles on the product and makes no divisor-generation assumption.

The invariant commutes with fibres of actual families on the fixed puncture. Compactifying a curve parameter and closing its cycle does not replace the ordinary equivalence relation. Vanishing of the mixed Hodge component makes the induced parameter-Jacobian homomorphism zero, so the source ordinary subgroup is killed. The target Poincare family has composite (−9)^(r−1) times the identity. Divisibility of Pic0(C)(C) proves the integral cokernel surjection, and the specified nontorsion point proves rational nonsurjectivity.

No flaw was found in the full-basis proof. It uses an algebraic tensor argument for every r, not extrapolation from finite matrices. The improved r2 threshold from C.3ae is correctly kept separate. The proof relies on the previously independently audited C.3ae local-geometric and Chern-character constructions, with the changes in dimensions and ranks explicitly checked here.

## Sharper section construction: complete follow-up audit

**Passed at 09:06:40 UTC, 2026-11-19**, after reading the newly typed Step5 in full and independently reproducing its finite certificate. This extends the certification to the stated bound **N≥4^(r−1)+2r−1**.

The independent script `code/audit_quartic_column_pairs.py` reads only the selected coefficient tuple, assembles the three column matrices separately, and uses lexicographic Groebner bases rather than the owner's grevlex order. All twelve chart ideals are [1] modulo101; the column determinants are independently reproduced as −36,4,200. Runtime0.190871seconds. Complete output is retained in `output/audit_quartic_column_pairs.json` and `.out`. Properness of the projective determinantal loci over Z makes this a characteristic-zero emptiness certificate.

The all-s argument also checks independently. For s≥2, the three first column factors are pairwise independent and the three remaining tensor factors are pairwise independent. Any dependence of the three full pure tensors must use all three terms. If the first three factors span dimension3 it is impossible immediately. If they span dimension2, the third has two nonzero coefficients in the first two, and comparison forces proportional remaining factors, again impossible. Thus the compressed sections are basepoint free. The s=1 case is exactly the already audited original C.3ae basepoint-free calculation.

The correlation of coefficients in the obstruction is correctly handled. The rank57 original map is an injection from Ubar tensor E_C. The linear map E_C→E_C^(tensor s) sending the chosen basis vector e_k to e_k^(tensor s) is injective. Composing its tensor with the s-fold product of the original injection produces exactly the coefficient products in the compressed sections. No additional C factors are introduced geometrically. Before passing to Ubar in each factor, the kernel is precisely the previously described sum with an H_i factor. The projection, correction terms, actual-family argument and integral conclusion therefore remain valid.

The smaller rank m≥4^(r−1)−1 still satisfies m>r for every r≥2. No exception at r2 is hidden: m≥3>2 and the threshold is exactly N≥7.

# Complete independent proof audit: C.3ag

**Outcome: passed, 2026-11-19 at 09:08:47 UTC (tool clock).** File read in full: `research/agents/cycles/dimension_six.tex`. The auxiliary Hodge fragment is independently proved and source-audited in `C3_N6_Hodge_inputs.tex`, `C3_N6_Hodge_source_audit.md`, and `C3_N6_Hodge_references.bib`.

The conclusion is an integral surjection from the cokernel of ordinary CH2_alg restriction, for an excellent normal strictly henselian complex local ring of dimension six with regular source and divisor punctures, onto Pic0 of the Fermat plane quartic. It implies rational ordinary nonsurjectivity. The statement properly distinguishes its Hodge-generic quartic from the integer-coefficient Fermat construction of C.3ae. It asserts no vanishing for the image of every arbitrary Chow class.

## 1. Surface and section choices

The fixed-line quartic family has thirty coefficients (35 monomials of degree4 minus the5 supported on the line). A Hodge-generic complex point is part of the specified data. The separate Hodge lemma proves that its Picard lattice is generated by H,D and that End_Hdg(T)=Q. This is not inferred merely from Picard rank2, and it is not asserted for every complex embedding of generic rational coefficients.

The field K0 generated by the thirty selected coefficients over Qbar is countable, so thirty-six algebraically independent complex section coefficients exist. This guarantees every nonempty Zariski-open condition over K0. The Bertini condition on three sections of the very ample M is nonempty; their reduced transverse common zero scheme has length3·4·4=48.

The second rank condition is also nonempty and defined over K0. The common kernel of the four multiplication maps H1(Ω_W)→H1(Ω_W(1)) is precisely C·H. This follows for every smooth quartic from the Euler/normal sequences, not merely the Fermat monomial presentation: R3^4→R4 is surjective because the quotient is homogeneous. Since τ is primitive and has square−36, some linear form w gives μ_w(τ)≠0. The special triple wz_i makes the required three-column map injective. It is used solely to demonstrate the nonempty rank-open, so its nongeneric base locus is immaterial. The two opens intersect in irreducible affine section space, and the chosen independent tuple lies in both.

## 2. Incidence and local geometry

H⊂P2×S is a P1-bundle away from the48 basepoints and has P2 fibres above them. At each basepoint the ds_i form a cotangent basis, so Σx_i ds_i is nonzero for all projective x. This proves smoothness. The exceptional fibres have codimension2 and cannot supply extra irreducible components, so H is integral.

The previous C.3ae contraction construction applies: regularity near V follows from the smooth Fermat curve equation, not from basepoint freeness of the selected sections. The full Segre coordinate system, not the three selected sections, generates the zero-divisor ideal. Thus the scheme-theoretic exceptional fibre is still V and its first neighbourhood is preserved. The dimension is dim(P2×W×C)+1=6. The strict transform of f=t·ell is the line-bundle total space on smooth H; its intersection with V is transverse. No flatness of H→S is used.

## 3. Integral incidence invariant

Removing p^{-1}(B)×P, of codimension2 in smooth H×P, preserves its Picard group. Removing B×P from S×P also preserves Picard. On the remaining projective bundle the relative degree-one class h splits Pic, giving exactly p*Pic(S×P) plus h·CH0. This is sufficient to control every divisor in the localization ambiguity.

The hypersurface class h+a in P2×S gives p*1=0, p*h=1, p*h2=a, p*h3=0. Expanding (h−2a)(h+a)(γ0+hγ1) and pushing gives −3a2γ1. Its product with τ pushes to zero on C×P: the τH2 term is already zero by dimension on W and the 2τH·K_C term has coefficient degree(τH)=0. Hence the map Φ, and therefore ρ=Φσ, is integrally well defined on the localization quotient.

For source restriction b=b0+h b1+h2b2, the same identities give exactly b0−a b1−2a2b2 after the push. This verifies the signs and the coefficient2. Pullbacks of divisors and their products are defined despite the nonflat p; all refined restrictions and proper pushes commute with parameter fibres.

## 4. Obstruction and all-parameter Hodge control

The conormal/Chern-character obstruction is the already audited necessary condition from C.3ae. The deformation only contracts a curve differential. The projection to H0(O_P2(1)) tensor H1(Ω_W(1)) tensor H1(K_C) tensor H1(O_P) therefore excludes all positive projective-space differential degrees.

The new Hodge lemma applies to every algebraic b0 on W×C×P. Its relevant W factor lies in span(H,τ); it makes no assertion that arbitrary individual transcendental Hodge pieces are algebraic. The H component is killed by all multiplication maps. The τ component is killed only when its coefficient is zero, by the independently justified rank-three condition. Pairing with τ then annihilates the selected mixed component.

The correction terms are controlled exactly, not only numerically as a family. Since Alb(W)=Pic0(W)=0, Pic(W×C×P) splits as Pic(W) plus Pic(C×P). For b1=D'+λ, pushing τab1 leaves only degree(τD')·K_C, pulled back from C and constant in P. The a2b2 correction pushes to zero. Thus the complete line bundle ρ_P of a restricted source family has zero mixed component.

## 5. Ordinary equivalence and integral conclusion

For every actual proper curve parameter, its normalized Picard classifying morphism factors through the parameter Jacobian. The selected mixed Hodge component is its differential. In characteristic zero, zero differential makes that homomorphism zero. Compactification of an actual smooth parameter curve and closure of its cycle on the fixed product reduce ordinary algebraic equivalence to this case; no change of étale model with the parameter is assumed.

The target family uses only products of divisor pullbacks. Its composite with ρ is exactly degree(τD)=−9 times the identity, because p*(h−2a)=1. Divisibility of Pic0(C)(C) yields the integral cokernel surjection. The specified point with transcendental affine coordinate is nontorsion because the Abel immersion and all torsion points of the Jacobian are defined over Qbar. This supplies the rational failure as well.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3ai_suspended_calabi_yau_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent complete-proof audit: C.3ai

**Outcome: passed.** Full typed proof read by frobenius on 2026-11-19; final certification at tool clock 09:26:20 UTC. Reviewed source file: `research/agents/cycles/suspended_calabi_yau.tex`. This review also covers its k=0 case, C.3ah, in `regular_ambient_calabi_yau.tex`.

## Statement and dimensions

For r≥1 and r+2≤N≤2r+1, the substitutions d=2r−N+2, k=N−r−2 give d≥1, k≥0, r=d+k and N=d+2k+2. The Fermat degree e=d+2 makes T a smooth Calabi–Yau d-fold. The suspended Fermat V has dimension n=d+2k. The regular ambient strict-local ring has dimension N, and its principal divisor is the strict-local cone on V.

## Integral correspondence identity

Each incidence resolution Z_i is the projective bundle of the direct sum of the tautological line on T and a trivial rank-k bundle. It has fibres P^k, dimension d+k, and codimension r in T×V. The notation with homogeneous coordinates is compatible with this bundle and does not choose a nonexistent global trivialization of O_T(−1).

For k>0, the two paired linear systems a_j=λ_i b_j force a_j=b_j=0 in their intersection, since λ_0≠λ_1. Hence the two joins meet scheme-theoretically in T. This avoids both vertices, so both incidence resolutions are isomorphisms there. Their tangent directions in the paired-coordinate 2k-space are complementary; their common tangent directions are precisely those of T. In T×V×T the intersection defining the composite is consequently the diagonal T with multiplicity one. Thus transpose(Γ_1)∘Γ_0=Δ_T integrally, not only cohomologically. For k=0 the explicit diagonal convention gives the same identity.

## Generic-point relation and Hodge contradiction

The selected P is geometric generic over Qbar and o is Qbar-rational. Their difference is represented by an actual connected proper curve family. The incidence correspondence preserves ordinary algebraic triviality; its restriction to such a parameter is a projective bundle, so there is no flat-family issue.

A hypothetical hyperplane relation can be descended from C to the algebraic closure of Qbar(T) by specializing its finite cycle/rational-function data, then to a finite field extension. Norm gives a nonzero integer multiple of the generic relation over Qbar(T). Closing the cycle β on T×V gives B of codimension r−1. Localization produces precisely

`b Γ_0 = H B + b(T × (Γ_0)_*[o]) + Θ`,

with Θ supported on a proper source divisor. In particular, the constant correspondence is not incorrectly claimed to have that support.

On H^(d,0)(T), the constant and source-divisor terms vanish. B has degree shift (k−1,k−1), so its image is H^(d+k−1,k−1)(V), in total degree n−2. If k=0 the second Hodge index is negative. If k≥1, weak Lefschetz identifies this below-middle group with projective-space cohomology, and the off-diagonal part vanishes because d>0. The explicit integral correspondence inverse forces Γ_0 to be injective on cohomology, giving the contradiction. All degrees and both endpoint conventions are consistent.

## Local passage and source vanishing

The Fermat gradient gives an isolated vertex singularity. The normal, projectively normal hypersurface cone has a section ring generated in degree one. The previously independently audited strict-cone injection C.3e therefore applies to the nonzero rational hyperplane-quotient class. Flat pullback of the actual proper curve family to the fixed puncture preserves ordinary algebraic triviality and yields a non-torsion target class.

The map C→R is geometrically regular, R is Noetherian and henselian, and the base has dimension zero. Skalit Corollary 6.4(i) therefore gives CH^r(Spec R)=0 integrally; localization gives CH^r(X)=0.

Reopened C. Skalit, *Regular morphisms and Gersten's conjecture*, arXiv:1710.00303v1, 1 October 2017, full primary PDF https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.00303. # Independent complete-proof audit: C.3aj

**Outcome: passed.** Frobenius read the entire typed draft `research/agents/real_weil/C3_high_range_surjectivity_draft.tex`, its auxiliary product Hodge fragment, and the precise source passages. Final certification: tool clock **2026-11-19 09:26:20 UTC**. The requested explicit complex-conjugation sentence was added by the owner and reread before certification.

## Exact assertion

For every r≥2 and N≥2r+2, the construction supplies an algebraizable excellent normal strict-local complex ring with regular punctures X and Y, and an integral cokernel map onto Pic^0(C)(C)/Pic^0(C)(Kbar). The countable algebraically closed field Kbar contains all finite construction data. The quotient is nonzero and uniquely divisible. This proves rational nonsurjectivity of ordinary algebraically trivial cycles in this entire range.

The quotient by Kbar is essential: the incidence boundary is proved constant on every proper family, not identically zero as a divisor class. The proof does not silently replace the boundary by zero.

## 1. Geometry and fields

The jointly Hodge-generic tuple of quartics exists with the stipulated NS groups, rational endomorphism fields, and pairwise nonisomorphic transcendental structures. The finite coefficient field K0 is countable even though its selected complex embedding is Hodge generic. The finitely many independent section coefficients produce a further countable field K.

The external line bundle M is very ample. General m+1 sections have smooth transverse base locus of expected codimension or empty base locus. This and the rank-three condition are independent nonempty Zariski opens over K0, so the specified algebraically independent coefficients satisfy both.

The incidence divisor H is smooth at the base locus because its section differentials are independent. Its projective-bundle open is integral and the exceptional part has codimension m≥2. The construction F+tℓG has the stated line-bundle degree, is smooth near its exceptional V, and is integral: its graph is dense and the locus F=ℓG=0 has codimension two in the smooth base and cannot be another divisor component. The full Segre contraction, normalization and proper shrinking yield the same regular strict-local model mechanism as C.3ae. The connected exceptional fibre implies a single point over the vertex. The full coordinate set generates the exceptional ideal, regardless of the base locus of the chosen linear section ℓ.

The strict transform of f=tℓ is Tot(L_H^−1), is smooth, and meets V transversely in H. All geometry preceding complex base change is defined over Kbar. Dimension is m+2r=N.

## 2. Integral incidence and boundary calculation

With s=r−1, τ=product(4D_ν−H_ν), the operator Φ_P has codimension one on C×P. It uses only divisor intersections and proper pushforward, so it does not assume p:H→S is flat. Its exact identities are

`p_* h^(m−1)=1`, `p_* h^m=a`, and `p_* h^(m+j)=0` for j≥1.

For boundary classes γ∈CH^(r−1)(H×P), weak Lefschetz gives an ambient lift of the H^(2r−3)(H)⊗H1(P) cohomology component. It does not claim a Chow lift. Writing that lift as Σh^jζ_j yields exactly

`Ψ_*(ζ)=pr_C,* τ(2a ζ_0 + a² ζ_1)`.

Every relevant odd class has its odd degree on C. A K_C insertion gives H3(C)=0. A W_ν degree-four term is killed by τ_ν. In the other terms there are exactly j+1 missing degree-two factors, so a^(j+1) must insert a hyperplane in one or more missing positions; the product includes deg(τ_νH_ν)=0. Thus the mixed component vanishes for all ambient cohomology classes. The classifying homomorphism of parameter Jacobians is zero, so the line bundle has equal fibre classes integrally.

## 3. The countable boundary subgroup

Every cycle on the smooth projective H_C is ordinarily algebraically equivalent to a cycle over Kbar. For an integral component, its fixed-polynomial Hilbert scheme is projective of finite type over the algebraically closed Kbar. Its irreducible component is defined over Kbar and contains a Kbar-point. A chain of proper integral curves, normalized, joins that point to the chosen complex point. The universal flat family gives actual proper-curve equivalences with integral coefficients. Lower-dimensional or embedded components do not affect the fundamental cycle in the fixed dimension.

Since Ψ kills such differences and is defined over Kbar, its entire image lies in Pic(C)(Kbar). Localization and self-intersection therefore make ρ on the actual strict-local puncture well defined modulo exactly that subgroup, including rational-equivalence witnesses and changes of closure. Refined restriction and proper pushforward commute with parameter fibres. No finite-type parameter space for cycles on the puncture is invoked.

Pic(C)/Pic(C)(Kbar) is naturally Pic^0(C)/Pic^0(C)(Kbar), since all degree classes have a Kbar representative. Divisibility is inherited from complex Jacobian points. If nλ is Kbar-rational, every point of its finite [n]-fibre is Kbar-rational, proving torsion-freeness and hence unique divisibility.

## 4. Chern obstruction and the Hodge calculation

The conormal exterior-power sequence is exact, with kernel L⊗Ω^(r−1). Its connecting homomorphism is contraction with the first-neighbourhood deformation class. The rational Chern-character isomorphism for the finite-dimensional regular projective model supplies a virtual bundle for any chosen cycle closure. Its Hodge Chern class lifts through the middle conormal term by its Atiyah class. Refined restriction is compatible with the Chern character. Thus the stated contraction vanishes; no sufficient lifting criterion is asserted.

The deformation class has only a C-tangent component. In the selected target summand, a projective-space differential cannot disappear, so h^j b_j with j>0 contributes nothing. Nor can a W factor with another differential or cohomological degree enter. The only relevant b0 component is the selected tensor of H1(Ω1) on every quartic, H0(K_C), and H1(O_P).

The auxiliary Hodge lemma proves that the rational antecedent has every W factor in NS. For each quartic, the common kernel of multiplication by all linear forms on H1(Ω1) is precisely the hyperplane line; the Jacobian-ring dual calculation proves this for every smooth quartic. Since τ_ν is independent of H_ν, some w_ν has μ_wν(τ_ν) nonzero. The special three sections (product w_ν)z_i give three independent target vectors with coefficients x_i. This proves the rank-three open condition. It does not claim this special witness tuple has good base locus.

All NS tensors with a hyperplane factor are killed. The remaining τ tensor coefficient in H0(K_C)⊗H1(O_P) must vanish by the rank-three map. Its complex conjugate vanishes because the original cycle, Kunneth projections and NS tensors are rational. This last explanation is now explicit in the typed file. It gives zero full mixed component.

## 5. Actual source families and integral target

The exact source formula is `Φ_P(b|_H)=pr_C×P,* τ(b0+a b1)`. The b0 mixed component vanishes by the Chern obstruction and τ_νH_ν=0. The b1 mixed component has one missing W degree-two factor, forcing the a insertion into that factor and giving the same zero intersection. Consequently every actual proper-curve source-family difference is killed by ρ. Compactifying a smooth open curve and closing its cycle on the fixed product retains its original fibres, so this covers ordinary algebraic equivalence.

The target θ uses only first Chern classes of pulled-back line bundles, so it remains defined for the nonflat incidence projection. Its Poincare family provides actual ordinary algebraic equivalences. Direct pushforward gives ρθ=(-9)^s times the Jacobian class modulo Kbar. Divisibility provides the integral cokernel surjection.

The chosen q=(u:v:1), u transcendental over Kbar and v^4=−1−u^4, has no nonzero multiple of its Abel difference [q−o] over Kbar. Finiteness of [n] and the Abel closed immersion prove this. Hence the quotient is nonzero and the failure persists after rationalization.

These hypotheses hold for the projective regular model over the excellent local base.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3an_top_p_tangent_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3an

**Verdict:** passed the complete typed proof, with primary geometry and symbol-source checks. Historical certification: **2026-11-19 09:56:09 UTC**. The Dennis–Stein sign comparison was corrected before **09:59:01 UTC**. This file is a reconstructed copy following the 10:14 UTC scratch reset; the audit was actually performed before that reset. The separately rerun certificate records its own fresh runtime.

Owner: `agents/real_weil/C3_top_p_tangent_draft.tex`.

## Exact scope

For every algebraically closed field of characteristic five, the construction gives an excellent normal strictly henselian local ring of dimension three, with regular puncture X, and an integral surjection `CH²(X)/CH²_alg(X) → (k,+)`. It is an exponent-five invariant. No rational nonvanishing, characteristic-zero result, or unrestricted higher-dimensional conclusion follows.

## Geometry checked

The plane quintic `Y⁵−YW⁴=Z⁴W` is smooth, has its unique point at infinity `(0:1:0)`, and has genus six. The affine differential dz has divisor `10∞`. With `q=Y/Z`, `h=W/Z`, the equation is `h=q⁵−qh⁴` and `z−q⁻⁵=h³/q⁴=q¹⁰c`, where `c=q(h/q⁵)³` is a uniformizer. This verifies the exact normalization rather than just its valuation.

For the extension `0→O(−2∞)→E→O→0` arising from dz, the lines convention gives a section with positive normal bundle O(2∞). The inseparable curve G has class `5S∞−10g*∞`. The double cover has inverse-root line bundle `O(−3S∞+5g*∞)` and equation `T²=s⁵−z`. The reduced section S has normal bundle O(∞), hence S²=1. For `D=S+g*∞`, the intersections are D²=3, D·S=2, and D·F=1; irreducible fibres and nonnegative intersections of distinct horizontal curves prove ampleness. The line bundle is L=5D.

At the section, `x=1/s_i`, `w=T_i/s_i³` give `w²=x−c_i x⁶` and `dT_i=−x³ dc_i/(2w)`. The vanishing order is five. The base-infinity transition `dT=q⁵dT_i` contributes the other part of 5D. The local calculation at the intersection verifies that no additional zero is hidden. Thus `η=dT` defines a nonzero section of `Ω¹_V⊗L⁻¹`.

The full section ring of L is normal and finitely generated. No degree-one generation or global generation of L is assumed. A sufficiently positive Veronese gives the standard proper contraction of Tot(L⁻ᵉ); the fibre-power map from Tot(L⁻¹) is finite. Properness over the original cone follows by the graph argument. Ratios of nonvanishing sections of consecutive sufficiently high powers give an invertible degree-one element locally away from the vertex, proving that the puncture is the Gm-bundle. The reduced exceptional fibre is V; the scheme fibre can be nonreduced. Pointed étale base change and strict henselization preserve regularity and the split first infinitesimal neighbourhood.

## Integral detector and actual curves

The typed proof separately establishes the integral map from CH² of the regular resolution to H² of relative K₂ on V₂. The relevant K₀ filtration comparison has coefficient `(2−1)!=1`; this is not a rational Chern-character shortcut. The only incoming descent differential comes from global units, which lift to actual K₁. Restriction to the surface thickening kills the third filtration step. The exceptional pushforward has K₀ representative `π*γ(1−L)`, hence pulls back from V and has zero relative class. Serre pairing with η gives the additive invariant.

The actual curve equations are `s=0,T=at,z=−a²t²,y⁵−y=a⁸t⁸`. For a≠0 the Artin–Schreier equation is irreducible: a hypothetical rational solution has no finite poles, and its pole order at infinity would make eight divisible by five. The germ at t=y=0 is smooth. Its closure and the finite normalization of its cone image allow a henselian branch factor to isolate precisely the chosen point. The proof therefore uses actual cycles on one fixed scheme, rather than a formal germ or a nonexistent simultaneous family.

The local Koszul difference maps to `ta ds/(sT)`. With the positive Steinberg normalization and the chosen wedge order, pairing with `η=t⁻¹dT` gives a. Ordinary algebraically trivial cycles form a divisible group because actual proper-curve families factor through Pic⁰ of their parameter curves. Every homomorphism from this group to the exponent-five additive target is zero. These facts yield the claimed surjection after quotienting by ordinary algebraic equivalence.

## Primary sources and sign correction

Mukai, RIMS1736 (2011), full primary PDF: <https://www.kurims.kyoto-u.ac.jp/preprint/file/RIMS1736.pdf>. Read Example1.3 p3, Lemma1.4 p4, §1.3/Definition1.6/Proposition1.7 pp5–6, Lemma2.2/Proposition2.3 p8, equation(25) p13, and p14. The p5 display and p14 divisor convention conflict. The minus sign used above was independently derived from local equations and the normal bundle. This is a statement about the opened preprint, not about an unopened published version. RIMS1505 pp2–3 was also opened; its normal-bundle derivation has the required minus sign.

Weibel, Chapter III, Definition5.11 p43, Theorem5.11.1(b) p44 and Exercise5.14 pp46–47 were opened in full. His map sends `⟨m,r⟩` to `+m dr`, and with his definition `⟨r,s⟩={r,1−rs}` it sends `{1+tc,u}` to `−tc dlog u`. The map used in C.3an is explicitly the negative, so it sends `⟨m,r⟩` to `−m dr` and `{1+tc,u}` to `+tc dlog u`. This is a conventional negation, not a change of group or scope. The source comparison is explicit in the reconstructed auxiliary lemma.

Van der Kallen's 1971 original four-page scan was visually read in full. Its dual-number isomorphism assumes 1/2, and therefore applies in characteristic five without any characteristic-zero hypothesis.
