# Independent audit of C.3aq

**Verdict:** the entire typed all-positive-characteristic proof passed at **2026-11-19 10:06:45 UTC**. Parent also approved the complete result. This report is reconstructed following the 10:14 UTC scratch reset; it records an audit actually completed before the reset.

Owner: `agents/real_weil/C3_top_all_positive_characteristics_draft.tex`. Scope: an integral surjection from the top ordinary-algebraic-equivalence quotient of a regular punctured strict-local threefold to the additive ground field, in every positive characteristic. This extends the edge-characteristic coverage of C.3an. It does not give rational nonvanishing, a characteristic-zero example, or a general-dimensional statement.

## The relative K₂ map is only a quotient in characteristic two

For a split square-zero extension S=R⊕I, the relative Dennis–Stein presentation gives a natural surjection `K₂(S,I)→I⊗Ω¹_R/Z`. With the positive Steinberg convention it is `⟨m,r⟩↦−m d r̄`, `⟨r,m⟩↦+m d r̄`. All three placements of the infinitesimal entry in relation D3 reduce to the Leibniz rule. D2's correction vanishes because I²=0; D1 gives skew symmetry. Symbols with both entries in I map to zero. No assertion that they themselves vanish in characteristic two is used. No surjectivity of H² is inferred from the sheaf surjection. Actual curves prove surjectivity of the final invariant directly.

The map glues for the conormal line bundle L. Since k is perfect of characteristic p, absolute differentials agree with Ω¹_V/k. The sign is the negative of Weibel's Exercise5.14(a), and it gives `{1+tc,u}↦tc dlog u`. The characteristic-two case does not need van der Kallen's 1/2 hypothesis.

## Uniform geometry

The choices `e=p+1`, `n=p(p+1)`, `d=n−3` give d>0 for every prime. The plane curve `Yⁿ−YWⁿ⁻¹=Zⁿ⁻¹W` is smooth, with one point at infinity and `div(dz)=n(n−3)∞=p·ed∞`. For q=Y/Z and h=W/Z, the exact identity is `z−q⁻ⁿ=qⁿ⁽ⁿ⁻³⁾c`, with `c=q(h/qⁿ)ⁿ⁻²` a uniformizer. Thus the claimed order is accompanied by the required unit differential.

The inseparable curve in the ruled surface is `G∼pS∞−p g*D₁`, where D₁=ed∞ and the infinity section has positive normal bundle O(D₁). The degree-e cyclic cover branched in G+S∞ has inverse-root bundle `O(−S∞+pd g*∞)` and affine equation `Tᵖ⁺¹=sᵖ−z`. Valuation one at the branch proves connectedness even for composite e. Smoothness follows because the two branch components are smooth and disjoint and e is prime to p.

For the reduced section S, `S²=d`. The divisor `D=pS+d g*∞` satisfies `D²=((p+1)²−1)d`, `D·S=(p+1)d`, and `D·F=p`. The fibres are integral cusps with coprime exponents p and p+1; all horizontal curves have positive D-degree. This proves ampleness in every characteristic, including p=2 and p=3.

At base infinity, `s=qᵉᵈs_i+q⁻ᵉ` and `T=qᵖᵈT_i`; at S, with x=1/s_i and w=T_i/s_i, `wᵖ⁺¹=x−cxᵖ⁺¹` and `dT_i=−xᵖ dc/wᵖ`. The section order is p² and the base-infinity order is pd. The intersection calculation shows that `div(dT)=pD` exactly, so with L=pD there is the required nonzero η in Ω¹_V⊗L⁻¹.

## Actual branches and the global quotient

The displayed equations `s=0,T=at,z=−aᵖ⁺¹tᵖ⁺¹,yⁿ−y=zⁿ⁻¹` have derivative −1 in y at the selected point. The proof chooses the unique irreducible component through this smooth point. It never claims irreducibility of the entire displayed polynomial. The finite-normalization and henselian branch argument then produces an actual integral curve on the fixed strict-local resolution.

The rest of the global Chow/K₂ construction is precisely the separately proved integral comparison from C.3an. It uses the quotient map above in place of an isomorphism. The local residue is a, so every element of k occurs. Exceptional pushforwards have zero relative class. Ordinary CH²_alg is divisible by the actual-proper-curve-family argument, and the additive target has exponent p, so the invariant descends. All coefficient, sign, type, and characteristic-two issues in this replacement have been checked.

The owner's original finite certificate was read with its stated scope; the arbitrary-p formulas above, rather than finite testing, are the proof. The owner reconstructs and reruns that certificate with fresh timings after recovery.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_Orlov_method_REVIEW.md

# A restricted Orlov-transform construction does not remove the even-surface denominator

## Status and scope

This note records a proved limitation of one attempted construction. It is not an obstruction to all algebraic cycles on A×Adual, and it does not resolve the remaining type(1,2) surface case. In particular we do not infer that the integral numerical class P²/2 is nonalgebraic.

The construction studied takes exterior products of algebraic K-classes on a fixed polarized abelian surface, applies the standard Orlov equivalence, and then forms integral sums, differences and tensor products before taking second Chern classes. The calculation below shows that when the polarization degree e is even, all resulting second Chern classes have an even coefficient in the P²/2 direction of the rational divisor-generated degree-four subspace.

## Primary source and exact normalization

We opened Markman's manuscript *Secant sheaves on abelian n-folds with real multiplication and Weil classes on abelian 2n-folds with complex multiplication*, arXiv:2509.23079v1, at https://arxiv.org/html/2509.23079v1 . Section10.1, equation(10.1.3) and the following sentence define the equivalence Φ as the inverse of μ_* composed with the Fourier–Mukai transform on the second factor, where μ(x,z)=(x+z,z). This is the normalization used here.

The same manuscript uses normalized characteristic classes κ(E)=ch(E)exp(−c1(E)/rank(E)) for deformation arguments. These are rational classes. The present fixed-surface integral calculation does not test all those deformed constructions, nor does a rational algebraicity conclusion by itself establish the needed integral class.

## Exact calculation

Let A have Néron–Severi group generated by a polarization θ of type(1,e). Its integral algebraic even Mukai lattice has basis 1,θ,pt, with θ²=2e·pt. Write

\[
 v=r+d\theta+s\,\mathrm{pt},\qquad
 w=r'+d'\theta+s'\,\mathrm{pt},
\]

with integral coefficients. All such basis classes are represented by virtual algebraic K-classes: θ=ch(L)−1−e·pt. Todd classes are trivial on abelian varieties.

With x the first A-coordinate, z the integrated A-coordinate and y the dual coordinate, Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch gives

\[
 \operatorname{ch}\Phi(v\boxtimes w)
   =\int_{z\in A}v(x+z)\,w(z)\,\exp(-P(z,y)).
\]

In dimension two the inverse Fourier transform has this sign; replacing y by −y only reverses the sign of P and leaves the parity conclusion unchanged.

Put D=pr_A^*θ and Dhat=pr_Adual^*θdual. We resolve degree-four classes in the linearly independent rational basis

\[
 D^2/2,\quad DP,\quad DDhat,\quad P^2/2,\quad PDhat,\quad Dhat^2/2.
\]

The symbolic exterior-algebra calculation gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{rank}\Phi(v\boxtimes w)
   &=rs'+sr'+2edd',\\
 b:=\operatorname{coeff}_{P}(c_1\Phi(v\boxtimes w))
   &=sr'+edd',\\
 q:=\operatorname{coeff}_{P^2/2}
        (\operatorname{ch}_2\Phi(v\boxtimes w))
   &=sr'.
 \end{aligned}
\]

These identities follow by bilinearity from the nine basis calculations recorded in the output file. Since

\[
 c_2(E)=c_1(E)^2/2-\operatorname{ch}_2(E),
\]

the coefficient of P²/2 in c2 is b²−q. For even e we have q≡b modulo2, so b²−q is even.

This congruence persists under integral sums and differences of the K-classes, since both b and q are additive. It also persists under tensor products: if E and F have ranks r_E,r_F, then

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 b(EF)&=r_Eb(F)+r_Fb(E),\\
 q(EF)&=r_Eq(F)+r_Fq(E)+2b(E)b(F).
 \end{aligned}
\]

The other products of degree-two basis vectors do not contribute to the P²/2 coordinate. Thus q≡b modulo2 remains true throughout this ring of constructed classes. Products of two integral divisors likewise have even P²/2 coefficient.

Consequently the integral span of these particular second Chern classes and divisor products cannot contain a class with odd P²/2 coefficient, and in particular cannot produce P²/2 itself.

## What has not been shown

There is no claim that the full algebraic K-group of A×Adual is generated integrally by the transformed exterior products. A derived-category generation statement with idempotent completion would not establish the required generation of K-classes without further argument. Nor is there a claim about arbitrary surfaces, arbitrary sheaves on the product, cycles obtained after varying the surface, or all characteristic classes arising from Markman's deformation methods.

The exact residual question therefore remains the integral numerical algebraicity of P²/2 from C.4r. This calculation explains why this fixed-A method does not settle it.

## Reproduction

- Script: research/agents/frobenius/code/orlov_surface_parity.py.
- Complete symbolic output: research/agents/frobenius/code/orlov_surface_parity.out.
- The script implements the exterior product directly, integrates the four z generators, solves for the displayed basis coordinates, and checks the polynomial parity after substituting e=2d.
- No floating-point arithmetic or sampled value of e is used.

The full table, rather than only the parity assertions, is retained in the output for independent inspection.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_REVIEW.md

# Independent C.1f, C.4, and E.3e review

Agent: /root/frobenius  
Date: 2026-11-19  
Parent/master files were not edited.

## 1. C.1f incidence blow-up induction

**Independent check passed.** We checked the complete induction in
../cycles/cycles.tex, including the explicit blow-up projections added
to the current version.

If \(X_{2i-1}\) is a smooth Cartier divisor in a smooth Tate-motive
\(R_{2i}\), then \(X_{2i-1}\times\{0\}\) is the regular zero scheme of the
two-component section of
\(\operatorname{pr}_1^*O(X_{2i-1})\oplus\operatorname{pr}_2^*O(1)\) on
\(R_{2i}\times P^1\). The incidence equation in the projective bundle of
lines is exactly the Rees-algebra presentation of its blow-up. Thus the
new blow-up is a smooth Cartier divisor in a Tate-motive ambient variety
of one higher dimension. There is no unsupported small-codimension
embedding claim.

For the codimension-two centre, the blow-up formula and its displayed
forward/inverse maps are correctly typed. The exceptional self-intersection
has the required minus sign; its pushforward is the identity on the
centre. These algebraic correspondences preserve algebraic equivalence.
The unique Griffiths summand therefore moves from degree \(i\) to
\(i+1\), while the ambient part stays finite dimensional. Multiplication
by projective space gives the claimed string of Griffiths groups in
degrees \(i+1,\ldots,n-i\). The source in degree \(i\) remains finite
dimensional and the target in degree \(n-i\) infinite dimensional.
This validates all \(1\le i<n/2\).

## 2. New C.4 deliverables and proved results

C4_additions.tex contains C.4h–l and an unnumbered remaining-work paragraph.
C4_references.bib adds only the two
new keys; the other keys already occur in the cycles bibliography.

1. Scalar factorizations through finite products of Jacobians form
   an ideal \(I_J(A)=s_J(A)\mathbb Z\). Its generator is one exactly when
   \(A\) is an actual direct summand. Duality preserves it and products
   take the least common multiple.
2. In every characteristic, \(s_J(A)\mid(2g-1)!\). This follows from the
   explicit integer composite in Scavia–Suzuki Proposition 3.4,
   applied to the Poincaré line bundle on \(A\times\widehat A\).
   No unproved integral Hodge conjecture is used.
3. For \(g\le3\), \(s_J(A)\) also divides
   \(\delta(A)=\gcd_{L\in\mathrm{Pic}(A)}\chi(L)\). Mumford's group-scheme
   principalization has degree \(\chi(L)\), and Oort–Ueno identifies the
   principally polarized quotient with a product of Jacobians.
4. Consequently
   \(s_J(A)\mid\gcd((2g-1)!,\delta(A))\).
   Different polarizations may be used at different primes. A finite
   Bézout combination of the resulting maps gives one exact retraction.
5. Surface case: the sufficient local checks are at 2 and 3. Threefold
   case: 2, 3, and 5. The check at the characteristic is retained for
   characteristic 2 or 3. We use group-scheme kernels throughout.
6. A surface with NS rank one and primitive type \((1,d)\) is settled
   whenever \(\gcd(d,6)=1\). For a complex threefold with primitive type
   \((1,d_2,d_3)\), the sufficient test is
   \(\gcd(d_2d_3,30)=1\).
7. For NS-rank-one complex surfaces with End equal to \(\mathbb Z\),
   the identity coefficients obtainable from products of divisors
   on \(A\times\widehat A\) form exactly \(\gcd(6,d)\mathbb Z\).
   This is a limitation of that subring, not a counterexample for all
   algebraic curves.

The strongest isogeny formulation retains kernel exponents: if
\(A\to B\) has kernel killed by \(e\), then
\(eI_J(B)\subset I_J(A)\) and conversely. For a separable polarization
of type \((d_1,\ldots,d_g)\), one may use \(e=d_g\). Inseparable cases
use the actual kernel group scheme, or the always valid degree bound.

## 3. What remains unproved

### Priority and targeted-search record

No priority or novelty claim is made for the factorial bound, the
scalar-factorization ideal, or the polarization gcd criterion. These are
elementary quantitative deductions from the opened Matsusaka formula,
principalization, and the low-dimensional Jacobian theorem. Searches for
the exact direct-summand formulation and its nonprincipal surface and
threefold variants found Beckmann–de Gaay Fortman, Voisin, and
Scavia–Suzuki; their exact theorems are identified above. The search
neither established that these elementary deductions are new nor
established a published resolution of every residual small-prime case.
Accordingly we record them as proved deductions and reserve every
priority judgment.

We have not shown that every nonprincipally polarizable surface or
threefold has \(s_J=1\), nor produced one with \(s_J>1\). In particular,
the computations do not settle generic type \((1,2)\) or \((1,3)\)
surfaces.

The following proposed shortcuts were checked and do not close the gap:

- Integral Hodge for curves on \(A\) alone: the converse to the
  direct-summand condition in the opened theorems requires a unimodular
  line bundle. Without one, the criterion is on \(A\times\widehat A\).
- All rational Hodge classes are generated by divisors: rational
  generation does not imply integral generation; the explicit
  divisor-product lattice has the residual index above.
- Choosing many principalizations: if \(\ell\mid\delta(A)\), no
  prime-to-\(\ell\) isogeny from \(A\) to a principally polarized
  variety of the same dimension exists. Pulling back the principal
  polarization would contradict the definition of \(\delta(A)\).
  Thus merely varying these quotients cannot remove that prime.
- Zarhin's trick: the principalizable product has dimension \(8g\),
  so the dimension-three Jacobian theorem no longer applies.
- Engel–de Gaay Fortman–Schreieder 2026: the opened theorem concerns a
  very general ppav of dimension at least four. Its hypotheses do not
  automatically include the restricted family \(A\times\widehat A\).
  A separate degeneration argument is necessary.
- Rational Tate results for abelian varieties over finite fields do
  not themselves supply integral all-prime curve classes or eliminate
  the characteristic-prime condition in Scavia–Suzuki.

These statements distinguish a proof gap in this contribution from a
claim that the cases are known to be open in the literature. Further
non-divisorial degeneration or geometric constructions remain under
investigation at the parent's request.

## 4. Finite calculation

Run:

    python research/agents/frobenius/code/verify_c4_exterior.py

Output:

- output/verify_c4_exterior.json
- output/verify_c4_exterior.txt

The script computes in the exact exterior algebra over \(\mathbb Z[d]\).
It checks the factorial normalization for dimensions 1–4, verifies
\(D\widehat D P=-dP^3/6\), and computes the cross component of all ten
degree-three divisor monomials. Only the coefficients \(6\) and
\(-d\) are nonzero. It also checks three finite Bézout examples.
This is a certificate for the stated lattice calculation, not a
computation of the full Chow group.

## 5. E.3e independent review

**Independent check passed.** We checked
../cycles/effectivity_albanese.tex, E.3e, and independently opened
Bloch's original 1979 paper.

The repair from generator-wise torsion to specialization of the
entire torsion relation is valid. Spreading the Albanese map alongside
the parameter variety avoids assuming that formation of Albanese is
unchanged without verification. Integral \(H^1\) base change makes
\(\mathrm{Alb}(T_s)\to B_s\) a prime-to-\(\ell\) isogeny, so the
specialized degree-zero cycle has prime-to-\(\ell\) Albanese class.
Over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), its Chow class is torsion. Roitman's
prime-to-\(p\) theorem kills its \(\ell\)-primary component. The total
specialized relation remains \(\ell\)-primary, hence is zero. Bloch
Proposition 3.8 then kills the generic torsion Abel–Jacobi class.
Propositions 3.5 and 3.9 and Theorem 4.2 justify the final application
to an abelian parameter variety. Taking Tate modules causes no
additional torsion issue because the source is an abelian variety.

Only editorial suggestion sent: replace “choose finitely many
expressions” by “choose an expression” before \(z=\sum W_*\nu\).

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_prym_REVIEW.md

# Prym improvement and supplementary independent audits

## 1. Exact new result and limits

The new fragment `C4_prym_addition.tex` proves two numbered statements.

- **C.4m:** Every principally polarized abelian variety of positive
  dimension at most five over an algebraically closed field has
  multiplication by two factoring through a product of Jacobians.
  Characteristic two is included by polarized lifting and specialization
  of an actual integral curve cycle.
- **C.4n:** Every abelian surface satisfies
  `s_J(A) | gcd(2, delta(A))`. Thus an ample line bundle of odd Euler
  characteristic suffices for the exact direct-summand property.
  For Picard rank one and type `(1,d)`, every odd `d` is settled.

This improves the earlier surface bound six to two. In particular,
type `(1,3)` is positive despite the divisor-product index three
computed in C.4l. We have **not** proved the result for every surface
with even delta, and have not found a surface counterexample.
The general threefold bound remains `gcd(120, delta(A))` in this work.
Its possible residual primes are still 2, 3, and 5. We do not claim
that every unhandled case is known to be open in the literature.

## 2. Proof and exact coefficient checks

Beauville's generalized Prym includes a map into the generalized
Jacobian of a nodal curve. Composing with normalization gives a map
`j : B -> product J(C_i)` into an actual product of Jacobians of
smooth curves. Proposition 2.2 identifies the generalized theta as
the pullback of the product theta. Theorem 5.4 gives
`j^* theta_J = 2 Xi` in the Néron–Severi group. Since both
polarizations are principal, the polarized adjoint is an integral
homomorphism and `j^dagger j = [2]` exactly. No finite kernel is
inverted, and no principal polarization on a nonprincipal variety
is silently assumed.

The sum of the images of the normalization curves under `j^dagger`
has cohomology class twice the minimal class. This follows directly
from the associated alternating pairing, or equivalently from
`q lambda_J^-1 qhat = 2 lambda_B^-1`. The proof in the fragment
explains why that pairing determines the full curve cohomology class.

At characteristic two, Oort's valid lifting implication and
Corollary 2.4.2 provide a polarized lift over W(k). A finite base
extension defines the generic integral cycle. Its closures give
an integral special cycle. Fulton's Example 20.3.5 expressly supplies
the commutative diagram with the ell-adic cycle class map for
ell different from two. Numerical equality follows, and Matsusaka
then supplies actual homomorphisms whose composite is `[2]` over k.
No crystalline equivalence or rationally defined retraction is used.

For any fourfold or fivefold X, principalizing ample L and using
the preceding theorem gives `2 chi(L) in I_J(X)`. The ample gcd
lemma gives `s_J(X) | 2 delta(X)`. For the fourfold `A x Adual`,
the Poincaré bundle has Euler characteristic one, hence delta one.
Restriction and projection yield `[2]` on A. The earlier
same-dimensional principalization supplies `[delta(A)]`, and
Bézout gives the final odd-delta criterion.

The fragment includes a finite five-polarization interpolation
formula realizing the gcd construction. It also gives a concrete
alternative: `P tensor H^(72t)` has Euler characteristic congruent
to one modulo three, so a single such principalization and the
existing factorization of six yield a factorization of two.

## 3. Independent review

The cycles agent independently opened Beauville and Oort and checked
both the normalization map and the characteristic-two specialization
argument. Its conclusion was that the proof is sound, including the
finite-kernel issue and use of rational ell-adic equality only to
deduce numerical equality of an already integral cycle.

The script `code/verify_c4_prym_arithmetic.py` checks exact integers
in the finite interpolation formula on a polynomial basis and the
coefficient congruences for the one-polarization construction. This
is an arithmetic certificate only; the geometric existence theorem
comes from the proved construction and the cited primary results.

## 4. Integration instructions and correction to an earlier locator

Insert `C4_prym_addition.tex` after C.4l and before the final
remaining-work paragraph, or put it immediately after the previous
supplement and retain only the new remaining-work paragraph.
The old remaining-work sentence listing types `(1,2)` and `(1,3)`
as unresolved is superseded. A correct replacement is:

> After the Prym improvement in C.4m–n, the untreated surface cases
> in this contribution have even delta, for example Picard-rank-one
> type (1,2). Type (1,3), and all odd types (1,d), are settled
> positively. The analogous general threefold cases at 2, 3, and 5
> remain untreated here.

**Oort–Ueno page correction:** The CWI PDF is the original six-page
preprint with one cover page. Its Theorem 4 is printed on page 2
(PDF page 3), as shown in `output/oort-title-3.png`. It is not the
published pagination 377–381. Our earlier p.381 locator was wrong;
the theorem number and mathematical statement were correct.
`C4_additions.tex` and its local report ledger now use the exact
preprint locator. The parent and cycles agent were notified to
correct their integrated references as well.

The rendered preprint page 3 (`output/oort-4.png`) also expressly
corrects Oort 1971 Theorem 2.4.1: the words "and only if" must be
deleted. Our proof uses only the valid implication from separability
to formal smoothness and the stated lifting corollary. This is now
spelled out in the fragment and bibliography note.

## 5. Other independent audits requested by the parent

### A.1h descent across the finite quadratic field

The parent's determinant descent argument is correct under its
nonemptiness hypothesis: the isomorphism sets before and after
base change to the algebraic closure are torsors under the same
group `Z_p^*`. An equivariant map between nonempty torsors for this
same group is bijective. Naturality under the two field embeddings
then makes the descended determinant rigidification independent of
the choice of embedding. This avoids a finite-field geometric-model
claim. The two `F^2=+p` and `F^2=-p` strata cannot be linked by a
nonzero F-intertwiner: its square relation gives `2p b=0` in a
torsion-free lattice, including at p=2. Thus without compatibility
with geometric base change the strata can carry independent
constants; imposing that compatibility identifies the constants.

### C.2 pencil induction

The lexicographic induction proposed by the parent is sound with
global A-prime available over all algebraically closed extensions,
including the algebraic closure of k(t). The smooth pencil centre
has codimension two. For the exceptional contribution, the fibre
class restricts to `O_{P^1}(1)` on `E=B x P^1`, so the coefficient
of `i_B* beta` is positive one. Weak Lefschetz gives the required
preimage `b0` since `2i-2 < n-2`. The lower-codimension induction
and hard Lefschetz prove its algebraicity from the algebraic class
`L^(k+2) b0`. Finite trace with rational coefficients handles the
generic-fibre descent. No nodal Lefschetz-pencil hypothesis is needed.

### C.2 reduction to critical dimension

The parent's incidence construction is also correct. For
`d=2r+1-n >= 0`, the variety `T=X x P^d` has the critical dimension
`2r+1`. The incidence divisor defined by `sum s_j t_j=0` is smooth
when the common zero locus is a smooth complete intersection:
outside it a fibre derivative is nonzero; on it independence of
the differentials `ds_j` gives a nonzero derivative for every
projective coordinate vector. For d=0 this is just the initial
critical case. On `U=X\Y`, the section s0 trivializes the line
bundle and a triangular projective coordinate change sends the
incidence divisor to the standard hyperplane. Its Gysin image in
the Chow projective-bundle decomposition is exactly divisible by h.
Thus the constant coefficient restricts to zero in CH(U), and
localization puts it in the image of CH(Y). The cohomological
projective-bundle decomposition identifies its class with the
original z. This requires the given hyperplane Y to come from the
very ample embedding in the statement; no extra singular-pencil
assumption enters.

Mumford, *Prym Varieties I* (1974), §2 equations (2.1)–(2.3),

https://www.dam.brown.edu/people/mumford/alg_geom/papers/1974a--PrymVar-I-NC.pdf,

nonprincipal surfaces and direct summands of products of Jacobians
returned the Beckmann–de Gaay Fortman, Voisin, and Scavia–Suzuki
criteria and sources on ramified Prym surfaces, but did not yield

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_pt6_REVIEW.md

# Threefold improvement via Prym–Tyurin sixfolds

## Result

`C4_pt6_addition.tex` proves C.4o and C.4p. Every principally
polarized sixfold over any algebraically closed field has an integral
curve of class six times the minimal class, so `[6]` factors through
a product of Jacobians. It follows that every abelian threefold
satisfies

\[
 s_J(A)\mid\gcd(6,\delta(A)).
\]

Together with C.4n, the strongest current bounds are two for surfaces
and six for threefolds. The residual primes in this contribution
are respectively `{2}` and `{2,3}`. Thus rank-one surface type
`(1,3)` and threefold type `(1,1,5)` are affirmative. No general
resolution or counterexample is claimed for the residual cases.

## Primary theorem and field-transfer check

The published ADFIO2020 PDF contains **Theorem 1** and **Corollary 2**
on p.165. The latter explicitly states the effective curve assertion
for every complex principally polarized sixfold. The older preprint
numbers the main theorem 0.1, so that number must not be attached to
the published pagination. The exponent-six theta-restriction
identity is on p.164.

To pass from C to an arbitrary algebraically closed field of
characteristic zero, one must preserve more than an arbitrary
factorization of `[6]`: the curve class is needed for the next
specialization. We therefore spread the curves, their pointed maps,
and the exact identity

\[
\sum_a m_a f_a\lambda_{J(C_a)}^{-1}\widehat f_a\lambda_B=[6].
\]

These data spread to a nonempty finite-type scheme over the finitely
generated field of definition of `(B,Xi)`. A closed point has a
finite residue extension and can be embedded into the target
algebraically closed field. The same adjoint identity then holds.
The pairing calculation identifies the resulting curve class with
`6 gamma_Xi`; no argument based only on the existence of an
unpolarized retraction is used.

In characteristic p we use the polarized Witt lift already audited
in C.4m. Fulton's geometric specialization and compatibility with
cycle classes yield an actual integral special cycle. Rational
ell-adic equality for one ell different from p suffices for the
numerical equality used by Matsusaka. Neither p=2 nor p=3 is
excluded: the scalar six is never inverted.

For a sixfold X, principalizing every ample L gives
`6 chi(L) in I_J(X)`, hence `s_J(X)|6 delta(X)`. The Poincaré
bundle on `A x Adual` has Euler characteristic minus one, so delta
is one. Restriction to A and the earlier `delta(A)` factorization
complete the proof. The fragment gives a seven-polarization
integer interpolation formula, so the gcd construction is finite.

## Integration

Input the new fragment after `C4_prym_addition.tex`. Add
`C4_pt6_references.bib`, `C4_pt6_results.csv`, and
`C4_pt6_CHECK_EVENTS.tsv` to the corresponding master aggregations.

The final unnumbered scope paragraph in `C4_pt6_addition.tex`
supersedes the two older remaining-work paragraphs in
`C4_additions.tex` and `C4_prym_addition.tex`. For a clean narrative,
retain only the latest one. The earlier bounds 6/120 and their
tables are valid intermediate results, but they are no longer the
strongest bounds in the report.

| frC4EGFSOptimality2025 | https://arxiv.org/html/2512.04902v1; Theorem1.2, smallest multiple exactly6 for a very general complex ppav6 | turn998089042view1; turn453460396view2 |
| frC4Oort1971 | https://www.numdam.org/item/CM_1971__23_3_265_0.pdf; valid sufficient direction of Theorem2.4.1 p.286, Corollary2.4.2 and proof p.287 | turn988083530view0; turn337819996view3 |

to establish any theorem. The ADFIO DOI is printed on the opened
published PDF first page: `10.1515/crelle-2018-0005`.

The existing 2026 EGFS paper supplied the lead in Remark8.12, but
the upper-bound proof is based on the opened ADFIO primary article.
The companion EGFS2025 paper is used only to explain the scope of
generic sharpness. No very-general ppav theorem is applied to the
special family `A x Adual`.

## Why the requested matroidal obstruction does not directly apply

This is a proved limitation of that direct approach, not a claimed
obstruction to solving the residual cases by other degenerations.

Suppose a complex family `X=A x Adual` has unipotent monodromy around
a loop, and let N be its logarithm on `H1(A,Q)`. On the dual family
the logarithm is minus the transpose. Consequently

\[
 N_X=N\oplus(-N^{\mathsf t}),\qquad
 \operatorname{rank}N_X=2\operatorname{rank}N.
\]

Thus every nonzero such monodromy operator has even rank. Passing
to an isogenous abelian scheme conjugates rational monodromy;
finite ramified base change replaces N by a positive integer
multiple, which also preserves its rank.

In EGFS2026, Definition4.1(3), the monodromy form for each coordinate
divisor is a positive multiple of `y_s^2`, of rank one for a nonloop
of the matroid. Definition2.12 identifies that form with N through
the principal polarization, so its rank is the rank of N.
Hence a nontrivial isotypic family obtained in this way cannot
directly satisfy the stated matroidal-family condition. This
explains a concrete missing hypothesis in the proposed application
to `A x Adual`; an adaptation to rank-two monodromies would need
new work.

Opened locators: https://arxiv.org/html/2507.15704v3,
Definition2.12 (`turn596713770view0`), Definition4.1 and Remark4.2
(`turn950968762view1`), and Theorem1.6
(`turn199065391view3`). This rank calculation does not exclude a
different family or a different geometric obstruction, and does
not by itself imply that the residual cases are known open.

## Remaining search status

The principalization method has reached its precise local limit:
if ell divides delta(A), every same-dimensional principalization
has degree divisible by ell. The new generalized-Prym and
Prym–Tyurin constructions overcome some of those denominators
using `A x Adual`, but leave the primes recorded above. Searches
and checks in this contribution have not found an exact all-case
theorem or counterexample for nonprincipal surfaces or threefolds.
No novelty or priority claim is attached to the new deductions.

The same review confirmed polarized lifting and integral cycle specialization to every characteristic. `/root` independently checked both new proofs and preservation of integral coefficients.

The two superseded remaining-work paragraphs were removed from `C4_additions.tex` and `C4_prym_addition.tex`. Only the latest paragraph in `C4_pt6_addition.tex` is retained. The algebraic-equivalence macro in C.4m and the final table heading were corrected.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_residual_REVIEW.md

# Residual-prime research: exact lower-dimensional subvariety gate

## Status and deliverables

`C4_residual_gate.tex` proves C.4q. It does not resolve the remaining all-case surface or threefold direct-summand question. The all-characteristic upper bounds remain

\[
s_J(A)\mid\gcd(2,\delta(A))\quad(g=2),\qquad
s_J(A)\mid\gcd(6,\delta(A))\quad(g=3).
\]

The new result identifies the entire lattice generated by a natural further source of curves, beyond divisor products. For a simple complex A with End(A)=Z and primitive rank-one NS type (1,d2,...,e), curves supported in translates of proper abelian subvarieties of X=A×Â have Fourier images exactly

\[
\mathbb ZD\oplus\kappa(e)\mathbb ZP\oplus\mathbb Z\widehat D,
\qquad \kappa(e)=\prod_{\ell\mid e}\ell^{\lceil v_\ell(e)/2\rceil}.
\]

Thus that construction cannot produce the primitive Poincare curve class whenever e>1. For type (1,3) surfaces, for instance, this subgroup retains a factor three even though the generalized-Prym argument already proves actual splitting. This is a concrete distinction between a limitation of this subgroup and a true obstruction to all algebraic curves.

The proof is valid for both g=2 and g=3 over C. We do not claim the identical integral Fourier/divisor lattice assertion in positive characteristic, especially at the characteristic prime. This restriction does not weaken the earlier all-characteristic upper bounds.

## Independent verification

On 2026-11-19 `/root/cycles` independently read the fragment and confirmed all steps: dimensions of proper abelian subvarieties, the scalar determinant eac−b², the resulting valuations, positive semidefiniteness of the constructed divisor, integral descent to a quotient at the NS-class level, and realization of its inverse Fourier class by integral curves in dimension at most three. The reviewer specifically confirmed that no Pic0 rigidification is required for the NS-class descent. No gap was found.

## Exact primary-source audit

| Source | Opened URL and locator | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Beauville1983 | <https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/u/beauvill/pubs/fourier.pdf>, Proposition1 p.241; Proposition3(iii) pp.243–244 | `turn115473020view0`; primary author scan opened. Proposition3(iii) is isogeny-only, so our inclusion formula is proved directly from the Poincare identity and projection formula. |
| Grabowski2004 | <https://www.math.ens.psl.eu/~obenoist/textes/Grabowski.pdf>, Proposition3.1.8 pp.28–29, Corollary3.1.9 pp.29–30 | `turn597037374view0`; full scan downloaded. Primary pages rendered and visually inspected as `output/grabowski-36.png` and `output/grabowski-37.png`; OCR text also saved. Title pages confirm Craig Grabowski, Duke, 2004. |
| Totaro2021 | <https://www.math.ucla.edu/~totaro/papers/public_html/hodge.pdf>, Theorem7.1 and Proposition7.2 pp.17–19 of author version; Section1 p.2 | `turn429003636view0`, `turn353423871view0`, `turn425675127view0`. Every used statement read in the primary text. |
| Beckmann–deGaayFortman2023 | <https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/compositio-mathematica/article/integral-fourier-transforms-and-the-integral-hodge-conjecture-for-onecycles-on-abelian-varieties/AC12934E24BDAA5B2CD04372A3090416>, Lemma3.4 equation(6), introduction after Remark1.4 | `turn410590374view0`, `turn597037374view3`; exact Fourier sign and the distinction between general IHC3 and the product criterion checked. |

No result is inferred solely from search snippets. Totaro journal metadata and DOI are on the opened Cambridge publisher page; the theorem's page locators are explicitly those of the author version. Grabowski's thesis is an image scan, so the primary theorem was visually inspected rather than trusting OCR alone.

## Nearby theorems and their exact limitations

Grabowski's Corollary3.1.9 proves IHC for curves on every complex abelian threefold, including nonprincipally polarized ones. Proposition3.1.8 explains the mechanism: express integral divisor classes by differences of ample classes, principalize each ample class, and push the Fourier images of principal theta classes from the dual principalized varieties. The output is a curve class on the dual threefold. It is not a theorem about all curve classes on the sixfold A×Â.

Totaro's Theorem7.1 proves the integral Tate theorem for abelian threefolds over the separable closure of a finitely generated field. Its Section1 formulation uses primes invertible in that field; no crystalline-at-p assertion follows merely from that theorem. Proposition7.2 is the Fourier-and-principalization analogue of Grabowski's reduction. It likewise does not address the needed class on A×Â.

The new proof uses the full strength of the dimension-three theorem for the proper abelian subvarieties of X. Even after allowing every integral curve class on all those subvarieties, the off-diagonal coefficient remains divisible by kappa(e). Thus the missing curves would have to include at least one component whose smallest containing translated abelian subvariety is all of X. This conclusion follows from the proved lattice equality and does not claim that such generating curves cannot work.

## Other independent approaches considered

1. **Same-dimensional principalization.** The earlier delta criterion is exact at each prime for this route; if a prime divides every Euler characteristic, changing the ample polarization does not give a prime-to-that-prime principalization. No additional improvement emerged.
2. **Curves on proper subvarieties or graph subvarieties.** This now has the exact C.4q answer above, stronger than a check of individual graph examples. It does not settle generating curves on X.
3. **Very-general ppav parity or sixfold sharpness.** The very-general results do not apply to the special isotypic family A×Â. The earlier rank-of-monodromy calculation also explains why the published rank-one matroid-family hypothesis cannot be applied directly to that family.
4. **Prym or Prym–Tyurin constructions of another exponent.** The exponent-two result in dimensions four/five and exponent-six result in dimension six give the proved upper bounds. We found no primary theorem furnishing a coprime exponent uniformly on the required special products. No claim that none exists is made.
5. **K-theory/Chern-class route.** The fact that the Poincare line bundle is algebraic does not make its divided Chern-character components integral algebraic cycles. The integral Fourier problem is precisely sensitive to these denominators. No additional divisibility removal was proved by this route.

No novelty, priority, or literature-wide open-status claim is made. The residual cases remain unproved in this contribution, with the exact theorem and method gates recorded rather than replaced by an assertion of completion.

## LaTeX verification

A wrapper containing all four C4 additions compiled successfully with LuaLaTeX and BibTeX, then two further LuaLaTeX passes. The final log has no undefined command or citation. The initial undeclared `mathscr` command was replaced with `mathcal`, avoiding an additional package dependency. No overfull box occurs in C.4q; the five previously reported small boxes from the earlier minimal wrapper remain. The final 12-page PDF and all compilation logs are under `output/C4_residual_check.*` and `output/C4_residual_compile_*.txt`.

A further primary recheck of Voisin2024, Theorem1.4 p.2470, Proposition2.1 and Corollary2.5 pp.2474–2475 (`turn123682617view0`, `turn624805281view2`), confirms the precise role of the product with the dual. Her Definition1.9 distinguishes algebraicity of the first Kunneth projector (called splitness there) from being an actual direct summand of products of Jacobians; those notions are not silently identified in our argument.

### Record: agents/frobenius/D4_CM_source_status_REVIEW.md

# D.4g: exact CM Hodge input and the boundary of our independent audit

## Finding

The current D.4g explicitly assumes **Theorem I of Pol van Hoften and Jack Sempliner, _The Hodge conjecture for CM abelian varieties_, Hexagon preprint 2610.00259v2**. It does not cite Grabowski. A search of the current arithmetic fragments, bibliography, root sections, and notes found no occurrence of that name. This corrects the attribution of the input without making any claim about other papers.

Theorem I, author-PDF p. 1, asserts the rational Hodge conjecture in every codimension for every complex abelian variety admitting complex multiplication. The definition immediately preceding it is surjectivity of the rational algebraic-cycle map onto rational Hodge classes. The source is a 25-page preprint. Its Hexagon history records the initial version on 15 October 2026 and version 2 on 18 October 2026; the record identifies no DOI or journal publication. Van Hoften's own page lists it under expository work. We have no source establishing completed peer review. The appropriate wording is therefore a theorem **claimed in this explicitly identified preprint and taken as an input**, with the downstream statement conditional on its validity.

## Exact dependency chain used in D.4g

1. The preprint's Theorem I supplies the hypothesis of Milne (2002), Theorem 3.3, printed pp. 607–608, in the published-layout arXiv v3. That theorem gives numerical equals prime-to-characteristic etale homological equivalence on all abelian varieties over an algebraically closed field, and the Hodge standard conjecture for their etale cohomology.
2. Milne (2002), Corollary 3.4, printed p. 608, supplies the unique geometric polarization of the corresponding category of abelian motives. Theorem 2.1 supplies the geometric positivity used earlier in that paper. These exact primary statements were freshly reopened.
3. D.4g then uses its explicitly assumed exact faithful specialization tensor functors and the common relative forms on degree-one generators. Restriction to summands, reflection of nondegeneracy, positivity of the numerical trace form, and uniqueness of the compatible positive classes prove the common-polarization statement. This tensor argument was independently checked in our earlier D4_refinements_independent_REVIEW.md.

The related finite-field Tate consequence uses Milne (1999), Theorem 7.1, author-PDF p. 25, and the interpretation of (0.1) on author pp. 2–3. That source was also freshly reopened. The journal pagination of this article is 45–76, as confirmed in Milne (2002)'s reference list; the arithmetic bibliography currently says 47–81 and should be corrected by its owner. This is a bibliographic correction, not a change to the theorem used.

The 2026 preprint itself refers to a differently numbered Milne Theorem 4.6. We do not silently transfer that locator to the published-layout 2002 source: the exact locators used by D.4g are Theorem 3.3 and Corollary 3.4 in that version.

## What was and was not independently verified

The statement, version, hypotheses, and formal applicability of the cited 2026 theorem were checked. The arithmetic agent opened the full preprint and checked the elementary surface-to-cycle reduction and the structure of its proof chain. Our independent D.4g audit checked the published Milne implications and the new common-generator polarization argument. **We have not independently verified the full analytic heart of the 2026 proof or re-proved all imported analytic results.** Opening a complete PDF, or checking a dependency chain, is not such a verification.

The relevant new analytic claim is Theorem 4.0.2, author p. 12: four specified degree-one Hodge structures admit maps into the first cohomology of a Picard modular surface with a nonzero fourfold cup product. Its proof uses the theta-lifting description and spanning result in Theorem 3.1.11 and Proposition 4.1.8, which draw on Liu (2021) and Bergeron, Millson, and Moeglin (2016). The final period argument is in Section 4.2. In particular, Lemma 4.2.7, pp. 17–19, proves nonvanishing of a diagonal theta-period functional from its archimedean value at zero; Lemma 4.2.9, pp. 20–21, supplies that nonzero archimedean value using the doubling map, Kudla–Millson forms, and approximation; Lemma 4.2.10, pp. 21–22, gives the final finite character expansion. A complete independent audit would have to check those arguments and the exact hypotheses, normalizations, and convergence statements in their analytic sources.

Section 1.3, p. 4, explains the relationship with the prior OpenAI preprint [Ope26]. The authors describe their own argument as a variant, and explicitly do not claim a detailed verification of [Ope26]. Thus our cited input is their stated Theorem I and their written proof, not an independent certification of [Ope26]. Section 1.4 and the Hexagon provenance statement disclose extensive model assistance. That disclosure establishes provenance; it does not by itself establish a mathematical error. We found no specific flaw in this source-status audit and make no claim that the theorem is false.

## Recommended status language

Retain D.4g as **proved conditional on the CM Hodge theorem claimed by van Hoften–Sempliner (2026, Theorem I)**. Describe the independent work as verification of the cited theorem's precise scope, the established Milne implications, and the tensor argument. Do not label the 2026 analytic proof independently verified, peer reviewed, or unconditionally certified by this project.

## Primary sources freshly opened

Full author PDF, 25 pages: https://polvanhoften2.github.io/Pol%20van%20Hoften%20and%20Jack%20Sempliner%20-%20The%20Hodge%20conjecture%20for%20CM%20abelian%20varieties.pdf ; retrieval turn212171446view0. ### Record: agents/frobenius/D4_refinements_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent review of D.4f–g

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius. Date: 19 November 2026.

We independently read research/agents/real_weil/D4_refinements.tex and reopened Milne's primary 2002 article. The two proofs pass under their stated hypotheses, including the explicit requirement that specialization functors be exact, faithful morphisms of Tate categories.

## D.4f: effectivity and the Tate conjecture

The numerical composition–trace pairing between the two rational correspondence spaces is nondegenerate by the definition of numerical equivalence; the customary graded trace sign does not alter nondegeneracy. Rational dual bases therefore give the claimed injection after extension to \(\mathbf Q_\ell\). Pairing a purported linear relation with the realized dual basis recovers each coefficient, since realization preserves composition and trace. Mere faithfulness of an arbitrary rational-linear representation would not by itself prove this scalar-extension assertion; the supplied pairing argument does.

Frobenius is central because it commutes with all correspondences after realization and that realization is faithful. The centre of a finite-dimensional semisimple rational algebra is a product of number fields, so each central element has square-free minimal polynomial. The rational Chinese-remainder projector therefore realizes exactly the Frobenius-fixed subspace. If this projector image is effective, its weight zero forces it to be a summand of degree-zero motives: all other degrees have disjoint Frobenius absolute values, and faithfulness removes the cross maps. Thus it is Artin. Frobenius equal to the identity then makes it a direct sum of unit motives, producing all the fixed classes by algebraic cycles.

In the converse, Tate surjectivity on products gives all Frobenius-equivariant realization maps, while the preceding pairing gives injectivity. A common Frobenius constituent yields a nonzero realization map from a simple motive to the effective Honda–Tate realization constructed in Part B. Fullness makes the rational morphism space nonzero, and semisimplicity splits the simple object off that effective motive. The weight-zero and negative-weight qualifications are necessary and are retained in the referenced D.4b. The all-varieties assertion over the fixed finite field is exactly what is used; no automatic extension from the abelian-type case is asserted.

## D.4g: common polarization from relative generators

Milne (2002), the definition preceding Lemma 1.2 and Lemma 1.2 with its proof, printed pp. 602–603, explicitly describes compatibility classes and restrictions of positive forms to direct summands. Therefore a fixed generator form extends by sums, tensors, duals, and summand restriction to a form on every object of the stipulated generated category. Its specialization is positive and nondegenerate. Exact faithfulness reflects invertibility of the associated map to the dual, and its trace form is positive on the rational endomorphism subspace. This proves the pulled-back form is a Weil form.

The same construction uses exactly the same relative generator forms at every specialization. Each object therefore has a common Weil form lying in every pulled-back positive class. Since a polarization consists of these compatibility classes, all the pullbacks coincide. No independent choice of an involution, fibre identification, or positivity class is smuggled into the generator argument.

Milne's Theorem 3.3 and Corollary 3.4, printed pp. 607–608, supply numerical–homological equivalence and geometric polarization under the CM Hodge hypothesis. The stated 2026 theorem is an explicitly cited input owned by the arithmetic agent; this audit does not independently verify its entire proof. The specialization argument using smooth proper local systems is valid for the specified extending cycles and projectors. Between any two points, an auxiliary prime distinct from both residue characteristics gives a connected open on which vanishing is locally constant. Existence and typing of the category and specialization functors remain hypotheses, as the fragment states.

## Opened primary source

Milne, J. S. (2002). Polarizations and Grothendieck's standard conjectures. *Annals of Mathematics, 155*(2), 599–610. Opened https://www.jmilne.org/math/articles/2002aP.pdf and compared the local published-layout arXiv-v3 text. Checked Remark 1.1, the definition before Lemma 1.2, Lemma 1.2 and its proof, Proposition 1.4, Theorem 2.1, Theorem 3.3, and Corollary 3.4. Printed-page locators above refer to the published-layout text.

### Record: agents/frobenius/E3_Saito_corrected_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of the corrected Saito reductions

## Outcome and exact distinction

The current `research/agents/cycles/saito_filtration.tex`, statements E.3o–q, passes this independent audit. This is a positive audit of the corrected statements. It does not reinstate the withdrawn general closure recorded in `E3_Saito_correction_REVIEW.md`.

We directly viewed the primary images of Saito's printed pp.321,323,325, in addition to the decisive definition on p.296. The two quotients must remain distinct:

\[
\operatorname{Gr}^{1}\mathrm{CH}^{i}(X)
 =\mathcal A^i(X)/(\mathcal A\cap\mathcal H^{*2})^i(X),
\qquad
{}'\operatorname{Gr}^{1}\mathrm{CH}^{i}(X)
 =\mathcal A^i(X)/(\mathcal A*\mathcal H)^i(X).
\]

The first denominator is the corrected reading of equation(1.8.2). The second quotient is explicitly introduced in Lemma6.2.1 on printed p.321. Neither is defined as the quotient by the zero-cycle-generated relation N in general.

## E.3o: exact integral Walker kernel

The source supports the finite-kernel assertion used in the draft. On printed p.323, Saito gives the surjective factorization from the primed quotient to the unprimed quotient and then to the algebraic intermediate Jacobian. He constructs a surjection from an abelian variety to the primed quotient whose composite with the Jacobian is an isogeny. Consequently every kernel in that factorization is finite. Remark6.4 on p.325 visibly records the inclusions

\[
\mathcal A*\mathcal H\subset\mathcal A\cap\mathcal H^{*2}
 \subset\ker(\psi_{\mathrm{AJ}}|_{\mathcal A})
\]

and equality up to finite groups, with exact equality under the torsion-injectivity hypothesis of Theorem6.2(iii).

The draft's integral strengthening is correct. The product relation A*H is divisible because one divides its algebraically trivial factor. Its Walker image lies in the finite kernel of the Walker isogeny and must therefore vanish. The quotient kerWalker/(A*H) is both finite by the preceding source statement and divisible by the already checked ACMV divisibility theorem for the Walker kernel. It is zero. This proves kerWalker=A*H, with no replacement of the intersection denominator by N.

On zero-cycles the torsion-injectivity hypothesis holds, and the p.323 proof explicitly treats p=dimV. The inclusions A*H⊂H^{*2}⊂kerAJ then identify H^{*2} on zero-cycles with the Albanese kernel. The second inclusion is given by Example1.11, pp.297–298; the integral relation is the H=H_Z defined in Example1.8. Taking all correspondence images is exactly N=<H^{*2}>_0. Thus the remaining general gate is the equality of this generated relation with A*H, which is not proved here.

The optional replacement of the second H factor of A*H by rational-homologically-trivial cycles is also valid: multiply that factor by an integer killing its integral cohomology class and divide the A factor by the same integer. It does not justify changing the definition of H^{*2} itself.

## E.3p: integral surface generators

The complete-intersection argument is sound integrally. A sufficiently positive smooth connected surface can be chosen through every point in the finite support of an integral zero-cycle on a smooth projective complex variety of dimension at least three. Successive weak Lefschetz isomorphisms on integral H1 give an actual Albanese isomorphism. The same formal zero-cycle on the surface therefore remains Albanese-zero.

The pullback of the correspondence is the refined pullback along the smooth regular immersion. Compatibility with proper pushforward gives the equality of cycle actions without requiring the chosen correspondence representative to meet the surface properly. No denominator or degree factor is introduced. Dimension one contributes no Albanese kernel; dimension zero contributes no connected degree-zero zero-cycle. Saito's Lemma7.1.1, pp.326–327, is consistent with this direct argument.

## E.3q: codimension two under surface support

We also audited the appended codimension-two statement. Transposing the support decomposition gives two well-typed routes:

- CH2(X)→CH2(S)→CH2(X), with dimS≤2;
- CH2(X)→CH1(D)→CH2(X), with dimD=dimX−1.

The first route takes an Abel–Jacobi-zero algebraically trivial cycle into a surface Albanese kernel, hence into N after pushing forward. The second route takes it to an Abel–Jacobi-zero divisor, which is zero. The resulting uniform integer M kills kerWalker/N. This quotient is divisible, so the bounded-exponent quotient is zero. This is an integral argument, even though the diagonal construction initially requires clearing one integer.

For codimension two, Corollary6.3 and Remark6.4 use the exact torsion theorem, so kerAJ=A*H=kerWalker. The Walker isogeny therefore has zero kernel. No corresponding assertion in arbitrary codimension is inferred.

## Primary-page record

Primary PDF: https://www.numdam.org/item/CM_1992__84_3_289_0.pdf , independently opened in this session.

Visually inspected local source pages:

- `research/agents/cycles/output/Saito1992_pdf9_printed296.png`;
- `research/agents/cycles/output/Saito1992_pdf34_printed321.png`;
- `research/agents/cycles/output/Saito1992_pdf36_printed323.png`;
- `research/agents/cycles/output/Saito1992_pdf38_printed325.png`.

We used OCR only to locate the additional passages; the decisive quotient and finite-kernel statements were checked on the primary page images. This audit does not erase the earlier source-reading error: it records the corrected, narrower conclusions.

### Record: agents/frobenius/E3_Saito_correction_REVIEW.md

# Saito 1992: correction of a failed closure inference

## Status

**The proposed closure of the general integral E.3 converse is withdrawn.** No result row or theorem fragment asserting that closure was written. The initial message to the other agents misread the denominator in equation (1.8.2). The corrected reading leaves the same general subgroup equality unproved.

## Source and visual check

H. Saito, *Generalization of Abel's theorem and some finiteness property of zero-cycles on surfaces*, Compositio Mathematica **84** (1992), 289–332.

Primary PDF, opened: https://www.numdam.org/item/CM_1992__84_3_289_0.pdf

The decisive page is printed p. 296, Example 1.8, equation (1.8.2). We independently viewed the local primary-page image supplied by the cycles agent, then cropped its lower portion without altering the source:

- Source PDF: `research/agents/cycles/sources/Saito1992.pdf`.
- Source page image: `research/agents/cycles/output/Saito1992_pdf9_printed296.png`.
- Independently viewed crop: `research/agents/frobenius/output/Saito1992_p296_bottom_crop.png`.

The equation reads

\[
\operatorname{Gr}^{l}\operatorname{CH}(V)
=\frac{\langle H^{*l}\rangle_0\operatorname{CH}(V)}
 {\bigl(\langle H^{*l}\rangle_0\cap H^{*(l+1)}\bigr)\operatorname{CH}(V)}.
\]

The intersection sign and the absence of the zero-cycle-generation brackets around the second term are visible in the primary page. The incorrect reading replaced this denominator by the subgroup generated from the next step, which is a different expression.

## Mathematical consequence

Example 1.9 identifies the first zero-cycle-generated step with algebraic equivalence. Consequently, the degree-one quotient in Theorem 6.2 is

\[
\operatorname{Gr}^{1}\operatorname{CH}^{p}(V)
=A^{p}(V)/(A\cap H^{*2})\operatorname{CH}^{p}(V),
\]

not the quotient by the subgroup generated under correspondences by Albanese-zero zero-cycles. Corollary 6.3 and the zero-cycle case can identify the relevant relation on zero-cycles, but that does not justify replacing the displayed intersection by its zero-cycle-generated subrelation in every codimension.

In the notation of the E.3 investigation, identifying the latter generated relation with N therefore does **not** imply

\[
N=(A\cap H^{*2})\operatorname{CH}^{p}(V).
\]

That equality is precisely an additional missing argument. Theorem 6.2, Corollary 6.3, and Remark 6.4 cannot be cited as proving it from the displayed definitions alone. We make no general identification of P with the Walker Jacobian on this basis.

## Error detection and reproducibility

The root and cycles agents independently noticed that the proposed conclusion would also imply a general universal regular algebraic representative, conflicting with the still-conjectural status discussed in modern Walker literature. This was an effective consistency check, but the decisive correction is the directly inspected source formula above. We explicitly distinguish a source-reading error from a counterexample to the desired mathematical equality: the equality remains unproved here.

The earlier E.3m–n reductions remain unchanged. Their role is conditional or structural; this source does not close their remaining general hypothesis.

### Record: agents/frobenius/E3_explicit_walker_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of the specified generic Walker counterexample

## Outcome

The proof of E.3r in research/agents/cycles/explicit_walker_counterexample.tex passes this independent proof and primary-source audit. It specifies the generic fourfold and the exact Legendre point of order two, and gives a mathematically exact selection rule for the auxiliary integral cycle. It does not give polynomial equations for that auxiliary cycle. The construction is a function-field example over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, subsequently embedded in the complex numbers.

The only suggested clarification concerns the quantifier over embeddings. If the statement intends one selected cycle gamma to work for every embedding K into C, the proof should explicitly add the intrinsic mod-two étale-cohomology argument below. This is a valid strengthening of the written proof, not an obstruction to its conclusion for a fixed embedding.

## 1. Geometry of the family and integral parity transfer

There are exactly 72 independent coefficients: two rows, three columns, two monomials in the first projective-line coordinates, and six quadratic monomials in the appropriate projective-plane coordinates. The first-row zero scheme has degree 12 because the degree of (h+2l)^3 on P1 times P2 is 12. On an open set where the threefold is smooth and this scheme is finite étale, its product with the second projective plane is an effective Cartier divisor. One may choose this open to contain an Ottem–Suzuki rational matrix having their parity obstruction.

The relative Hilbert argument preserves integral coefficients. A curve over the complex realization of the generic coefficient tuple gives a point of a finite-type relative Hilbert scheme whose image in the coefficient space, considered as a Qbar-scheme, is the generic point. An irreducible Hilbert component containing that point therefore dominates the coefficient open. Its proper image is closed, so it has a point over the chosen Ottem–Suzuki matrix. The special subscheme has a one-cycle with integral multiplicities; no irreducibility of that special subscheme is required. Degrees against the two specified line bundles are constant in the flat Hilbert family, by the constancy of Euler characteristics. Embedded and zero-dimensional components do not contribute to these degrees. Thus the parity congruence transfers with coefficient one. There is no finite-cover trace or extension-degree multiplier.

The current wording, “one-cycle with integral multiplicities,” correctly avoids confusing an integral-coefficient cycle with an irreducible curve.

## 2. The defect class, its minimal multiple, and descent

Parallel transport of the Ottem–Suzuki integral homology class along the connected smooth coefficient open preserves its two intersections, one and zero. Their torsion-freeness result applies to the integral cohomology. Their H2(O)=0 assertion, Hodge decomposition, and hard Lefschetz make every rational fourth-cohomology class algebraic and every integral fourth-cohomology class a Hodge class. Thus the algebraic subgroup B has finite index in the torsion-free lattice H4.

Let m be the least positive integer with m beta in B. The transferred parity condition forces m to be even. If m beta were in 2B, torsion-freeness of H4 would imply that (m/2) beta belongs to B, contrary to minimality. Therefore m beta tensor 1/2 is nonzero in B tensor Q/Z, while its image in H4 tensor Q/Z is zero. Any algebraic representative gamma consequently has gamma tensor 1/2 nonzero in CH2(W) tensor Q/Z.

The descent of gamma to the algebraically closed coefficient field k is valid. For each integral component in a complex representative, take a component of the Hilbert scheme of the fixed k-variety containing that geometric point and then a k-point on it. Over an algebraically closed field, the relevant reduced irreducible components are geometrically irreducible. Connecting points by curves in such a component gives algebraic equivalence of the corresponding integral cycles, hence preserves integral cohomology classes. No norm is needed and no integer is introduced.

For a single gamma to work under every embedding, use the following intrinsic formulation. Its nonzero class in CH2(W/k) tensor Q/Z has already been established over k. Its mod-two étale cycle class is zero over k, because smooth proper base change identifies that group with the mod-two comparison group under the embedding used to construct gamma. Hence its cycle class is even under every complex embedding. Schoen injectivity and the Deligne calculation then apply unchanged under each embedding. This supplies the optional uniform-embedding clarification.

## 3. Exact Schoen theorem and the selected point of order two

We directly opened the local primary PDF and viewed the theorem on printed p.177. Schoen's Theorem (0.2) assumes precisely: k and K algebraically closed, W smooth proper over k, E a genus-one curve over K with j-invariant transcendental over k. Its injectivity statement is for CH(W) tensor CH(E)tors as a whole. It does not remove the prime two and does not single out a special torsion direction.

For the specified Legendre equation, the discriminant is 16 t^2(1-t)^2 and its j-invariant is the nonconstant rational function 256(1-t+t^2)^3/[t^2(1-t)^2]. Transcendence of t over k gives the required transcendence of j. The difference (0,0)-infinity is a nonzero point of exact order two. Any such point is the image of 1/2 in a direct summand Q/Z of the elliptic torsion group (Q/Z)^2: extend that nonzero vector in the two-torsion subgroup to a basis of the divisible torsion group. Consequently gamma tensor tau is nonzero, and Schoen's theorem makes its exterior product nonzero of exact order two.

## 4. Deligne cohomology and Walker torsion injectivity

The Deligne sequence has divisible kernel J2(W). The tensor product of a divisible group with a torsion group is zero. Right exactness of tensoring therefore identifies the tensor product of H_D^4 with the elliptic torsion group with the corresponding tensor product of the integral Hodge lattice. Multiplicativity of the Deligne cycle class sends the exterior product through this latter tensor product. Its value is m beta tensor tau, which is zero because m is even. Thus the product is Abel–Jacobi trivial. This is exactly the mechanism in Suzuki's Proposition 3.1, author pp.5–6, now checked for the specified field and elliptic point.

Ottem–Suzuki Lemma 2.4 gives CH0(W_C)=Z. Moving each point of a fibre W times {e'} to the fixed point w0 shows that CH0(W times E) is supported on {w0} times E. Suzuki Theorem 1.1, author p.2, applies to a smooth projective variety whose CH0 is supported on a subvariety of dimension at most three and to codimension p in {3,dim(X)-1}. Both conditions hold for this fourfold in codimension three. Its Walker map is injective on algebraically trivial torsion. The nonzero two-torsion exterior product therefore has nonzero Walker image and is not in the zero-cycle-generated relation N.

The already audited family-converse theorem E.3j applies to this same fourfold, so the indicated example does refute the literal integral biconditional. It does not close or alter the separate general Walker-generation equality.

## Primary-source opening ledger

1. Ottem, J. C., and Suzuki, F. (2020). A pencil of Enriques surfaces with non-algebraic integral Hodge classes. Primary author PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1906.08994 . Opened in full through the web tool and read locally at research/agents/cycles/sources/OttemSuzuki2020.pdf. Checked Lemmas 2.3–2.7, author pp.4–6; Theorem 3.1, pp.6–8; parity equation (1), p.7; and the integral homology class, p.8. Retrieval refs: turn125996541view0 and turn663610137view0.

2. Suzuki, F. (2020). A remark on a 3-fold constructed by Colliot-Thélène and Voisin. Primary author PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1805.01067 . Opened in full through the web tool and read locally at research/agents/cycles/sources/Suzuki2020Remark.pdf. Checked Theorem 1.1, author p.2, and Proposition 3.1 with proof, pp.5–6. Retrieval refs: turn125996541view1 and turn663610137view1.

3. Schoen, C. (2000). On certain exterior product maps of Chow groups. Mathematical Research Letters, 7, 177–194. Primary publisher URL: https://intlpress.com/site/pub/files/_fulltext/journals/mrl/2000/0007/0002/MRL-2000-0007-0002-a004.pdf . The web-tool request returned HTTP 403 and is not counted as a successful web opening. The independently acquired local primary PDF research/agents/cycles/sources/Schoen2000Exterior.pdf was read and its printed p.177 was rendered and visually checked. Exact locator: Theorem (0.2), p.177. Own image: research/agents/frobenius/output/Schoen2000_p177_theorem_crop.png.

This is an independent audit; no shared source fragment was edited.

### Record: agents/frobenius/E3_family_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of E.3m–n

**Auditor:** GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius.  
**Date:** 19 November 2026.  
**Files checked:** agents/cycles/family_converse_reduction.tex and agents/cycles/family_countability.tex.  
**Outcome:** E.3m passes. E.3n passes after the moving-lemma notation was corrected; we also read the replacement Hilbert-scheme paragraph.

## E.3m: the exact Walker quotient

We checked the proof without assuming that its abstract quotient \(E\) carries a universal integral family. The original family on \(B\), its quotient by the connected kernel of the Walker map, and then its quotient by a finite subgroup are algebraic maps. Only the resulting injection \(E(\mathbb C)\to P\) initially remains an abstract homomorphism.

On enlarging the family from \(B\) to \(B\times A\), the map \(B\to E'\) remains algebraic. Its kernel contains the kernel of \(B\to E\) because both maps into \(P\) agree and \(E'(\mathbb C)\to P\) is injective. In characteristic zero these are reduced subgroup schemes, so the equality on complex points supplies the scheme-theoretic kernel containment needed for quotient descent. Hence \(E\to E'\) is algebraic. Its pointwise injectivity and equal dimensions make it an isomorphism. The enlargement argument therefore shows that a single image contains every abelian-family image.

The finite-family covering of the Walker Jacobian is also valid: each family has an abelian-subvariety image, finite sums remain abelian subvarieties, and strict enlargements increase dimension. Surjectivity of the Walker map together with the integral abelian parameterization of E.3g forces the terminal sum to be the whole Jacobian.

It follows that \(\ker(P\to J_W)\) is finite. It is a quotient of the divisible group \(\ker\psi_W\), so it is divisible and hence zero. We reopened Achter, Casalaina-Martin, and Vial (2022), Theorem 2.3, author manuscript p. 12, which is exactly the divisibility assertion required. No universality statement for arbitrary regular homomorphisms is substituted for this assertion.

The optional direct regularity argument also passes. If the integral map \(v:\operatorname{Alb}(T)(\mathbb C)\to E(\mathbb C)\) satisfies \(mv=f\) with \(f\) algebraic, then \(f\) kills the finite group scheme \(\operatorname{Alb}(T)[m]\). Thus \(f=g\circ[m]\) algebraically. The difference \(v-g\) has finite image and divisible source, so vanishes. Smooth projective compactification and closure of the cycle then handle quasi-projective parameter varieties; local constructions agree as morphisms because the target is separated.

## E.3n: countability with integral Albanese relations

### Correction identified and implemented

The original text asserted a map
\[
\mathrm{CH}_0(T^\circ)\longrightarrow\mathrm{CH}_0(T)
\]
for an open immersion. Such a pushforward is not defined in general. For example \(\mathrm{CH}_0(\mathbb A^1)=0\) while \(\mathrm{CH}_0(\mathbb P^1)=\mathbb Z\).

The needed statement is instead that every class in \(\mathrm{CH}_0(T)\) has a representative supported in \(T^\circ\), equivalently that \(Z_0(T^\circ)\to\mathrm{CH}_0(T)\) is surjective. The given curve-and-Riemann–Roch proof proves this integral representative statement. The cycles agent corrected the notation before this audit was closed.

### Parameter spaces

We checked that each operation uses finite discrete choices, indexed by countably many integers or Hilbert polynomials. Parameter varieties \(T\) can be embedded in projective spaces of varying dimensions; their smooth geometrically connected loci are obtained on finite-type strata. A marked point supplies a section.

The relative Albanese input was reopened in Javanpeykar and Mathur (2022), Theorem 2.2 and its following paragraph. It gives arbitrary-base-change compatibility for smooth proper characteristic-zero families, with the marked section turning the torsor into the pointed group scheme. Consequently the weighted Albanese-zero condition is an actual closed condition.

For correspondence cycles, the restriction to points of a common fibre-flat open is legitimate after the moving step. Vertical components act trivially because the zero-cycle can be represented away from their images. Relative Hilbert schemes parameterize the remaining flat families; fibre-flat loci are constructible, and finite stratification supplies cycle maps into fixed Chow schemes. These conditions retain integer multiplicities and do not replace the Albanese kernel by its saturation.

### Rational-equivalence witnesses

We read the revised argument using integral subschemes of \(\mathbb P^1\times X\), rather than a universal cycle over a Chow scheme. An integral subscheme dominating \(\mathbb P^1\) is flat over that regular curve. Its two fibre cycles differ by a rational equivalence. Conversely, closing the graph of a rational function produces precisely such a witness, with integral multiplicities. The Stacks Project, Lemma 43.9.1, Tag 0AZI, was opened and gives this exact equivalence, including the norm for generically finite projection to the cycle's support.

All finite signed sums of witnesses are parameterized by products of Hilbert schemes; endpoint equality is imposed in a fixed Chow scheme. Chevalley's theorem then gives countably many constructible image sets. We also reopened Schoen (1993), Lemma 4.4 and its proof, pp. 311–313, and checked its Hilbert–Chow witness construction. The additional relative Albanese-zero condition is supplied by this new proof, not attributed to Schoen.

### Uniform exponent and conclusion

The countably constructible sets for all positive exponents cover a nonempty open of the abelian variety if every family value is torsion. Baire gives a dense constructible member and thus a nonempty Zariski open \(V\) on which one fixed exponent kills the map. The kernel is a subgroup and \(V-V=A(\mathbb C)\), since any two translates of a nonempty open meet in an irreducible variety. Hence the same exponent kills the whole homomorphism. Divisibility of \(A(\mathbb C)\) then makes that homomorphism zero.

No uniform exponent is assumed in advance. The proof is integral and specific to the stated complex setting. Its final combination with E.3c and E.3m is an equivalence of remaining gates, not a proof that those gates always hold.

## Opened primary sources

- Achter, J. D., Casalaina-Martin, S., & Vial, C. (2022). *The Walker Abel–Jacobi map descends*. Theorem 2.3, author p. 12. https://www.math.uni-bielefeld.de/~vial/WalkerAJmap.pdf
- Javanpeykar, A., & Mathur, S. (2022). *Smooth hypersurfaces in abelian varieties over arithmetic rings*. Theorem 2.2 and following paragraph. https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2022.87
- Schoen, C. (1993). *On Hodge structures and non-representability of Chow groups*. Lemma 4.4, pp. 311–313. https://www.numdam.org/item/CM_1993__88_3_285_0.pdf
- The Stacks Project Authors. *Rational equivalence*, Lemma 43.9.1, Tag 0AZI. https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0AZI
