### Record: agents/frobenius/E3_morphic_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit: the full morphic Abel–Jacobi obstruction

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), inherited effort, /root/frobenius.

This is an independent source and proof review of the proposed cycles-agent counterexample. The complete typed E.3t and E.3u in research/agents/cycles/morphic_counterexample.tex have now been independently read and pass. This includes the exact Lawson criterion, the degree-fourteen sixfold construction, the integral singular-class vanishing, the fixed elliptic family, and the geometric-generic point witness. The smooth-dominant spread formulation correctly allows auxiliary transcendental coefficients.

## 1. Exact primary sources

**[OPENED]** Walker, M. E. (2007). The morphic Abel–Jacobi map. *Compositio Mathematica, 143*, 909–944. https://doi.org/10.1112/S0010437X07002278

Full published PDF:
https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/4A44146CE680F4F3C857048FDEDD9F48/S0010437X07002278a.pdf/the-morphic-abel-jacobi-map.pdf

Exact locators independently read: Example5.5 p.928; Theorem5.7(1)–(4) p.930 and proof p.931; Corollary5.9 and its preceding factorization p.932; Theorem6.1 and Theorem6.2 pp.933–934, including the full cap-product proof; Corollary6.3 p.934; Definition3.1 pp.915–916 and Proposition3.3 p.916.

**[OPENED]** Friedlander, E. M. (2000). Relative Chow correspondences and the Griffiths group. *Annales de l'Institut Fourier, 50*(4), 1073–1098. https://doi.org/10.5802/aif.1785

Published primary PDF: https://www.numdam.org/item/10.5802/aif.1785.pdf

Author primary PDF, also opened: https://dornsife.usc.edu/ericmfriedlander/wp-content/uploads/sites/233/2023/06/17.pdf

The needed published result is **Theorem3.4, p.1082**, with Definition3.1 and Theorem3.2 pp.1081–1082 and proof pp.1095–1097. Walker's citation to this paper as “4.5” does not match the published numbering: Proposition4.5 pp.1089–1090 concerns relative characteristic classes. The author copy likewise numbers the result Theorem3.4 (p.8; proof pp.18–20). We must cite the verified locator rather than reproduce the mismatch.

The effective Nagel sources and their exact numerical hypotheses were independently opened in the earlier Nori_independent_REVIEW.md. They are reused here only for the same universal relative-cohomology conclusion.

## 2. The full morphic map kills the generated relation

Example5.5 identifies the zero-cycle morphic Abel–Jacobi map on every smooth projective parameter with its Albanese map. Theorem5.7(4) gives naturality for arbitrary Chow correspondences of nonnegative dimension shift. A generator defining \(N^i(X)\) starts with an Albanese-zero zero-cycle on a smooth projective parameter and has target dimension \(\dim X-i\ge0\). Its full morphic image is therefore zero.

The theorem's printed notation reuses its index inconsistently in the displayed diagram, but both its statement and proof give the required correspondence shift. The specific zero-cycle-to-positive-dimensional-cycle application has no negative shift.

## 3. A single fixed cycle gives a nonzero elliptic family

Let \(Y\) be smooth projective of dimension \(2r+1\), and let \(\gamma\) be an \(r\)-cycle with zero rational singular class and
\[
s\gamma\ne0\quad\text{in }L_{r-1}H_{2r}(Y)_{\mathbb Q}.
\]
For an elliptic curve \(E\), put \(X=Y\times E\). The map
\[
\lambda_\gamma:H_1(E,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow
L_rH_{2r+1}(X,\mathbb Q),\qquad v\longmapsto\gamma\times v
\]
is injective. Indeed, for nonzero \(v\) choose \(w\) with nonzero intersection pairing. Capping with the pullback of \(w\) and pushing to \(Y\), exactly as in Walker's proof of Theorem6.2, gives
\[
\langle v,w\rangle\,s\gamma\ne0.
\]
The map is a morphism of inductive mixed Hodge structures; its source is pure of weight minus one. Strictness therefore gives an injection of its one-dimensional complex Hodge quotient into the target Hodge quotient.

By Definition3.1 the integral Lawson group is countable. Modulo its image, the induced homomorphism
\[
E(\mathbb C)\longrightarrow J_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(X)
\]
has countable kernel and countable preimage of the torsion subgroup: upstairs a nonzero complex linear injection can meet each integral period, or each rational multiple of such a period, at at most one point. Consequently uncountably many points give nontorsion images. This argument does not require the full morphic Jacobian to be a finite-dimensional complex torus.

Theorem5.7(4) identifies this map on points with
\[
e\longmapsto\Phi_r^{\mathrm{mor}}\bigl(\gamma\times([e]-[0])\bigr).
\]
The normalized correspondence is \(\gamma\times(\Delta_E-E\times\{0\})\), with factors ordered as parameter \(E\), then \(Y\), then target \(E\). Its total rational Betti class is zero, so its cylinder action vanishes. Yet its map to \(P^i(X)\) has nontorsion values, since the full morphic map kills \(N\).

The finite-dimensional Walker family map also vanishes: it is a homomorphism of abelian varieties whose rational \(H_1\)-map is the zero cylinder. Thus no multiplication to kill a finite Walker image is necessary for this explicit family.

## 4. Effective degree-fourteen choice

Use \(r=2\), \(Y\subset Q^6\) the degree-fourteen geometric generic hypersurface, and
\[
\gamma=(\Pi_+-\Pi_-)|_Y.
\]
The ambient primitive class is nonzero; its square has degree minus two. Its restriction has zero rational singular class by the already checked weak-Lefschetz calculation.

Friedlander's Theorem3.4 proof uses connectivity only for the characteristic-class degree
\[
u=2n-2r+2j
\]
and the final degree \(2n-2r\), and again in degree \(u\) after passing to the resolved relation space. With \(n=5,r=2,j=1\), these degrees are eight and six. Relative vanishing through degree nine is sufficient for all the required isomorphisms. The Nagel bound supplies vanishing through degree ten.

Numerically, Nagel's connectivity parameter is five, \(\mu=5\), the bundle-rank-minus-one is zero, and \(m_{Q^6}=1\). His conditions reduce to \(14\ge6\) and \(14\ge12\). The omitted parameter-bound hypothesis is also satisfied with equality.

A failure of \(s\gamma\ne0\) admits the finite algebraic witness supplied by Friedlander's Theorem3.2: a homologically trivial one-cycle on a possibly singular projective threefold and a Chow correspondence. Spread this witness over a smooth dominant parameter base above the hypersurface parameter space, retaining the homological vanishing after stratifying and shrinking. Resolve the witness and the relation space and shrink so their structural maps are smooth. Friedlander's proof then applies with the explicit Nagel connectivity and forces the ambient primitive class to vanish, a contradiction.

One must not automatically describe a witness initially given after an embedding into \(\mathbb C\) as defined over a finite extension of the geometric-generic field: its coefficients can have additional transcendence. The smooth-dominant-base formulation avoids this error; Nagel permits every smooth base. A subsequent multisection is another possible way to reduce to an étale base. The relation is finite algebraic data and its zero homology class is locally constant on a topologically trivial stratum, which is the reason the spreading step is legitimate.

## 5. Scope of the conclusion

For this construction \(X\) has dimension six, the fibres are two-cycles, and the codimension is four. The contradiction is to the universal complex family converse, integrally and rationally. The result does not assert a codimension-two counterexample or settle all lower-dimensional cases. It depends on the published Lawson-homology results with the explicit connectivity substitution, not on a conjectural identification of the full morphic object with the finite-dimensional Walker Jacobian.

## Final typed audit

The typed proof has the correct dimensions: Y has dimension five, X has dimension six, gamma has dimension two, and Z has codimension four on E times X. The homological cylinder H1(E) to H5(X) and its Poincare-dual map H1(E,Q(1)) to H7(X,Q(4)) agree with these conventions. Integral weak Lefschetz makes H4(Y,Z) isomorphic to H4(Q,Z)=Z, so equal degrees give integral, not merely rational, vanishing of gamma.

The generic-point argument for e is valid: a finite expression in N, including its Chow rational-equivalence witnesses and the relative Albanese-zero conditions, spreads on a smooth dominant base over an open of E. The image contains a nonempty complex open. The entire open would then have morphic image killed by one fixed nonzero integer, contrary to the countable torsion-preimage statement. Additional transcendental coefficients do not invalidate the argument because the base is permitted to have positive relative dimension.

For the Lawson witness, proper topological stratification (equivalently constructibility and base change for the relative homology complex) makes the zero class persist after shrinking. The model is chosen from the field generated by all finite witness coefficients, so its defining complex point is generic over the original coefficient field and is retained on these dense opens. The two resolved bases are smooth over the hypersurface parameter after generic smoothness. Thus the all-embedding formulation does not assume that an unspecified analytic exceptional locus is defined over the original countable field.

No finite-dimensionality of the full morphic target is used. The conclusion refutes the universal complex converse integrally and rationally in the stated codimension-four example.

### Record: agents/frobenius/E3_one_motivic_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of E.3s: the large one-motivic quotient

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), inherited effort, /root/frobenius.

Owner file: research/agents/cycles/one_motivic_quotient.tex.

**Verdict: passed after the nonproper-parameter wording correction, now applied by the owner.**

## Primary source

Ayoub, J., & Barbieri-Viale, L. (2009). 1-motivic sheaves and the Albanese functor. *Journal of Pure and Applied Algebra, 213*(5), 809–839. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jpaa.2008.10.002

**[OPENED]** Author version arXiv:math/0607738v2, dated 21 October 2008, https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0607738. The version-qualified PDF request failed; the unqualified PDF succeeded, and the abstract page confirms that v2 is current. The owner's local primary PDF and text were read independently.

Exact author locators: §1.3.1 and Lemma1.3.3 pp.16–17; Corollary1.3.5 p.18; Theorem1.3.10 pp.23–24; Proposition1.3.11 and Lemma1.3.12 pp.24–25; Remark1.3.15 p.25; Theorem3.1.4 p.49; Remark3.3.6 p.52.

The characteristic exponent inverted in §1.3.1 equals one over \(\mathbb C\); this is an integral argument.

## Proof checks

The sum of Yoneda maps indexed by actual Chow sections surjects onto the associated sheaf. Smooth quasi-projective parameters suffice because smooth affine opens cover the site. Thus no global cycle representative is assumed for an arbitrary sheafified section.

After quotienting by the images of the Serre–Albanese kernels, the result is a quotient of a coproduct of Serre–Albanese schemes. Cocompleteness and the Serre property place it in the one-motivic category. Lemma1.3.12 gives the universal property. This uses cokernels and epimorphisms, not left exactness of arbitrary colimits.

Evaluation at \(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C\) is exact because étale covers of this point split. It commutes with sums, and sheafification does not change the value at the point. The kernel is therefore exactly the finite sums of images of the free-zero-cycle kernels \(K_T(\mathbb C)\), without saturation.

A smooth projective compactification \(\overline T\) sends a Serre–Albanese-zero cycle on \(T\) to a degree-zero Albanese-zero cycle on \(\overline T\). Chow localization extends the actual cycle, and refined point pullback agrees on the open. Conversely every defining generator of \(N^i(X)\) uses a proper smooth parameter. Both inclusions are integral.

The original sentence claiming factorization through ordinary \(\operatorname{CH}_0(T)\) for arbitrary nonproper \(T\) was too broad: a constant family on \(\mathbb A^1\times X\) already disproves that assertion. The owner replaced it by the correct statement for the smooth projective compactifications. The proof only uses free zero-cycles before compactifying, so no mathematical step was lost.

The component identity follows from successive left-adjoint universal properties. Theorem3.1.4 identifies the component quotient with cycles modulo algebraic equivalence; exact evaluation gives the connected-point statement. The example \(B/\mathbb Zb\) for a nontorsion point of an elliptic curve is a valid connected one-motivic sheaf which need not be an abelian variety. No finite-dimensional representability or Walker comparison is implied by E.3s.

Remark3.3.6 only anticipates a comparison with the full morphic Abel–Jacobi construction. The distinction between that construction and the finite-dimensional Walker Jacobian must be retained in subsequent revisions.

### Record: agents/frobenius/E3_walker_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent review of the integral Walker obstruction

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius. Date: 19 November 2026.

## Scope and conclusion

We independently read research/agents/cycles/walker_obstruction.tex, particularly E.3j–k, and reopened the primary Ottem–Suzuki paper. The stated integral conclusion follows. No mathematical correction was identified in the reviewed argument.

## The diagonal decomposition

The complex support hypothesis on zero-cycles suffices. Choose a countable algebraically closed field of definition and embed its function field in the complex numbers. The support relation for the resulting geometric generic point involves finitely many cycles and rational functions. Spreading those data over the algebraic closure of the function field and choosing a closed point in a good open gives the relation over that algebraic closure. Finite descent and trace then give a positive integer multiple of the generic point supported on the chosen curve. Closing the relation and using localization on the first factor gives
\[
M\Delta_X=\Gamma_C+\Gamma_D,
\qquad
\operatorname{supp}\Gamma_C\subset X\times C,\quad
\operatorname{supp}\Gamma_D\subset D\times X .
\]
Universal support over arbitrary extensions is not required.

The contribution supported on \(X\times C\) lifts integrally to divisor correspondences on \(X\times\widetilde C\), after normalizing each curve component. A component dominating a curve component lifts with degree one under the normalization; a component over a singular point lifts after choosing a point of its normalization, again with degree one over the algebraically closed base. The resulting action on \(\mathrm{CH}^{n-1}(X)\) factors through \(\mathrm{CH}^0(\widetilde C)\). This group has no algebraically trivial part. Components supported at isolated points have a still simpler zero action.

For a projective resolution \(f_j:T_j\to D_j\to X\), every component supported on \(D_j\times X\) has a generically finite multisection in its inverse image. Thus it lifts after rational coefficients, including components over singular strata. Clearing the finitely many denominators gives integral \(G_j\in\mathrm{CH}^{n-1}(T_j\times X)\), with \(\dim T_j=n-1\), and the exact action
\[
M\operatorname{id}=\sum_j(G_j)_*f_j^*
\]
on algebraically trivial codimension-\((n-1)\) cycles. The degrees are correct: \(f_j^*\) produces zero-cycles on \(T_j\), and \(G_j\) has exactly the codimension needed to return codimension-\((n-1)\) cycles on \(X\).

## The integral family conclusion

Functoriality of Albanese equivalence makes this action factor through
\[
\prod_j P^{n-1}(T_j)
=\prod_j\operatorname{Alb}(T_j)(\mathbf C).
\]
For a family whose cylinder map is zero, the pullback family on \(T_j\) has zero cylinder in
\(H^{2n-3}(T_j,\mathbf Q)=H^{2\dim T_j-1}(T_j,\mathbf Q)\).
Faithfulness of the homology realization on homomorphisms of abelian varieties implies that the induced homomorphism to \(\operatorname{Alb}(T_j)\) is zero. Consequently \(M u_Z=0\).

This is sufficient integrally. For each \(a\in A(\mathbf C)\), divisibility supplies \(b\) with \(Mb=a\), and hence \(u_Z(a)=M u_Z(b)=0\). No assertion that the target \(P^{n-1}(X)\) is torsion-free is used.

## The same fourfold separates the assertions

Ottem and Suzuki, Lemma 2.4, printed author p. 4, proves \(\mathrm{CH}_0(W)=\mathbf Z\) for the threefold used in their construction. Therefore \(\mathrm{CH}_0(W\times E)\) is supported on \(\{w_0\}\times E\), by applying the equality of point classes in each fibre \(W\times\{e\}\). E.3j applies to every codimension-three abelian family on this same fourfold.

Corollary 4.2, printed author p. 10, supplies a surjective regular Walker map and a nontrivial finite kernel in the isogeny from the Walker intermediate Jacobian to the ordinary algebraic intermediate Jacobian. A point lifting a nonzero element of that kernel is ordinarily Abel–Jacobi trivial but not Albanese equivalent to zero, since regular homomorphisms annihilate the defining subgroup of Albanese equivalence. E.3g supplies an integral abelian-family parameterization of this individual point.

Thus the family-map converse holds and the proposed integral equality of point equivalence relations fails on the same fourfold. This refutes the literal integral biconditional without asserting that its family-map side fails. The rational formulation is unaffected.

## Related surface-radical calculation

We also checked the direct composition argument communicated by /root/cycles. For \(S=C^2\), let the homologically trivial modified diagonal be the degree-zero correspondence \(\gamma:S\to C\), and let \(\pi^*:C\to S\) be the transpose graph of a projection. Then \(a=\pi^*\gamma\in\operatorname{End}(S)\). For every endomorphism \(b\), the composite \(\gamma b\pi^*\) is a homologically trivial divisor correspondence on \(C\times C\), so it is zero modulo algebraic equivalence. Hence \(aba=0\), and \((ba)^2=0\); the inverse of \(1-ba\) is \(1+ba\). Thus \(a\) lies in the Jacobson radical. A fixed-point section in the fourth factor recovers \(\gamma\) from \(\gamma\times C\), so a nonzero modified-diagonal class gives a nonzero radical element. The remaining nonvanishing input is the primary Ceresa theorem, owned and audited by /root/cycles; we do not claim to have independently opened that source in this review.

## Opened primary source

Ottem, J. C., & Suzuki, F. (2020). *A pencil of Enriques surfaces with non-algebraic integral Hodge classes*. arXiv:1906.08994v2. Opened https://arxiv.org/pdf/1906.08994. Checked Lemma 2.4, author p. 4; Theorem 3.1 and its construction data, author pp. 6–8; Theorem 4.1, Corollary 4.2, and Remarks 4.3–4.4, author p. 10. We use the stated construction and Walker results as primary theorem inputs; this targeted review does not independently reprove their degeneration argument.

### Record: agents/frobenius/F2_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of F.2a–b

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent /root/frobenius. Date: 19 November 2026.

We read research/sections/F_state.tex, the two supplied source files, and the current Milne and published Debarre–Ein–Lazarsfeld–Voisin PDFs. The corrections and their proofs are sound. A minor scope clarification was suggested to the root: the elliptic curve in F.2b is over \(\mathbf C\), as required by the positive-form formulation.

## F.2a

Milne defines \(S^r\) using the generalized eigenvalue-one space of \(H^{2r}(-,\mathbf Q_\ell(r))\), printed p. 3. Theorem 1.6 on the same page instead prints \(S^{2d}(X\times X,\ell)\), while its proof explicitly uses \(H^{2d}(X\times X,\mathbf Q_\ell(d))\). The corrected sufficient hypothesis is therefore \(S^d\). We checked the independent linear-algebra argument: after extending scalars, a generalized Frobenius block \(V_a\) pairs with its Poincaré-dual reciprocal block, and the tensor is \(\operatorname{End}(V_a)\) with conjugation action. A nontrivial unipotent factor cannot act trivially by conjugation, because the centre of the full endomorphism algebra consists of scalars and a unipotent scalar is one. Partial semisimplicity at eigenvalue one thus forces every block to be semisimple.

The common-field extension powers on printed p. 2 are crossed: a \(p^{n_1}\)-Frobenius and a \(p^{n_2}\)-Frobenius satisfy \(\pi_1^{n_2N}=\pi_2^{n_1N}\) for some positive \(N\). Printed p. 4 uses uncrossed powers in the Tate-structure equivalence definition. The \(H^2(\mathbf P^1)\) test with eigenvalues \(p,p^2\) proves the incompatibility directly. Both errors also occur in the supplied TeX; they are not introduced by extraction.

## F.2b

The published Theorem 2.1, Corollary 2.2, and proof on pp. 1799–1801 say exactly what F.2b attributes to them. In particular Corollary 2.2 has the range \(2\le k\le n-2\), whereas the supplied early TeX prints the upper endpoint \(n\), despite correctly writing \(n-2\) in the preceding sentence.

The published proof expressly takes the closure of the convex cone of rank-one forms \(i\ell\wedge\overline\ell\) with coefficients in the CM order. The supplied proof instead infers generation of that cone from real positive generation of the vector space of one-forms. This inference is not valid without closure: a sum of positive semidefinite rank-one forms has rank one only if every nonzero summand has the same complex line, and an arbitrary line need not contain a vector with coefficients in the CM fraction field. The root's corrected approximation proof is exact. The fraction field is dense in \(\mathbf C\), simultaneous approximation and clearing denominators produce CM-order directions, and positive rescaling gives a convergent sequence of the corresponding rank-one forms. Each is the class, up to a positive real scalar, of the pullback of the origin under a homomorphism \(E^n\to E\). Closedness of the pseudoeffective cone supplies the limit.

## Opened sources

- Milne, J. S. (2007, author revision 3 November 2021). *The Tate conjecture over finite fields (AIM talk).* https://arxiv.org/pdf/0709.3040. Checked printed pp. 2–4, particularly the definition of \(S^r\), Theorem 1.6 and its proof, and the Tate-structure equivalence definition. The arXiv header retains an older upload date while the body states the 2021 revision.
- Debarre, O., Ein, L., Lazarsfeld, R., & Voisin, C. (2011). Pseudoeffective and nef classes on abelian varieties. *Compositio Mathematica, 147*, 1793–1818. https://doi.org/10.1112/S0010437X11005227. Opened full published primary PDF at https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~roblaz/Reprints/Debarre.Ein.Laz.Voisin.PNAV.pdf. Checked Theorem 2.1, Corollary 2.2 and proof, pp. 1799–1801, and the equality of the positive-form cones at endpoint codimensions on p. 1794.
- Supplied primary source witnesses read: background/literature/milne_tate_conjecture_finite_fields/Tate2007.tex and background/literature/debarre_ein_lazarsfeld_voisin_pseudoeffective_nef/1003.3183.tex.

### Record: agents/frobenius/K0_actual_family_descent_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit: actual K0-family descent from completion

**Outcome: passed as a K0 auxiliary lemma.** Audited the complete §7 of `research/agents/real_weil/TOP_QUOTIENT_RESEARCH.md` on 2026-11-19, with primary-source checks completed at approximately 08:59 UTC. This is not a new numbered result and does not assert any Chow-group conclusion.

Let R be an excellent henselian local ring equipped with its specified coefficient field k equal to the residue field. For a qcqs R-scheme Z, the quotient of K0 of perfect complexes by differences of evaluations of actual classes on products with smooth projective connected k-curves injects integrally into the analogous quotient after completion.

The proof is correct. Excellence makes R to its completion regular. Popescu expresses that completion as a filtered colimit of smooth finitely presented R-algebras. Perfect complexes, their morphisms, and finite triangle relations descend to a single stage. In particular an equality in K0 uses finitely many generators and relations; it is not necessary to claim any inverse-limit continuity of K-theory. The residue map from that smooth stage to k lifts to R by the henselian lifting lemma. Pulling back the already descended actual complexes and relations proves the assertion. The selected coefficient field and all proper curve parameters are preserved because this is an R-algebra map.

All four cited primary sources were opened and inspected:

- Stacks Project, Theorem 16.12.1 (Tag 07GC), https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/07GC: regular maps between Noetherian rings are filtered colimits of smooth maps. Web reference `turn612165832view0`.
- Stacks Project, Situation 36.29.1 (Tag 09RC), https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/09RC: qcqs terms and affine transition maps; the adjacent Lemma 36.29.2 also supplies full faithfulness. Web reference `turn115228967view0`.
- Stacks Project, Lemma 36.29.3 (Tag 09RF), https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/09RF: perfect derived categories commute with the indicated limit. Statement and proof read. Web reference `turn612165832view1`.
- Stacks Project, Lemma 15.13.3 (Tag 0H74), https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0H74: a map from a smooth R-algebra to A/I lifts to A for a henselian pair (A,I). Statement and full proof read. Web reference `turn115228967view1`.

The limitations written by the owner are essential and accurate: this descends an already existing actual family over the completion; it does not algebraize arbitrary compatible formal K0 classes, and it does not establish a comparison with ordinary Chow equivalence.

### Record: agents/frobenius/Nori_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.1i: explicit counterexamples in every non-top range

Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), agent `/root/frobenius`.
Date: 2026-11-19.
Reviewed file: `research/agents/cycles/nori_counterexamples.tex`.
Conclusion: **the proof and its numerical hypotheses are valid as written**. No change to the statement or degree choice is required. We did not modify the cycles agent's files.

## Exact primary statements opened

1. Nagel (2002), *Effective bounds for Hodge-theoretic connectivity*, author PDF <https://nagel49.perso.math.cnrs.fr/connect.pdf>, Theorem 3.13, printed p.23. Opened full PDF (`turn683175876view0`), exact theorem (`turn191802653view3`), and independently inspected the rendered primary page `research/agents/cycles/output/nagel-theorem313.png`.
2. Nagel (2004), *Lectures on Nori's connectivity theorem*, author PDF <https://nagel49.perso.math.cnrs.fr/grenoble.pdf>, Theorem 5.11 and following bibliographical paragraph, printed p.35. Opened full PDF (`turn683175876view1`) and exact theorem (`turn546446229view0`). The paragraph explicitly says that the condition that the connectivity parameter be at most n should be added to the 2002 statement. Theorem 6.2 and its proof, printed pp.36–37, were also read (`turn546446229view2`).
3. Nagel (1998), *Effective bounds for Nori's connectivity theorem*, author PDF <https://nagel49.perso.math.cnrs.fr/Nori_CRAS.pdf>, printed p.3, immediately before Theorem 2, states that the regularity constant is one for every smooth quadric (`turn546446229view1`). The same fact is Exercise 5.8 in the 2004 author version, printed p.34 (`turn546446229view0`). The 1998 announcement's sharper condition (C) is not used. Nagel2002 Remark3.14(i), printed p.26, explicitly corrects it (`turn191802653view3`).

These are primary author versions. Their printed pagination is not silently identified with journal pagination. The 2002 theorem works over the smooth-fibre open U of the projectivization of the direct sum of section spaces, exactly the parameter space used in C.1i; it applies to every smooth morphism T→U, without a dimension restriction on T.

## Parameters and inequalities

To avoid the collision between the cycle codimension and Nagel's connectivity parameter, write c for the cycle codimension and b for the latter parameter. We have

- c<n<2c;
- dim Q=2c;
- s=2c−n is a positive integer;
- r=s−1=2c−n−1<n;
- b=n, so b≤n and μ=floor((n+b)/2)=n;
- every degree is D=4c+2;
- m_Q=1.

Nagel's condition (C) is

\[
\sum_{\nu=\min(b,r)}^r d_\nu\ge m_Q+\dim Q-1.
\]

Since r<b, its left side is exactly D and its right side is 2c. Each condition (C_i), for 0≤i≤min(b−1,r)=r, is

\[
\sum_{\nu=i}^rD+(\mu-b+i)D
 = (r-i+1)D+iD=sD
 \ge1+2c+n-i.
\]

The right side is at most 4c, since n≤2c−1. The left side is at least D=4c+2. Thus every inequality has positive margin, including the hypersurface case s=1. No asymptotic choice remains.

The conclusion is relative cohomology vanishing in every degree at most n+b=2n. In the algebraic-equivalence argument, injectivity in degree 2c uses H^{2c}(Q×T,X_T)=0, and surjectivity in degree 2c after base change to the curve family uses H^{2c+1}(Q×C,X_C)=0. Since c≤n−1, both degrees lie within the asserted range. The extra degree needed for surjectivity is present.

## Finite witness, field, and trace

A relation on the geometric generic fibre is finite algebraic data. In characteristic zero, a finite definition-field extension becomes finite étale over a nonempty open of the original smooth parameter space. After further shrinking, the curve family is smooth projective, its degree-zero divisor has degree zero on every connected fibre component, and the Chow equality and any rational-equivalence witnesses hold on total spaces. Both T and the total curve space C are therefore permitted smooth bases for Nagel's theorem.

The cohomological lifting and subsequent pushforward are precisely the mechanism in Nagel2004 Theorem6.2. Proper base change for the curve projection identifies its fibre restriction. The degree-zero divisor has zero class on each fibre curve, so the class of the original ambient cycle vanishes. This is a direct contradiction argument for finite data on the specified geometric generic fibre; no assertion about fields of definition of analytic exceptional loci is needed.

For the stronger Lefschetz-image obstruction, tracing a cycle over a finite extension to the rational function field is legitimate with rational coefficients. All conjugate algebraic-equivalence relations yield the trace relation. The universal common-zero incidence is the projectivization of the kernel of a surjective evaluation map over Q. Its projective-bundle hyperplane class is pulled back from the parameter projective space and becomes zero on the generic fibre. Thus the projective-bundle formula expresses the traced generic cycle as the restriction of an ambient cycle, with the asserted codimension. This step does not assume that an arbitrary geometric generic cycle descends before taking trace.

## Quadric and homological calculations

The two displayed maximal linear spaces meet in a projective (c−1)-plane and are the two components of the indicated linear section, so their sum is h^c. The normal sequence gives self-intersection (1+(-1)^c)/2. Hence, from Z=2[Π_+]−h^c, its square has degree 2(-1)^c. The equal degrees of the two planes imply h cl(Z)=0 because H^{2c+2}(Q) is one-dimensional. The primitive class is therefore nonzero and orthogonal to every h times an ambient codimension-(c−1) class.

On the complete intersection, weak Lefschetz applies in degree 2n−2c<n. The push-pull pairing contains h^s with s≥1 and hence annihilates cl(Z); Poincaré duality gives cl(z)=0. Together with the finite-witness injectivity, this proves the nonzero Griffiths class and the stronger exclusion from L A^{c−1}_{alg}(X).

The persistence assertions use only finite-type spreading and specialization over an algebraically closed base field. A hypothetical new relation after an algebraically closed extension would specialize to one over the original algebraically closed field. They therefore apply also after adjoining the generic hyperplane and under every embedding into C.

## Scope and status

The construction covers exactly every integer pair c<n<2c, beginning at (c,n)=(2,3). In the remaining nonzero high-codimension case c=n, cycles modulo algebraic equivalence are determined by degree on a connected smooth projective variety, and a point of the smooth hyperplane has degree one. Thus the universal dichotomy stated in C.1i follows. Groups with c>n vanish. This review does not claim a positive-characteristic version of Nori's connectivity theorem.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C3_uniform_gysin_REPORT.md

# C.3t: explicit nonzero ordinary Gysin images

The owned LaTeX fragment C3_uniform_gysin.tex proves a non-torsion ordinary algebraic Gysin image for every r>=2 and r+1<=N<=2r-1. The endpoint N=2r-1 is exactly the proposed low-degree critical equality i=(N-1)/2, so the construction extends the earlier three-dimensional example to every odd critical dimension.

We use the Segre cones from C.3s and a smooth incidence divisor with explicitly specified linear sections. Its base is a smooth weighted Fermat hypersurface; inside it, an explicit smooth plane Fermat curve contains both the generic point witness and the fixed comparison point. Thus the ordinary algebraic equivalence comes from an actual flat curve family and needs no Bertini or abstract local-Picard family identification. The proper pushforward has coefficient1. C.3e preserves the nonzero quotient class in the strict-local puncture.

The construction depends on the already audited C.3e and C.3s. It introduces no new literature theorem and no claim of priority. The main limitation is exact: for m=r-1 the base of the incidence becomes zero-dimensional and this family argument no longer applies. Characteristic zero is part of the statement, and nothing here asserts that its transcendental-point witness remains non-torsion over Fpbar.

Both the root and cycles have independently audited the complete typed proof and passed it. Cycles has been asked, with the root's authorization, to integrate the fragment after C.3s. The script and output are mathematical certificates of the explicit algebraic identities in a finite grid; the uniform argument and non-torsion are proved in the fragment itself.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_lagrangian_divisor_obstruction_REPORT.md

# Exact obstruction for Lagrangian-surface divisor pushforwards

Status: **proved method exclusion; no main-gate movement and no priority claim**.

We prove that every divisor on each of two proposed full-span Lagrangian surfaces has an even mixed Matsusaka coefficient after mapping to `A × A^dual`, when `A` is a generic type-(1,2) surface with `End(A)=Z`. This checks all divisor classes, not merely the fundamental surface class or the restrictions of ambient divisors.

The decisive property is that the surface's Albanese variety admits an odd-degree isogeny from a power of `A`. On `A^n`, every integral curve homomorphism is `lambda_d S`, with `S` a symmetric integral matrix. A homomorphism to `A × A^dual` has rows `(r, lambda s)`, and its mixed curve coefficient is `2 r S s^t`. Finite-flat pullback along an odd-degree isogeny preserves the parity obstruction. Albanese factorization handles every divisor curve on the surface.

The full proof is `C4_lagrangian_divisor_obstruction.tex`. It is intentionally unnumbered and separate from the integrated main results. The parent may assign a label if it is useful in the final methods discussion; it should not be counted as a resolution of the remaining surface case.

## Opened primary sources

* Bastianelli, Pirola, and Stoppino, *Galois closure and Lagrangian varieties*, author version v2 (14 June 2010), <https://arxiv.org/pdf/0912.3377>. We opened the complete primary PDF and read Theorem 6.8, author pp. 25–26, including the Albanese-lattice proof. It identifies the Albanese variety of their LG surface with the square of the associated type-(1,2) surface. The publication is Advances in Mathematics 225(6), 3463–3501 (2010), DOI 10.1016/j.aim.2010.06.006. No assertion here relies on their Proposition 7.6 or on a claim that the surface's Neron–Severi group is generated by ambient divisors.
* Grossi and Moretti, *An explicit class of Lagrangian surfaces*, author version v1 (18 February 2025), <https://arxiv.org/html/2502.13087v1>. We opened the full primary HTML and read Proposition 4.2, Theorem 5.6, Proposition 6.2, and Corollary 6.3. Their associated type-(1,2) surface is `A_0/G`, with `|G|=3`, and the quotient surface has Albanese variety `(A_0 × A_0)/diag K`, with `K=(Z/3)^2`. The two are related by odd-degree isogenies. Publisher metadata gives International Journal of Mathematics 37(10), article 2650052 (2026), DOI 10.1142/S0129167X26500527; the mathematical locators used here refer to the opened author version, not to an independently read published PDF.

## Independent check

The cycles agent independently checked the full algebraic argument and its treatment of arbitrary surface divisors. In particular, that audit confirmed the direction of the isogeny, the factor of two in the mixed block, the odd-degree pullback argument, and the absence of any ambient-NS-generation hypothesis.

## Remaining gate

The argument controls only curves obtained from varieties whose Albanese is odd-isogenous to a power of the original surface. It does not control all curves spanning `A × A^dual`. The exact integral numerical Poincare-cycle criterion remains unchanged. Likewise no consequence for arbitrary supersingular threefolds in characteristics two or three is asserted.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_positive_characteristic_RM_REPORT.md

# C.4x — full real multiplication in positive characteristic

The original Yu 2003 paper gives a direct argument in positive characteristic. No canonical lift, supersingular realization, or ordinary hypothesis is needed.

## Exact proved scope

For a dimension-g abelian variety over an algebraically closed field of characteristic p, with a full degree-g totally real integer-ring action and a compatible polarization, the polarization Euler-characteristic gcd is a p-power. Under the intrinsic equality of Lie-module and regular-module classes in the Grothendieck group, that gcd is one. Total ramification of p forces the equality because the coefficient algebra is local Artinian with a unique simple module and both modules have length g.

Combining with the previously proved actual-factorization bounds gives s_J dividing 2's p-part for surfaces and 6's p-part for threefolds. The Lie condition removes the remaining characteristic-prime factor. No failure statement is inferred if the condition fails.

The complete typed proof passed independent checks by both `/root/cycles` and `/root`. The parent additionally opened the complete published primary source, checked the multiple-prime reduction and visually inspected the decisive pages. Their audit receipts were logged at the tool-clock time 2026-11-19 07:45:14 UTC. The owner of the C section confirmed integration after C.4w at 07:47 UTC.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_real_multiplication_REPORT.md

# C.4v: full real multiplication

We prove a new affirmative family rather than merely a reformulation of the residual gate. For a characteristic-zero abelian variety of dimension g with the full ring of integers of a totally real degree-g field acting and fixed by the Rosati involution of a polarization, the gcd of Euler characteristics of line bundles is one. Therefore all such surfaces and threefolds are actual direct summands of products of Jacobians by C.4j.

The proof is an integral trace-lattice argument. The homology is projective of rank two over the full integer ring. Its balanced integral alternating forms form an invertible codifferent-valued determinant module. A local generator supplies a perfect pairing at any chosen rational prime; its residue lifts globally, and a large positive multiple of that prime times an existing polarization supplies positivity without changing perfection. All balanced forms have Hodge type (1,1), as shown explicitly on the real two-plane decomposition. The prime may be two; no inversion of two is hidden.

The same result includes simple varieties whose full endomorphism ring is the maximal order of a CM field, using the unique positive involution. This exact input was freshly opened in Milne's primary notes, Proposition1.39/Corollary1.40pp20–21. Appell–Humbert and the positivity theorem were freshly opened in the proof of Proposition6.1pp46–47. No priority claim is made.

Root and cycles completed full typed proof and source reviews and approved integration. It also includes a good-reduction consequence, justified by extending each of the finitely many required line bundles to the regular abelian scheme and preserving Euler characteristic. The generic End=Z type(1,2) surface and the nonmaximal obstruction-prime cases remain untreated.

Cycles completed a full typed proof/deduction audit, received by 2026-11-19T06:53:44Z, and passed it. Root source audit remains separately recorded.

Root completed the full typed proof and primary-source audit and approved integration; logged at tool-clock time 2026-11-19T06:54:33Z (parent prose timestamp corrected against tool clock).

## Local maximality refinement, 2026-11-19T07:10:04Z

For an order O of index c in O_F, the integral lattice of balanced alternating forms is cut out by finitely many integral linear equations and therefore localizes exactly. At each prime not dividing c the perfect codifferent form exists and the same positive residue-lift proof produces a line bundle with Euler characteristic prime to that prime. Thus every prime divisor of delta(A) divides c. Combining the established universal factors two and six with s_J dividing delta gives actual splitting for surfaces with odd c and threefolds with c prime to six. The ordinary canonical lift and good-reduction specialization preserve this conclusion. This is a sharpening of the existing statement ID, not a separate result. Root proposed and prechecked the same argument; final targeted cycles audit requested.

Cycles completed and passed the targeted full typed audit of the local-order refinement. Root has also read and passed the typed refinement; the precise clock event is recorded separately.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_residual_followup_0835_REPORT.md

# Further residual checks, 19 November 2026

Tool clock during these checks: 08:35:53 UTC. These are source and method checks, not a new closure of the type-(1,2) surface or supersingular-threefold cases. No new result number is assigned.

## Supersingular cycles: kernel information is not saturation

We opened Oliver Gregory's complete published HTML, *Torsion codimension 2 cycles on supersingular abelian varieties*, Canadian Mathematical Bulletin 66(2) (2023), 458–466, DOI 10.4153/S0008439522000431, at <https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/canadian-mathematical-bulletin/article/torsion-codimension-2-cycles-on-supersingular-abelian-varieties/9FA8E4E719B07441E78F9FF936C6FDC2>.

Theorems 1.1 and 4.1(1) prove that torsion codimension-two cycles on a supersingular abelian variety over any algebraically closed field of positive characteristic are algebraically trivial. Theorem 4.1(2) proves integral equality of homological and algebraic equivalence in codimension two over the algebraic closure of a finite field. These are precise results about the kernel of the cycle class map. They do not assert saturation of its image in the integral crystalline lattice. In §2 the displayed crystalline comparison explicitly tensors with `K=W(k)[1/p]`. Thus that comparison does not eliminate the remaining characteristic-prime coefficient in C.4u. We read the proof strategy in §4, including its use of a denominator-cleared diagonal decomposition and divisibility for the torsion Abel–Jacobi cokernel; this divisibility argument does not apply to the finite-index numerical cycle lattice in C.4r.

## Prym schemes in characteristic two

We independently opened the full 52-page author PDF of Achter and Casalaina-Martin, *Putting the P back in Prym*, dated 18 December 2024, at <https://www.math.colostate.edu/~achter/math/prym1224.pdf>. Theorem A (author p. 2), Theorem 5.12, Theorem 6.4 (author p. 46), and Lemma 5.17 (author pp. 44–45) retain characteristic two and the finite separable-cover hypotheses. The odd exponent-three case is an étale noncyclic degree-three cover of a genus-two curve and produces a **principally polarized two-dimensional** Prym. It is not an existence theorem for the generic nonprincipally polarized type-(1,2) surface or an arbitrary supersingular threefold. Theorem 5.3 and the relative Theorem 6.1 use numerical curve equivalence, consistent with the Matsusaka method already used in C.4. We do not infer a new existence result from their all-characteristic comparison theorem.

## Recent Weil-fourfold theorem

We opened the complete 24-page author PDF of Floccari and Fu, *The Hodge conjecture for Weil fourfolds with discriminant 1 via singular OG6-varieties*, at <https://irma.math.unistra.fr/~lfu/articles/HCWeilViaOG6.pdf>. Published metadata: Journal de Mathématiques Pures et Appliquées 210 (2026), article 103876, DOI 10.1016/j.matpur.2026.103876. Theorems 1.1–1.3, author pp. 2–3, give rational Hodge algebraicity and an isogeny description. Theorem 1.5, author p. 4, is explicitly formulated with rational l-adic coefficients. The footnote on author p. 5 states that the argument needs the Kuga–Satake construction only up to isogeny. These assertions do not supply an integral divided Poincare class on the residual product. We checked exact scope, not every proof in this paper. Its singular-locus/double-cover geometry remains a possible source of new cycles, but no odd mixed coefficient has been constructed from it here.

## Integral Tate counterexamples in characteristic zero

We opened the full primary HTML of J. S. Milne, *On the integral Tate conjecture for abelian varieties*, arXiv:2509.06707v1 (8 September 2025), at <https://arxiv.org/html/2509.06707v1>. Its unnumbered theorem transfers a failure of integral Hodge over C to a failure of integral Tate after a finite extension of a field finitely generated over Q. The field has characteristic zero. It neither proves a failure for the particular product of a type-(1,2) surface with its dual nor supplies a positive-characteristic counterexample by specialization. The distinction is retained.

## Other search leads not used as theorems

A primary arXiv abstract for Huang–Jiang–Yang, *Degree of irrationality of abelian surfaces*, arXiv:2609.02169, states a classification of the surfaces admitting a degree-three rational map to the plane. We did not audit its full proof or use this as a new manuscript theorem. A degree-three rational map alone does not furnish an odd scalar factorization through Jacobians. The explicitly associated Lagrangian Galois-closure construction is already covered by the all-divisor parity lemma in the separate report.

The remaining main cases are unchanged. This conclusion means that these particular checks did not resolve them; it is not a claim that their status in the entire literature has been proved open.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_residual_literature_2026_REPORT.md

# Residual C.4 investigation: source checks and unsuccessful routes

Date: 2026-11-19. This report records further work after C.4x. It contains no new claim that Question 2 is settled for a generic endomorphism-ring-Z type-(1,2) surface or for an arbitrary supersingular threefold in characteristics 2 and 3. No new numbered theorem or completion row is created for these searches.

## 1. Precise remaining objects

The main surface test remains a complex abelian surface A with End(A)=Z and primitive Neron–Severi generator of type (1,2). The exact ordinary integral numerical Poincare-cycle criterion of C.4r remains decisive. The calculation for cycles supported in proper abelian-subvariety translates in C.4q only treats that subgroup. The non-Jacobian result C.4t for the principal polarizations of A×Ahat does not exclude signed minimal cycles or a direct summand in a larger product of Jacobians.

For supersingular threefolds, C.4u reduces the obstruction to the characteristic prime and the universal bound reduces its exponent to one: only 2 or 3 can remain in characteristics 2 or 3, respectively. C.4x disposes of real multiplication examples satisfying its full action and Lie-module hypotheses; no such action has been supplied on an arbitrary supersingular threefold.

## 2. Kahn 2026: the latest version distinguishes the required comparison explicitly

Opened the complete current HTML of Bruno Kahn, *Divided powers on abelian varieties*, arXiv:2602.11135v2, submitted 8 June 2026 (internal date 5 June). URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2602.11135v2 . The earlier author PDF https://webusers.imj-prg.fr/~bruno.kahn/preprints/div-ab01.pdf is the first version dated February 2026; it is not silently used as the current version.

Exact checks:

- Proposition 1.1 proves weak divided powers in **etale Chow groups**, away from the characteristic.
- Theorem 1.2 and Corollary 1.3 give the canonical structure and Fourier transform in the stated etale quotient, with the precise treatment of 2-torsion.
- The introduction, under “Algebraic and etale cohomology classes,” and Question 1.11 explicitly distinguish ordinary algebraic classes from etale classes. Their rational equality and the saturation of the latter do not identify the two integral lattices.
- Proposition B.6 identifies the torsion cokernel of the ordinary one-cycle class map as a quotient, with divisible kernel, of H^{g−3}(X,H^g_et(Q_l/Z_l(g−1))). Question B.7 asks for control of multiplication maps on this group. This is an obstruction formulation, not a proof of its vanishing on A×Ahat.
- Theorem 1.10 has been corrected in v2. Its relative statement is restricted to the appropriate eigenspaces for symmetric line bundles and inverts M!p, with M=min(2g,cd(S)+2). The blanket relative assertion of v1 is explicitly withdrawn by the author. We use neither the withdrawn assertion nor a relative ordinary Chow assertion.

Opened references: turn968976604view1; turn561926606view0–3. The relevant statements and surrounding text were read, not merely their abstracts. No independent verification of the whole new preprint is claimed. In particular, existence of an etale class P^3/3! on a fourfold does not finish C.4.

## 3. Hasan–Hassan–Lin–Manivel–McBeath–Moonen 2025

Opened the full published article *Integral aspects of Fourier duality for abelian varieties*, manuscripta mathematica 176, article 64 (25 August 2025), https://doi.org/10.1007/s00229-025-01662-7 .

Theorem 4.5 constructs a transform lifting (2g+d)! times the usual one. Theorem 5.5 gives the Fourier identities and Beauville decomposition after passing to Z[1/(2g+d+1)!]; the polarization-dependent sl2 statement additionally inverts the polarization degree parameter. The positive-characteristic hypotheses in Section 3.2 are retained. These results control denominators but invert the very small primes at issue here. They therefore supply no missing odd surface class or characteristic-2/3 supersingular class.

Opened references: turn537918067view2; turn598248693view2–3. This is a source-scope check, not a new obstruction theorem.

## 4. Why the literal matroidal-family theorem is not immediately applicable

Opened the latest full Engel–de Gaay Fortman–Schreieder version, https://arxiv.org/html/2507.15704v3 . The relevant hypotheses are in Definition 4.1 and Theorem 4.10; the monodromy formalism is in Section 2.5, especially Definition 2.12 and Lemma 2.13.

Their matroidal family has nonzero rank-one monodromy logarithms around the distinguished independent boundary components. For a family of abelian varieties of the form A×Ahat, a relative polarization identifies H1(A,Q) and H1(Ahat,Q) as rational local systems. Thus every monodromy logarithm on the product is a direct sum of two conjugate operators and has rank twice the rank of the operator on A. This persists under isogeny and finite base change. Consequently a nonzero rank-one logarithm cannot occur in such an isotypic family.

This elementary rank observation shows that the literal matroidal-family hypothesis cannot simply be asserted for the residual surface family. It does **not** rule out adaptations to grouped rank-two degenerations, and does **not** imply algebraicity or nonalgebraicity of the desired cycle. The very-general ppav conclusion also does not specialize in either direction to this special locus.

Opened references: turn864092994view0; turn763967464view3; turn681223935view0. The later Engel–Schreieder 2026 degree paper and the sixfold optimality paper were already checked in the C.4x report; their hypotheses do not erase this distinction.

## 5. Supersingular polarizations and the small-Mumford–Tate lifting route

Opened Oort, *Newton polygons and formal groups: Conjectures by Manin and Grothendieck*, Annals of Mathematics 152(1) (2000), 183–206, complete published PDF at https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/oort.pdf . Remark 6.4, pp. 204–205, records a three-dimensional supersingular component in the moduli of dimension-three varieties with polarization degree p^6, for every prime p. The paper later writes p^3 once for that degree; we retain the initial moduli convention and do not propagate this inconsistent exponent.

The comparison source https://webspace.science.uu.nl/~oort0109/OortFinalEdited.pdf was also opened. Theorem 7.5(3), author p. 28, states dimension floor(g^2/4) for the principally polarized supersingular locus, and Remarks 7.6 and 8.6 explain failures of this behavior without principal polarizations. These sources prevent treating principal or prime-to-characteristic polarizability of arbitrary supersingular threefolds as automatic. They do not by themselves resolve the Jacobian-summand problem. The original references Katsura–Oort (1987), Remark 6.10, and Oda–Oort (1978), Corollary 3.4, were identified from Oort's bibliography but have not been marked as independently opened in this continuation.

Opened Lam–Oswal, *Lifts of supersingular abelian varieties with small Mumford–Tate groups*, complete author version arXiv:2208.07809v1, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2208.07809 . Theorem 1.2(2), p. 2, lifts supersingular threefolds over Fpbar to varieties isogenous to a product of elliptic curves. Remark 1.3 specifies Witt-vector liftings; Section 1.6, p. 3, assumes p odd throughout. Proposition 2.1 makes the filtered Dieudonne isogeny formulation explicit. Publisher metadata for the later IMRN 2024 paper was located, but the full publisher text failed to load; the theorem locators and odd-characteristic restriction here refer explicitly to the opened author version.

The lift does not produce an actual product or a retraction of prime-to-p degree. No opened primary result upgraded an arbitrary integral gluing of elliptic factors into a Jacobian-product summand. We therefore do not use the lift to remove the residual p-primary factor. The theorem does not cover p=2 in the opened version.

Opened references: turn537918067view0–1; turn598248693view0–1; turn770069476view0; turn968976604view0; turn971900698view1. Publisher full-text failure: turn971900698view0.

## 6. Odd Prym–Tyurin and secant-sheaf leads

Opened Lange–Recillas–Rojas, *A family of Prym–Tyurin varieties of exponent 3*, complete author version https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0412103 . The actual Theorem 5.2 on author p. 13 identifies the constructed principally polarized varieties as products of two trigonal Jacobians. The introduction on p. 2 misnumbers this as Theorem 5.3; the actual Lemma 5.3 is a representation identity. We checked the theorem and its proof on pp. 13–17, as well as Corollary 6.4 on p. 20. These were used only to test applicability: this family does not assert that the residual type-(1,2) product A×Ahat is covered. An odd Prym–Tyurin realization there would be useful, but is not supplied by this source. Opened references: turn945148879view0 and turn141016688view0–1.

Reopened Markman's complete current arXiv HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2502.03415v2 and its Sections 1.2–1.3. The entry labels itself v2 while its internal date is 24 August 2026; we record that metadata rather than assigning an invented version. The Orlov exterior-product construction has already been tested by the explicit parity calculation and script in C4_Orlov_method_REVIEW.md. That calculation limits that construction only. Rational algebraicity of Weil classes and later deformation statements do not provide the missing integral coefficient automatically. Opened reference: turn433611671view0. No full independent proof audit of this preprint's deformation theorem is claimed.

## 7. Status after these checks

These are additional primary-source and method checks. They have not produced a new counterexample or an integral splitting in either main residual case. In particular, “no theorem found in these searches” is not recorded as a proof that the remaining cases are open in the literature. The strongest proved positive subfamilies, uniform numerical bounds, and exact numerical criterion remain those already stated in C.4h–x.

### Record: agents/frobenius/C4_supersingular_REPORT.md

# C.4u: supersingular threefolds away from characteristics two and three

The full proof is in C4_supersingular_addition.tex. It supplies an actual new affirmative family in Question 2: **all supersingular abelian threefolds over algebraically closed fields of characteristic at least five**, without assuming principal polarizability. We do not count the accompanying factorial reformulation as a separate gate closure.

The new primary input is the p-primary minimal-isogeny construction in Yu's Lemma 3.2 proof, author p. 7, using Lemma 2.4, p. 5. The full arXiv v2 was opened, saved, and extracted locally. Since the base field is perfect, the field extension in the statement disappears. The finite-index Dieudonne lattice quotient is killed by a power of p. Hence a power of p factors through a product of supersingular elliptic curves, which is a product of Jacobians. The already proved factorization of six for arbitrary threefolds now combines with it to give the displayed exact identity factorization.

Cycles independently reopened the exact primary proof and passed the complete mathematical argument. Root independently opened the exact primary source and passed the full typed proof, including the explicit retraction; integration is authorized. No claim for all supersingular threefolds in characteristics two or three is made; the bound is reduced to the characteristic prime in each case. The literature question of principal polarizability in those two cases is being checked separately. No priority claim is made.

# Independent root audit of C.3ae

Complete typed proof audit: PASS.

The first full draft was read after scheduled review4. This result does not retroactively alter that dry review. It narrows the higher ordinary-J restriction gate for the next scheduled review.

1. The four integer sections are base point free on the specified product over C: their projective common-zero scheme is proper over Z and the independently computed empty fibre at101 excludes a generic point. The finite rank calculation, independently reconstructed, has first57-row determinant47 modulo101. Those tests by themselves were never a theorem certification.
2. The Segre contraction is projective and an isomorphism off the zero section. The hypersurface is integral because its graph is dense and its vertical locus has codimension2 in the base. Properness removes every nonsmooth point away from the exceptional fibre after shrinking the base. Normalization is finite; the connected exceptional fibre and proper surjectivity give a unique point over the vertex. Segre coordinates generate the exceptional ideal, and every extra normalized maximal-ideal function vanishes on the reduced fibre. Thus the actual fibre and its first thickening have the stated form after strict henselization.
3. The regular section is the line bundle over P_lines(E), rankE=m>=3. Localization and self-intersection give sigma on the ordinary Chow groups of the actual strict local puncture, including families. Degree2 is below the projective-bundle relation, so h=-a gives exactly CH2(S times P). Refined pullback commutes with parameter fibres and transverse restriction, giving b0-a b1+a^2 b2 with no varying-neighbourhood spread.
4. The root reopened Stacks Lemma42.58.1 Tag0FEY and Remark42.59.9 Tag0FF8, including their proofs and hypotheses. Relative-to-C differentials on the regular model give the conormal exterior sequence. The Atiyah/Chern-character cocycle lifts before restriction. Rational Chern character supplies a virtual bundle with the prescribed codimension2 cycle, and refined Gysin compatibility gives the necessary first-order Hodge obstruction. This invokes no converse variational Hodge assertion.
5. The quartic Euler/normal sequences identify the dual multiplication maps R4* to R3*, with hyperplane class exactly the common annihilator. The curve Jacobian-ring classes delta(z_kG) form a basis; using their dual basis avoids an unjustified monomial self-duality. The chosen Kunneth projection receives precisely the W(1,1)-C(1,0)-P(0,1) component, and the rank57 matrix kills its primitive part.
6. Intersecting with tau=4D-H kills the hyperplane component. Every mixed divisor component in b1 comes from C times P because q(W)=0; multiplication by a contributes H_W and pairs to zero with tau. Codimension0 b2 is constant. These facts remove the actual localization ambiguity. The resulting Picard classifying map has zero differential and is constant in characteristic0.
7. Hence rho kills every ordinary source-family difference integrally. The target product cycles satisfy rho theta=-9 id on Pic0(C)(C), and multiplication by9 is surjective. The explicit transcendental Abel point is nontorsion because torsion and the Abel closed immersion are defined over Qbar. Thus the ordinary cokernel surjects onto Pic0(C)(C) for every N>=7, and rational surjectivity fails. No assertion that rho kills every CH2(X) class is made.

Exact primary URLs opened by root: https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FEY and https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FF8. Earlier root finite-certificate output is code/check_quartic_obstruction_independent_output.json. Frobenius independently passed the complete typed proof as well.

# Root complete C.3aj audit

Recorded 2026-11-19T09:27:41.750112+00:00. Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode), /root.

We read the entire typed high-range construction and the entire product-Hodge input independently. The result is certified for every r>=2 and N>=2r+2, with ordinary algebraic equivalence represented by actual fixed-product curve families. No unrestricted Chow-image annihilation is asserted.

1. The chosen complex tuple of quartics has explicitly imposed Hodge genericity, rank-two Neron--Severi groups, scalar transcendental endomorphisms and no pairwise Hodge maps. The SO20 subdirect-product argument, D10 standard representation, rational intertwiner descent, CM torus and precise selected Kunneth level calculation all pass.
2. The countable coefficient field is generated only after choosing that Hodge-generic tuple. Independent section coefficients meet two independently nonempty opens: smooth expected base locus and rank-three Chern-obstruction map. The special three-section tuple witnesses only the latter.
3. Incidence H is smooth, integral and ample even over its exceptional projective fibres. Normalization, Segre contraction and strict henselization preserve the regular punctures and first neighbourhood; dimensions N=m+2r and the divisor model type-check.
4. The exact operator Phi has boundary Psi. On proper families weak Lefschetz applies to H^(2r-3), and the ambient pushforward is exactly 2a*zeta0+a^2*zeta1. Every term vanishes after tau and projection to H1(C): an a factor fills a missing K3 degree and has tau_i.H_i=0, or overshoots the curve degree. This proves integral constancy of the Picard family.
5. Only the projective exceptional section H uses Hilbert schemes. Every cycle on H_C is algebraically equivalent to a Kbar-defined cycle through universal flat families over chains of normalized proper curves. Therefore every boundary maps into Pic(C)(Kbar). This does not assume a finite-type cycle space for a strictly local puncture.
6. Chow localization and refined self-intersection define rho into Pic(C)(C)/Pic(C)(Kbar). Finite multiplication on the Jacobian makes the subgroup saturated, so the quotient is uniquely divisible.
7. The necessary degree-r Chern obstruction uses the exact exterior conormal sequence and the rational Chern-character isomorphism. Only the specified b0 Kunneth summand reaches the chosen target; the product-Hodge lemma puts its rational antecedent in the NS tensor. Hyperplane factors vanish, while the remaining tau tensor line is killed by the injective rank-three map. Rationality also kills the conjugate mixed component.
8. The exact source pushforward is tau*(b0+a*b1); both mixed components vanish, so rho kills every ordinary source-family difference. No sufficiency claim about deformation of cycles is needed.
9. The target map theta is defined through pullbacks of line bundles despite nonflat incidence fibres. Its composite with rho is (-9)^(r-1). A curve point transcendental over Kbar gives a nonzero uniquely divisible quotient class by the finite multiplication map and the Abel closed immersion.

# Root source and proof refinements after the first scheduled review

Date: 19 November 2026. AI-produced by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode). This record covers actual checks after the05:50UTC movement review. It is not a scheduled review and does not reset any checkpoint or review time.

## Exact correspondence-image gate

C.1n was checked using the full arbitrary-source and correspondence-degree quantifiers in manuscript14:46. A numerically trivial but homologically nonzero source cycle defines a nonzero cohomological functional to an ample target class while acting as zero on algebraic cycles. Thus the criterion implies standard D on all source varieties. The zero correspondence also forces the target Griffiths group to vanish. Conversely, under D on all products, realization of whole numerical motives is faithful; Jannsen's Theorem1 supplies an image projector and hence an algebraic preimage modulo numerical equivalence. Vanishing of the target Griffiths group upgrades it to algebraic equivalence. This is the exact fixed-target gate, with endpoint codimensions reducing to D alone. The proof does not assume algebraic Kunneth projectors. The same argument works for a fixed Weil cohomology over an algebraically closed base field. Root and frobenius independently checked the degree conventions and proof.

## Ordinary local degree one and the torsion comparison

C.3o gives the missing dimension-two injection counterexample and dimension-three surjection counterexample over C, using the strict local Fermat-quartic cone and its regular ambient space. Together with C.3m/n, the exact degree-one table is: N1 both maps are trivial isomorphisms; N2 injectivity fails and surjectivity is automatic; every N>=3 has injectivity and a counterexample to surjectivity. In positive characteristic C.3n gives a p-primary integral kernel and rational injectivity under its coefficient-field hypotheses.

For C.3p, root opened Elkik1973 at https://numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1258.pdf: SectionIII Theorem3 printedp569, the unnumbered Picard corollary printedp573, and Theorem5 with its corollary printedpp577–579. These concern noetherian henselian pairs without the separate principal-ideal hypothesis of the later Theorem7. Root also opened Stacks0EK5,0EK9 and02IM. Completion preserves the puncture and regularity here; the complete ring is f-adically complete because each ideal(f^n) is closed in its maximal-ideal topology. A maximal point of A_f is closed in the Jacobson puncture, hence has one-dimensional closure and regular local dimension N−1. Purity then applies. Kummer groups are Picard n-torsion because global units on the punctures and their finite-normalization components have n-th roots. This proves the invertible-order torsion isomorphism for N>=4, including mixed characteristic.

The second assertion of C.3p compares the local Picard functors on finite-type parameter algebras by their common completed henselian tensor pair. It is not an assertion that every henselian family is an actual line bundle on X times that parameter. The attempted norm argument does not close this gap: local henselian charts on a proper parameter need not descend to one global etale neighbourhood, and extra branches in an affine finite compactification can contribute uncontrolled classes to the norm.

C.3q closes the subgroup-lifting step in the essentially-finite-type characteristic-p scope. Local NS finite generation and the unipotent restriction kernel are the exact Kollár2016 inputs. Dense prime-to-p torsion forces the target abelian subgroup into the source connected image. A power of p kills its unipotent kernel as a group scheme, so multiplication descends through the quotient and supplies a proper abelian subgroup with a p-primary isogeny onto the target. Root checked that the normalization target is legitimate and that nonreduced kernels are covered. The actual-product-family step remains unproved in general.

Over Fpbar, ordinary algebraically trivial Chow classes on these excellent punctures are torsion in every codimension. The proof closes each horizontal curve-family component over a projective curve, pulls back a principal divisor killing the marked-point difference, and properly pushes it to the excellent base. No local cycle-functor representability is invoked. This is separately recorded in C.3o.

## Explicit higher local counterexamples

C.3r uses the odd-dimensional Fermat cubic V of dimension2r−1. Two explicitly resolved joins of a fixed elliptic Fermat cubic with complementary linear vertices have transverse intersection exactly E. Their composed correspondences equal the integral diagonal of E. Weak Lefschetz and Abel–Jacobi functoriality show that this elliptic family survives CH^r(V)/(HCH^(r−1)); the degree-three intersection removes the remaining ample scalar. C.3e transfers it to the strict-local cone divisor of a regular ambient local ring of dimension2r+1.

For the source Chow vanishing, the full primary Skalit2017 PDF https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.00303 was opened. Corollary6.4(i), authorp27, applies directly to C→R with R henselian and geometrically regular, and gives integral CH^r(R)=0 for r>0. Localization gives the same source vanishing on the puncture. The Quillen university PDF endpoint was searched and attempted both through the web tool and direct HTTP; the former failed and the latter returned503. Its indexed TheoremVII.5.11 was visible, but the proof instead uses the fully opened Skalit theorem. The bibliography explicitly marks this Quillen retrieval as not obtained.

The Fermat-motive literature search included 'Fermat cubic hypersurface odd dimension elliptic curve correspondence direct summand motive'. Fu–Vial's published author offprint at https://irma.math.unistra.fr/~lfu/articles/DistinguishedCycles.pdf was downloaded and opened; §5.3 and Proposition5.7 printedpp91–94 give the nearby Shioda–Katsura induction. The two join-correspondence identity used here is proved directly. No priority claim follows from that search. Exact download hashes, failed retrievals and runtimes are in code/fetch_fermat_boundary_sources_output.json.

C.3s gives an explicit normal Segre cone for every N>=2 and1<=r<N. Its projective base is P^(N−1−r) times a Fermat Calabi–Yau r-fold. The quotient by the Segre polarization eliminates the projective hyperplane variable and leaves CH^r(T)/(H^(m+1)CH^(r−m−1)(T)). The generic-point zero-cycle survives because it is not supported on a hyperplane; the differential-form diagonal obstruction proves this after the necessary finite extension and norm. Its actual algebraic family survives on the strict-local puncture. Thus all positive-codimension universal Chow/ordinary-J finiteness ranges are now settled: counterexamples for1<=r<N and zero groups forr>=N. This does not turn the ordinary algebraically trivial class into a nonzero class modulo algebraic equivalence.

C.3t uses explicit coordinate hyperplanes through that generic point and a fixed point. Their smooth base contains an explicit smooth plane Fermat curve, and the associated incidence divisor is smooth even above its base. The resulting actual family has coefficient-one pushforward to the surviving zero-cycle class. This proves nonzero ordinary Gysin and non-torsion open-restriction kernels throughout r+1<=N<=2r−1, including every odd critical dimension.

Scientific certificates and exact runtimes are retained in code/check_fermat_boundary_output.json (r1–7;0.051894s), code/check_segre_nonvanishing_output.json (66 pairs;0.167565s), and the contributed code/check_uniform_gysin output (45 pairs; approximately0.278s). These finite calculations check equations and ranks only. They do not claim to compute non-torsion, Chow groups, or the differential-form proof.

## Smooth-cone ordinary Picard theorem and supersingular splitting

Root independently checked the full typed proof of C.3u. ## Ordinary divisor quotient, including dimension two (C.3v)

The strict local cone on the fixed plane Fermat quartic has actual Picard group (Pic(C)/Z[K_C]) plus the additive group C. Properness of a compactified actual parameter curve forces its additive coordinate to be constant, so ordinary Pic_alg is exactly the Poincare image of Pic0(C). Therefore B1 is C plus Z/4 integrally, and B1 tensor Q is infinite-dimensional. Segre stabilization gives C^(N-1) plus Z for every N>=3. The explicit cocycle exp(radial contraction of dt/t) has residue-one additive coordinate; proper GAGA and Elkik's actual Picard bijection specify its unique descended class. This closes the smallest N2 ordinary divisor-finiteness case. It does not identify ordinary algebraic equivalence with the connected local Picard relation.

### Milne normalization checks

Theorem1.6 in both the supplied and current PDF prints S^(2d)(X×X,ell), while its proof uses the generalized eigenvalue-one space in H^(2d)(X×X,Qell(d)). With the source's definition of S^r, the hypothesis needed is **S^d(X×X,ell)**. The corrected claim has the following elementary proof. Over a splitting field, let V_a be a generalized a-eigenspace of Frobenius on H^r(X). Poincaré duality identifies the corresponding reciprocal block with V_a^*. Their tensor is End(V_a), and Frobenius acts by conjugation. If a nontrivial unipotent Jordan factor U occurred, conjugation by U would be a nontrivial unipotent operator on End(V_a): an operator commuting with every endomorphism is scalar, and a scalar unipotent operator is the identity. The hypothesis S^d makes that conjugation the identity, a contradiction. Hence every block is semisimple. We do not treat the printed top-degree hypothesis as an unconditional proof of general Frobenius semisimplicity.

The definition of equivalence of a p^(n1)-structure pi1 and a p^(n2)-structure pi2 on printedp4 uses pi1^(n1 N)=pi2^(n2 N). Base change requires the crossed powers **pi1^(n2 N)=pi2^(n1 N)**, as the preceding Frobenius discussion on printedp2 correctly states. For example, H²(P¹) over Fp has pi1=p; over Fp² it has pi2=p². The crossed equality holds, whereas the printed uncrossed equality would require p^N=p^(4N), impossible for N>0. No power-change argument here uses the uncrossed formula.

### Debarre–Ein–Lazarsfeld–Voisin version checks

The supplied early Corollary2.2 prints 2≤k≤n. Its preceding argument and the published Corollary2.2 have the correct range **2≤k≤n−2**. At k=n−1 and k=n the claimed strict cone difference does not occur. The early proof of Theorem2.1 also phrases a claim about nonnegative combinations of CM-lattice one-forms too strongly for the subsequent rank-one forms. The published proof correctly takes the **closure** of the convex cone of rank-one forms arising from CM-lattice directions. To check the step directly, the fraction field of the CM order is dense in C; approximate each coefficient of a one-form by elements of that field, clear their common denominators, and rescale the rank-one forms by the positive square of that denominator. Their limits give every rank-one positive form. Pullback of the origin along the corresponding map E^n→E supplies the algebraic divisor; closedness gives the asserted pseudoeffective limit. Thus the published result is supported without the faulty early finite-combination wording.

## Unavailable targets and searches

Griffiths (1969), On the periods of certain rational integrals II: the exact target remains **not obtained as a full text**.
