Bloch (1976), Some elementary theorems about algebraic cycles on abelian varieties: **full primary text now obtained and opened, superseding the earlier failed retrieval**. The initial exact-title/PDF searches and Springer PDF endpoint led only to the publisher's abstract and subscription landing page. By08:21UTC we had located the public GDZ volume METS, identified article LOG_0017, and downloaded https://gdz.sub.uni-goettingen.de/download/pdf/PPN356556735_0037/LOG_0017.pdf. The complete15-page PDF (cover plus printed215--228) has SHA256 b201e94c224afab09016ba7fb51115e11f28294e4762b698a89b69db20268c4b. The program fetch_bloch_gdz_article.py, its output and runtime9.861639478s are retained. All14 article pages were OCR-read, and original scans of printed216,219,221,223,224,226,227,228 were actually visually inspected. Theorem(0.1), p216, proves integral Pontryagin nilpotence over every algebraically closed field; its proof on p221 passes from Jacobians using Lemma(2.1). Lemma(1.3), p219, is divisibility of algebraically trivial cycles. Theorem(3.1), p223, separates characteristic-zero uncountable-field nonvanishing from the algebraic-closure-of-finite-field vanishing. Bloch expressly omits the proof of the Roitman-derived Lemma(3.2); we do not claim that the article independently proves that input. Lemma(3.3), p224, supplies the full alternating-form calculation. Proposition(4.6), pp226--227, and Theorem(4.7), pp227--228, concern the specified Pontryagin-generated subgroups, and do not settle the full ordinary algebraic-equivalence Lefschetz gate. The earlier direct differential-form proof remains in place. The separately opened Bloch1979 paper is a different source used for torsion and specialization.

Roitman1980, Manin1968 and the Peters2026 survey remain subject to the explicit failed retrievals in the cycles contributor's record. Lichtenbaum2009 is publication metadata plus the separately opened2005 full version, as recorded above. The original historical scans were not supplied or retrieved. Failure to retrieve a source is not a proof that a mathematical statement is false.

Searches for B.2–B.4 included all-weight Weil-number realization, Honda–Tate, norm-one divisor lattices and unit norms, and motivic realization versus fixed-CM-field factorization. Searches for C.3–C.4 included strict local Chow groups of cones, localization through blow-ups and refined Gysin, algebraic-equivalence localization, integral Poincaré curve classes, minimal classes, arbitrary polarizations in dimensions2 and3, and direct factors of Jacobians. Searches for D.1 and E.4 included rational Newton cocharacters, the character-Q protorus, real polarizations, local inertia support, Artin–Verdier real conventions, negative motivic twists and all finite coefficient comparisons. Exact current inputs and unsuccessful approaches are also retained in the corresponding contributor records.

## Post-checkpoint03 proof and primary-source audits, 2026-11-19

- C.3i–j: reopened Kollár (2016), *Maps between local Picard groups*, https://content.algebraicgeometry.nl/2016-4/2016-4-022.pdf, Theorem1.7(i) p463, §2.5 p466, Definition3.3 pp467–468, §3.5 p469, and Example4.2 pp469–470. The full local Picard identity component is not the subgroup defined by ordinary Chow algebraic equivalence. The latter is covered by images of proper Picard varieties after descending each family to one algebraic neighbourhood and extending its line bundle to a smooth projective compactification. Its intersection with a characteristic-zero unipotent restriction kernel is trivial. The explicit cone example still has kernel (C,+)^3 after quotienting by ordinary algebraic equivalence. The target section ring need not be generated in degree one; the argument uses the proper normal-cone contraction, not an unjustified maximal-ideal blow-up description.
- For that compactification step, opened Włodarczyk (2005), *Simple Hironaka resolution in characteristic zero*, https://www.math.purdue.edu/~wlodarcz/singularities/Resolution.pdf, Theorem1.0.2(b)–(c) p781 and Theorem1.0.3 p782. The resolution is projective and unchanged on the smooth open. A divisor on the product of two smooth compactifications is Cartier, giving the required extension of the line bundle.
- C.4r: reopened Scavia–Suzuki, arXiv2507.09821v2, Theorem1.2 and its following paragraph, together with equations(1.1)–(1.2). The direct-summand-to-Chern-character implication has no unimodular-polarization hypothesis. The crystalline cycle source is CH tensor Z_p, not merely an untracked coefficient extension. Pairing with integral complementary numerical cycles places the rational target in every local numerical lattice, which is sufficient for integral membership without numerical=homological or Tate. The sufficiency direction uses numerical Matsusaka and the already proved integers2 or6.
- C.4s: opened Fu–Li (2021), *Supersingular irreducible symplectic varieties*, https://irma.math.unistra.fr/~lfu/articles/Supersingular%20ISV.pdf, Proposition6.1 p184 and Lemma6.2 p185. The classification explicitly includes characteristic2. The lattice contains a class of square2; one sign is ample on an abelian surface, hence every supersingular surface has a principal polarization. This is a known positive subcase, not a decision of the residual nonsupersingular surface problem.
- E.3m: rechecked the finite-family covering of the Walker Jacobian, the finite kernel after quotienting the connected kernel, and the comparison of all family images through algebraic quotient maps. Once P is the point group of an abelian variety isogenous to the Walker Jacobian, its remaining kernel is finite and divisible by ACMV Theorem2.3, hence zero. The optional regularity proof is separate from this argument and uses division of an algebraic homomorphism only after it kills the full m-torsion subgroup scheme.
- E.3n: opened Javanpeykar–Mathur (2022), *Smooth hypersurfaces in abelian varieties over arithmetic rings*, full publisher article, Theorem2.2, for the relative Albanese torsor and base change; a marked section supplies the group structure. Opened Stacks Lemma43.9.1, Tag0AZI, for rational-equivalence witnesses in X×P1. Opened Schoen (1993), *On Hodge structures and non-representability of Chow groups*, https://www.numdam.org/item/CM_1993__88_3_285_0.pdf, Lemma4.4 and proof pp311–313: countably many Hilbert–Chow relation spaces and the Baire argument are explicit. The extra relative Albanese-zero conditions used here are proved separately; they are not attributed to Schoen's lemma. Moving zero-cycles uses representatives supported in an open, not a covariant Chow pushforward for the open immersion. The final fixed exponent follows from a dense constructible subset inside a subgroup, and divisibility kills that bounded image.
- C.3l: searched for Beauville decomposition, negative degrees modulo algebraic equivalence, and algebraically trivial degree-zero pieces. Opened Beauville (2004), *Algebraic cycles on Jacobian varieties*, https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/u/beauvill/pubs/cycjams.pdf, §2.2 p684, and reopened Beauville (2010), Equation(5.9) and Proposition5.11 p262. The latter is the exact ordinary vanishing conjecture; the former explicitly distinguishes the algebraic-equivalence quotient. We prove the additional universal equivalence by a relative projector and a Poincaré divisor: a class of J^r_s is evaluated from a class of index s−1 on a product with a parameter Jacobian. No priority is claimed, and no fixed-dimension consequence beyond C.3h is inferred.
- C.3k: opened Varbaro (2009), the published author copy at https://www.matteovarbaro.com/Connectedness%20properties%20of%20Groebner%20deformations.pdf, Theorem1.6 p2497 and Corollary1.12 pp2498–2499. Opened Stacks Lemma52.14.1 (0ECR) and Lemma15.53.6 (0C23). A complete normal local domain of dimensionN has connected punctured principal divisor for N≥3, since the local-cohomological dimension of a principal ideal is at most1. The finite normalization of the divisor gives the required global nth roots of units; this is necessary to conclude that the Kummer cover restricts trivially. Completion is used only after proving the finite covering normalization is an excellent normal local domain.
- For the dimension-three closed-kernel step, opened Boutot (1978), the publisher's frontmatter PDF https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/bfm%3A978-3-540-35936-4/1, Introduction Theorem1(i)–(ii), printedpVII/PDFp5, and inspected its screenshot. The depth inequality is at least2, and the unit section is a finitely presented closed immersion. Full ChapterII was not obtained. The pullback is along a morphism of local Picard functors from the abelian subgroup; no universal line bundle on that subgroup is assumed. This closed subgroup, if nontrivial in characteristic zero, has nontrivial torsion, which the independently proved Kummer/connectedness argument excludes.
- C.3l proof refinement: the final version forms the Poincaré-composed cycle first and applies ordinary Beauville projectors on the abelian product. The first-factor eigenvalue is1 and the total eigenvalue determines the second-factor grading. Thus no separate relative Chow–Künneth theorem on C×A is needed. Cancelling first-factor pullback uses only its finite-flat injectivity over Q. This supersedes the earlier relative-projector phrasing without changing the result.

### Record: agents/cycles/all_degree_local_REPORT.md

# C.3af: ordinary local restriction in every codimension

Finalized, independently passed by root and frobenius, and integrated after C.3ae. For every r≥2 and N≥4^(r−1)+2r−1, the specified product-K3 construction gives an integral ordinary restriction cokernel surjecting onto Pic⁰(C)(C), where C is the Fermat plane quartic. Hence the ordinary rational J^r map is not surjective in this entire range. The first thresholds are N=7 for r=2, N=21 for r=3, and N=71 for r=4.

The proof retains a simple full-basis construction using 3·4^(r−1) sections as an independently checked first stage. The sharper construction uses 4^(r−1) sections whose coefficients are exact products of the four integer matrices in C.3ae. There is no generic section choice or unspecified degree.

The reduction in section count has two separate proofs. For base point freeness, every pair of columns of the original4×3 matrix B(w) is independent at every point of the Fermat K3 surface. Twelve exact finite-field chart ideals and properness of the projective determinantal locus prove this in characteristic zero. Three pure tensors with pairwise independent first and remaining factors are linearly independent. The values of the new sections are the linear combination of these three tensors with coefficients z0,z1,z2, so they cannot vanish simultaneously.

For the deformation obstruction, the actual correlated-coefficient map is the tensor power of the original injective57→64 map, precomposed with the linear diagonal on the three-dimensional curve factor, e_k↦e_k^tensor-s. This is injective for every s=r−1. It does not replace the fixed curve by a product of curves. All extra Kunneth components are excluded by their separate cohomology and differential degrees. Each exceptional boundary correction inserts a K3 hyperplane class killed by its test class, or a curve canonical class with the wrong cohomology degree. Thus the same actual proper-family detector is zero on the ordinary source image.

The target product of the displayed lines with a degree-zero divisor on C maps by multiplication by (−9)^(r−1). This is surjective on Pic⁰(C)(C); a geometric generic point difference gives a specified nontorsion witness. The proof applies integrally to ordinary algebraic-equivalence families and also after tensoring with Q. It does not assert that all cycles on the source are killed, does not settle intermediate dimensions, and gives no characteristic-p extension.

The all-codimension proof is a tensor argument, not an extrapolation from a finite list. The finite coefficient and determinantal checks have reproducible scripts, outputs and runtimes. The column-pair calculation took0.32555199800117407s; frobenius independently repeated it in lex order in0.190871s. The clean standalone typesetting build took3.1218027389986673s. Root and frobenius read the full typed proof and its compressed step.

Files: `all_degree_local_surjectivity.tex`, `all_degree_local_results.csv`, `all_degree_local_CHECK_EVENTS.tsv`; the primary references are shared in `higher_local_references.bib`. The full-basis-only historical draft is retained as `output/C3af_full_basis_draft.txt`. No novelty claim is made.

### Record: agents/cycles/dimension_six_REPORT.md

# C.3ag: dimension-six ordinary restriction

Complete proof and independent audits passed. The result gives an integral cokernel surjection to Pic0(C)(C), where C is the Fermat plane quartic. The proof uses a precisely specified very-general quartic surface through a fixed line; Hodge genericity of the complex point is part of the data, separately from the geometric genericity of the three sections. It does not assert the required Hodge group for arbitrary Q-generic embeddings or a selected arithmetic specialization.

The hypersurface incidence model has48 transverse base points. Its non-projective-bundle fibres are handled by deletion in codimension2 for Picard groups. The invariant uses h−2a; its localization ambiguity dies integrally after pairing with4D−H. Exact arithmetic is in code/check_dimension_six_incidence.py and its JSON output, runtime0.046509863s. This calculation does not establish a Hodge-group claim by experiment.

All source-family cycles live on the fixed strict local scheme times a proper parameter curve. The first conormal/Chern obstruction, the rank-three open and the maximal level-one Hodge lemma force the resulting Picard family to be constant. The target product family maps by−9. This yields the integral cokernel and a specified nontorsion Abel-point witness.

The standalone PDF had five overflow diagnostics across two preliminary builds, the largest76.2237pt; all were repaired. The final four-pass standalone build has no overfull or undefined-citation diagnostics and took3.051295767s. The master PDF still receives its own visual/layout review before delivery.

This narrows the ordinary J2 surjectivity gate from N4/N6 to N4. C.3ah, if separately certified, supplies the latter from the earlier Calabi-Yau cone and regular-source vanishing. No assertion about arbitrary CH2 source images or other codimension tables is inferred.

Recorded: 2026-11-19T09:19:05.417041+00:00

### Record: agents/cycles/higher_local_REPORT.md

# C.3ae: ordinary codimension-two local restriction

The proof is finalized and independently passed by root and frobenius. It has been integrated immediately after C.3ad. There is no novelty claim.

For every N≥7, the displayed four integer coefficient matrices specify an excellent normal strictly henselian local complex ring R of dimension N, with regular puncture X and regular punctured principal divisor Y. The ordinary integral restriction cokernel CH²(X)_alg→CH²(Y)_alg surjects onto Pic⁰(C)(C), where C is the Fermat plane quartic. Thus the ordinary rational J² restriction map is not surjective. The explicit target cycle is the pullback of a displayed line on the Fermat K3 surface times the divisor of a geometric generic point minus a displayed algebraic point on C.

The cycle invariant first lifts a class from the puncture to the regular proper divisor model and restricts it to the exceptional projective bundle. Its ambiguity is multiplication by the normal-bundle class. In codimension two, the projective-bundle relation has degree at least three, so quotienting by this normal class is precisely evaluation h=−a. Intersection with τ=4D−H on the K3 surface and pushforward then gives an integral Pic(C) invariant. The target family maps by multiplication by −9.

For a source ordinary family, its fixed proper parameter curve permits closure on the fixed regular model. The rational Chern-character isomorphism lifts that closure class to a virtual vector bundle. The rank-one conormal exterior sequence forces contraction of its degree-two Hodge class with the first-neighbourhood deformation class to vanish. The exact quartic Jacobian-ring calculation makes the primitive part of the relevant mixed tensor vanish. Boundary corrections contribute only the hyperplane factor or constant classes, which the detector removes. The resulting Picard-family morphism is constant. This is why the invariant kills every ordinary source family difference, integrally, despite using rational Chern characters in the Hodge argument.

The two finite certificates use the same fixed coefficient tuple. The 64×57 matrix has rank 57 modulo 101, and its first 57 rows have determinant 47 modulo 101. All twelve projective charts of the common-zero scheme of the four sections have unit ideal modulo 101. Projectivity over Z proves base point freeness in characteristic zero. Root and frobenius independently reproduced these calculations with different algorithms. The complete group-theoretic and deformation argument is in `higher_local_surjectivity.tex`; the finite computations alone are not the proof of the Chow statement.

The unrestricted-CH spread route was rejected before integration. A cycle on a strict local puncture can descend to a pointed etale neighbourhood varying with the parameter, and a class on an open first neighbourhood need not extend over the compactified parameter. The proof makes no such extension assumption: it starts from an actual ordinary family on the fixed product and closes it before applying the obstruction. This distinction is retained in `higher_local_CHECK_EVENTS.tsv`.

The exact remaining universal ordinary J² restriction-surjectivity dimensions over C are N=4 and N=6. N≤3 has no codimension-two target cycles; N=5 was already refuted in C.3r; N≥7 is refuted here. No assertion for arbitrary CH² preimages, characteristic p, or other codimensions follows without an additional proof.

Artifacts: `higher_local_results.csv`, `higher_local_CHECK_EVENTS.tsv`, `higher_local_references.bib`; code and JSON/stdout under `code/` and `output/`. The clean standalone PDF has six pages. Full integrated rendering is performed by root.

The literature search used the queries “local Lefschetz theorem algebraically trivial higher Chow groups restriction hypersurface infinitesimal obstruction” and “local Picard higher codimension Chow algebraic equivalence restriction isolated singularity counterexample.” We opened the complete primary author PDFs of Patel–Ravindra, *Weak Lefschetz for Chow Groups: Infinitesimal Lifting*, Theorem1.4 on authorp3, and *An Infinitesimal Noether–Lefschetz Theorem for Chow Groups*, Theorem7 on authorp2. Their precise setups concern smooth projective ambient varieties, smooth ample divisors and continuous K-cohomology of formal neighbourhoods. The present theorem concerns actual ordinary algebraic equivalence on punctured singular local models. These source checks are comparative; their theorems are not inputs to the counterexample, and no novelty claim is made.

### Record: agents/cycles/local_surjectivity_REPORT.md

# Complete ordinary local surjectivity classification over C

C.3ak in local_surjectivity_classification.tex proves the exact universal range:

\[
J^r(X)\longrightarrow J^r(Y)\text{ is always surjective}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad N\le r+1
\qquad(r\ge1).
\]

The quantified objects are excellent normal strictly henselian local complex rings of dimension \(N\), residue field \(\mathbb C\), with regular puncture and regular punctured principal divisor. The equivalence relation is ordinary algebraic equivalence defined by actual fixed-product families. The classification is valid for the integral ordinary groups as well as their rationalizations.

For \(N\le r+1\), the target has no codimension-\(r\) points. Every other dimension has a specified counterexample: C.3ai covers \(r+2\le N\le2r+1\); C.3m covers \(r=1,N\ge4\); C.3aj covers \(r\ge2,N\ge2r+2\). These three rows exhaust the complementary range. Every example has a nontorsion ordinary cokernel witness.

The root read and passed the full corollary, including the target-zero boundary \(N=1\), integral versus rational coefficients, and the exhaustive quantifiers. Its standalone build is clean and took **0.9312339300013264 seconds**. The separate result register has one row; the check ledger has four actual events.

This closes the universal complex ordinary local **surjectivity** dimension question. Higher-codimension restriction injectivity, the exact groups for an individual variety, the abelian Beauville injectivity problem, and higher-degree positive-characteristic restriction retain their separate statuses. The remaining-scope paragraph in cycles.tex has been updated accordingly.

# C.3ah: regular ambient Calabi–Yau counterexamples

The complete typed proof is `regular_ambient_calabi_yau.tex`. For every (r\ge1), it specifies a regular strictly henselian complex local ring of dimension (N=r+2), namely the strict henselization of affine space at the origin, and the Fermat equation of degree (r+2). The punctured divisor is the strict-local cone on the Fermat Calabi–Yau (r)-fold. Its specified geometric generic point minus a specified algebraic point survives the hyperplane quotient rationally, by the diagonal-support argument already proved in C.3s. C.3e transfers it to a non-torsion ordinary algebraically trivial target class. The source Chow group vanishes integrally by Skalit, Corollary 6.4(i), author p. 27, freshly reopened in full.

This closes the previously unlisted dimension-four case for (J^2). Together with C.3r at dimension five, the independently audited C.3ag at dimension six, and C.3ae in every dimension at least seven, ordinary rational (J^2) restriction is universally surjective over (mathbb C) exactly in dimensions (N\le3). In those dimensions the target group is zero. Higher degrees still require their separately stated dimension ranges.

The root read the entire typed proof and authorized integration. `/root/real_weil` independently checked the final typed proof and combined dimension table, passing at 09:18:25 UTC after freshly opening Skalit Corollary 6.4(i) and its proof. No new external theorem beyond the already opened source is imported; the existing canonical bibliography key is `rootSkalit2017`.

The exact finite certificate `code/check_regular_ambient_calabi_yau.py` checks twelve dimensions, all gradients, the point equations, the hyperplane support, and adjunction. The standalone four-pass build is `output/C3ah_check.pdf`. Its first pass exposed a routine alignment typo; the repaired build has no undefined citations, errors or overfull boxes. ### Record: agents/cycles/suspended_calabi_yau_REPORT.md

# C.3ai: every low-dimensional ordinary restriction range

The full proof in `suspended_calabi_yau.tex` covers every pair (r\ge1), (r+2\le N\le2r+1). Set (d=2r-N+2\ge1) and (k=N-r-2\ge0). A Fermat Calabi–Yau (d)-fold is embedded in a Fermat hypersurface of dimension (d+2k) by adding (k) paired coordinate sets. Two distinct roots of (-1) define joins with complementary vertices. Their incidence resolutions give integral Chow correspondences with composite exactly the diagonal of the Calabi–Yau base.

The specified geometric generic point minus a specified algebraic point yields an ordinary algebraically trivial class. A hypothetical hyperplane relation spreads and norms to a correspondence equation. The proof retains both the constant correspondence and the proper-parameter-support correction explicitly. They kill the holomorphic top form. The remaining correspondence has codimension (r-1), so it sends that form into Hodge type ((d+k-1,k-1)), in degree two below the middle of the Fermat hypersurface. This is zero by weak Lefschetz, or by a negative Hodge index at (k=0). The transverse left inverse contradicts vanishing of the original incidence action. This proves the required non-torsion in the hyperplane quotient.

The regular ambient strict local ring and its principal Fermat equation are specified. Source Chow vanishing is integral by the freshly opened Skalit Corollary 6.4(i), author p. 27. The target survives by C.3e. The curve-family and incidence constructions preserve the fixed-product definition of ordinary algebraic equivalence.

The root and `/root/frobenius` both read and passed the complete typed proof. Frobenius independently reopened Skalit Corollary 6.4(i) and checked its precise hypotheses. The endpoints recover C.3ah and C.3r, but the entire interval is proved, not inferred from endpoint examples. No high-dimensional assertion is included in this fragment.

The exact certificate `code/check_suspended_calabi_yau.py` retains 210 range cases, twenty Fermat degrees, pair equations, complementary tangent determinants, cycle codimensions and Hodge shifts. Runtime: **0.10279798600095091 seconds**. Complete JSON and stdout are in `output/`. The finite checks do not establish the non-torsion result by extrapolation.

The standalone four-pass build in `output/C3ai_check.pdf` is clean after repairing prose and filename overflows. Runtime: **2.953216986999905 seconds**. All earlier and final logs are retained by `code/compile_suspended_calabi_yau.py`.

### Record: agents/cycles/C3ag_dimension_six_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3ag

**Outcome: PASS.** We independently read the entire root-owned `dimension_six.tex` and the auxiliary `agents/frobenius/C3_N6_Hodge_inputs.tex`. The completed source read was recorded at 2026-11-19 09:15:37 UTC. This is an independent audit by `/root/cycles`, not the author's own check.

## Geometry and integral invariant

The three generic sections on the threefold (W\times C) have 48 transverse common zeroes. The incidence hypersurface in \(\mathbf P^2\times W\times C\) is smooth: the section differentials are independent at every base point, and outside the base points it is a projective line bundle. Its exceptional projective planes have codimension two. Deleting them, and deleting the corresponding codimension-three subset downstairs, preserves Picard groups; this justifies the integral divisor decomposition used in the proof without claiming a projective-bundle formula for the whole incidence variety.

We independently recomputed the identities (p_*1=0, p_*h=1, p_*h^2=a, p_*h^3=0). Multiplication by (h-2a) sends the full localization ambiguity ((h+a)(\gamma_0+h\gamma_1)), after pushforward, to (-3a^2\gamma_1). Pairing with (\tau=4D-H) kills that expression integrally. The source expression is exactly (b_0-ab_1-2a^2b_2). The correspondence with an arbitrary codimension-two class is defined on smooth varieties by intersection theory; no flatness of the incidence projection is needed. In the target family only divisor pullbacks occur.

## Hodge input and genericity

The chosen quartic is explicitly Hodge-generic among smooth quartics containing the fixed line. We checked that this is a choice of a complex point, not the unsupported assertion that every embedding of an abstract coefficient field is Hodge-generic. The lattice generated by the hyperplane and the line is nondegenerate. Local Torelli and the simultaneous line-bundle period conditions give the full 18-dimensional period domain. Each nonscalar rational endomorphism imposes a proper eigenline condition. Countable avoidance, together with the cited Picard theorem, supplies the stipulated point.

The Hodge-group argument is correct: the derived group supplies (mathrm{SO}(T)\times1), and projection onto the CM torus supplies the full direct product. Over (mathbf Q(i)), the two character twists of the standard orthogonal representation are absolutely irreducible and conjugate. Hence (T\otimes H^1(E)) is simple over (mathbf Q), has a nonzero ((3,0))-part, and contributes no level-one summand. The argument consequently controls every source parameter curve, not merely a fixed family.

We checked the quartic Jacobian-ring duality and the degree-three to degree-four multiplication. Its joint image is all degree four, so the common kernel is exactly the hyperplane line. The class (	au) has nonzero primitive image and square (-36). The three-column rank condition is nonempty, witnessed by (s_i=wz_i), and is defined over the chosen countable coefficient field. Its simultaneous intersection with the transverse-base-locus open is therefore legitimate for the 36 algebraically independent coefficients.

## Actual ordinary families

The rational Chern-character lift and rank-one conormal wedge obstruction are those already checked in C.3ae. The selected Künneth component receives only the stated primitive divisor factor. The Hodge lemma reduces it to a one-dimensional primitive Néron–Severi factor, and the rank-three map kills it. The (ab_1) correction gives a line bundle constant in the parameter, while the (a^2b_2) correction vanishes after pairing. Fixed proper-curve families therefore map to constant Picard families. This proves the asserted vanishing on ordinary algebraic-equivalence differences without invoking the withdrawn varying-étale spread argument.

Finally, the target divisor construction has composite (-9\,mathrm{id}) with the Picard invariant. Divisibility gives the integral cokernel surjection. The specified generic Abel point is nontorsion. The example has dimension six throughout.

## Primary sources actually opened or read in this audit

- Brevik–Nollet, author version arXiv:0806.1243v3: Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.3(c), author p. 2, including the line hypotheses. Full primary PDF opened.
- Huybrechts, *Lectures on K3 Surfaces*, opened author version: Proposition 6.2.8 and its full local-Torelli proof, printed pp. 107–108, and Section 6.2.4 on line bundles and period hyperplanes. The local primary PDF/text was read directly.
- Cattani–Deligne–Kaplan: Theorem 1.1 and Corollaries 1.2–1.3, author pp. 1–2, in the full opened primary PDF. The explicit lack of a field-of-definition conclusion was checked and respected.
- Zarhin, original 1983 article: the rendered printed p. 211, Theorem 2.2.1 and Example 2.2.3, was independently inspected. We do not claim that this audit independently reproduces Zarhin's full proof.
- Beauville, published 2014 PDF: Lemma 2 and Example 3, printed pp. 106–107, supply the Fermat quartic Jacobian's CM elliptic decomposition. The exact Example 3 and its application were read.

No new conjectural input or hidden genericity assumption was found. No shared proof file was edited by this audit.

### Record: agents/cycles/C3aj_high_range_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent full audit of C.3aj

**Outcome: PASS.** We read the entire high-range draft (now finalized as agents/real_weil/C3_high_range_surjectivity.tex) and agents/frobenius/C3_product_Hodge_inputs.tex. The audit concerns every \(r\ge2\) and \(N\ge2r+2\), ordinary algebraic equivalence by actual fixed-product families, and the stated countable-field Picard quotient. The final source checks were completed on 2026-11-19, after 09:25 UTC.

## Incidence geometry and the countable-field invariant

We checked the dimension conventions \(s=r-1\), \(m=N-2r\ge2\). Generic \(m+1\) sections of the very ample bundle have a smooth transverse base locus of the expected codimension, or an empty one. At a base point their independent differentials make the incidence hypersurface smooth for every projective \(x\). The exceptional locus has codimension \(m\ge2\); the projective-bundle open is dense and integral. The deformed-cone construction, finite normalization, unique point over the vertex, ideal equality, regular model and transverse principal section are the constructions already audited in C.3ae, now with the stated product of surfaces. We found no additional component or hidden shrinking choice.

The integral operator is well defined on cycles on the incidence hypersurface before passage to the quotient. Its exact pushforwards are \(p_*h^{m-1}=1\), \(p_*h^m=a\), and \(p_*h^{m+j}=0\) for \(j\ge1\). For a boundary family of codimension \(r-1\), weak Lefschetz lifts only the needed cohomology component, not the Chow class. This is sufficient: the boundary's mixed image is exactly

\[
\operatorname{pr}_{C,*}\bigl(\tau(2a\zeta_0+a^2\zeta_1)\bigr).
\]

We rederived the missing-factor argument. The odd degree lies on \(C\). Each K3 factor must contribute total cohomological degree four after multiplication by its \(\tau_\nu\). A degree-four input already vanishes; a degree-zero input must receive an \(H_\nu\) from the indicated power of \(a\), which gives \(\tau_\nu H_\nu=0\) after integration. There is at least one such missing input in both terms. A \(K_C\) insertion kills the curve's degree-one component. Thus the boundary map kills every mixed class, not only algebraic classes. The resulting Picard family has zero differential and is constant, integrally.

The field argument is consequently legitimate. It is applied only to cycles on the smooth projective exceptional section \(H\). A Hilbert component containing a complex point is the base change of a component over the algebraically closed countable field; a point over that field exists. A chain of proper curves, normalized, and the universal flat family show ordinary algebraic equivalence to a cycle over that field. The boundary's image is therefore contained in \(\operatorname{Pic}(C)(\overline K)\). No finite-type parameterization of cycles on the strict-local puncture is assumed.

Localization and self-intersection then define \(\rho\) on all target Chow classes modulo the stated Picard subgroup. Its compatibility with parameter fibres follows by using the same closure on the fixed proper model times the parameter. The quotient is uniquely divisible: division is inherited from complex Jacobian points, and a point whose nonzero multiple is defined over the algebraically closed \(\overline K\) is itself defined there, since multiplication is finite.

## Product-Hodge input

The independently varying period domains supply the jointly Hodge-generic tuple with rank-two Néron–Severi groups, scalar transcendental endomorphisms and no pairwise transcendental Hodge isomorphisms. The chosen complex embedding is explicitly part of the data.

We verified the subdirect Lie-algebra argument in full. A proper subdirect semisimple Lie algebra would identify two \(\mathfrak{so}_{20}\) factors. In type \(D_{10}\), every diagram automorphism fixes the standard-vector highest weight. The representations would therefore have a nonzero intertwiner after scalar extension; the intertwiner equations are rational, so one exists over \(\mathbf Q\). Connectedness turns it into a Hodge isomorphism, contradicting the chosen tuple. Thus the Hodge group is the full product. The CM factor is independent because the derived group kills a torus and surjects onto the semisimple product.

For each nonempty transcendental support \(I\), the corresponding tensor representation with the CM elliptic \(H^1\) is rationally simple, after taking account of the two conjugate torus characters. The Néron–Severi factors and the three elliptic copies produce multiplicities. Every such constituent has level \(2|I|+1\ge3\). Hence the exact selected Künneth tensor has the asserted level-one part, uniformly for every parameter curve. This does not require the parameter curve to descend to \(\overline K\).

## Chern obstruction, source families and target witness

The three-section rank condition is nonempty: choose one linear form on each quartic whose multiplication does not kill its primitive \(\tau_\nu\), then use their product times the three curve coordinates. The generic section tuple satisfies this rank open and the independent geometric open simultaneously. The class and minors are defined algebraically over the countable coefficient field.

We checked the degree-\(r\) conormal exterior sequence, the rational Chern-character lift and the selected Künneth projection against the already audited C.3ae/C.3af arguments. Each factor's cohomological and differential degree is preserved, and the contraction acts only on the curve. The product-Hodge lemma reduces the relevant algebraic component to the Néron–Severi tensor, whose sole fully primitive line is killed by the rank-three obstruction. The source image is exactly \(\operatorname{pr}_{C,*}(\tau(b_0+ab_1))\); the \(ab_1\) term vanishes on the mixed component by the same missing-factor argument. Thus the image of every actual ordinary source-family difference is zero.

The target divisor construction uses legitimate line-bundle pullbacks even when the incidence projection is not flat. Its composite with \(\rho\) is \((-9)^s\) times the quotient map. Divisibility gives the integral cokernel surjection. The specified curve point with coordinate transcendental over \(\overline K\) has no nonzero multiple in \(\operatorname{Pic}^0(C)(\overline K)\); finite multiplication and the Abel closed immersion prove this. The target witness survives rationally.

## Exact primary source checks

The five K3/CM sources were independently read for C.3ag and are retained in that review: Brevik–Nollet, Huybrechts, Cattani–Deligne–Kaplan, Zarhin and Beauville, with the same exact locators and scope limitations.

For this audit we freshly opened the complete Milne *Reductive Groups*, version 2.00, 10 March 2018, and read 18.26–18.27 (pp. 91–92), 20.3–20.4 (pp. 102–103), 20.33 (p. 108) and 21.4 (p. 112). These are the precise root, highest-weight, integration and outer-automorphism inputs used above.

We also freshly opened Grothendieck's Bourbaki Exposé 221, the complete 29-page primary PDF, and read Theorems 3.1–3.2 (printed pp. 259–260), the projectivity argument (pp. 264–265), and the Hilbert specialization/universal-flat-family description (pp. 265–266). The separate 1962 erratum, printed p. 302, was opened and read. Its corrections do not remove or weaken the projective fixed-polynomial Hilbert scheme used here. The draft's field argument relies on these actual projective Hilbert schemes, not on an unsupported assertion about strict-local parameter spaces.

No change to a shared proof file was made by this audit. The result is ready for final owner formatting and integration.

### Record: agents/cycles/C3z_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of C.3z, including the repaired final transfer

We read the complete root-owned file `research/sections/C3_completion_algebraization.tex`, including its revised step 5. The complete statement passes within its specified excellent henselian normal local coefficient-field scope, with a regular puncture. It makes no mixed-characteristic extension.

## The scope correction

Our initial audit established the algebraization, actual regular map, regular proper alteration, and point-realization steps. Its unqualified endorsement of the final appeal to C.3q did not freshly check C.3q's algebraizability hypotheses for local Neron--Severi finite generation and the restriction kernel. When root identified that scope issue, we explicitly withdrew the final-transfer endorsement while retaining the checked core. Root supplied a replacement proof. We audited the replacement separately; it does not assume that the divisor equation descends to the algebraic model.

## Core algebraization and actual map

Excellence makes the completion map regular, preserves normality, and preserves the regular puncture because the closed fibre consists of the single residue-field point. Over the perfect coefficient field, the full formal Jacobian criterion supplies exactly the formal smoothness hypothesis of Artin (1969), Theorem 3.8, printed p.32. The resulting algebraic model has the prescribed completion and is normal by faithful flat descent. Approximation of its finitely many coordinate equations modulo the square of the maximal ideal uses the general henselian G-ring theorem, Stacks Tag07QY. The approximated coordinates generate the cotangent space, so the induced complete-ring map is surjective. Equal finite dimensions and the domain property make its prime kernel zero. Faithfully flat descent along the completion, Tag07NT, proves that the actual map from the algebraic local model is regular. Base-changing de Jong's proper regular alteration by that regular map produces a regular proper generically finite source; a component dominating the base is surjective.

## Repaired step 5

The comparison with the completion is a natural comparison of represented local Picard functors, not merely of their point groups. For every finite-type parameter algebra, the henselized tensor pairs have the same ideal-adic completion. Elkik's Picard corollary identifies the Picard groups of their ideal complements naturally. Sheafification and Yoneda identify the represented group schemes. Thus the finite generation of the local Neron--Severi group of the normal algebraic model transfers to the given excellent henselian germ.

For a target abelian subgroup, a prime-to-characteristic prime avoiding the component torsion gives lifts of all its primary torsion in the reduced identity component of the source. Their density and the closed image give a smooth connected source group mapping faithfully flat onto that abelian subgroup.

The scheme-theoretic kernel is treated correctly. Its reduced identity component has neither a torus nor an abelian quotient, since either would supply prime-to-characteristic torsion; it is therefore unipotent. The finite component group is a p-group. A p-power kills the reduced kernel. The whole kernel is an affine nilpotent thickening of that reduced affine group. A faithful finite-dimensional representation exists for this possibly nonreduced affine group. The universal matrix of the preceding p-power differs from the identity by a matrix with nilpotent entries; one further p-power kills it. This proves a scheme-theoretic p-power annihilator without excluding infinitesimal multiplicative groups such as mu_p. Fppf descent of multiplication then produces a proper abelian subgroup mapping to the target by a p-primary isogeny. The separately proved actual-family point realization supplies the desired ordinary algebraically trivial preimages.

## Exact primary sources checked

- Artin (1969), Theorem3.8, printed p.32, plus its proof and Theorem3.9: https://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1969__36__23_0.pdf . Full PDF and theorem page image opened and read.
- Hauser--Woblistin (2021), Proposition2.5 and the definition of the full formal power-series ring in Section2: https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2021.73 . Published primary HTML opened and exact statement read.
- Stacks, Theorem16.13.1 Tag07QY; Lemma15.42.7 Tag07NT; Lemma37.2.3 Tag06AD. Each primary page and proof opened.
- Elkik (1973), SectionIII, Theorem3 p.569 and the unnumbered Picard corollary p.573: local full primary text re-read; https://numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1258.pdf . This applies to noetherian henselian pairs and their ideal complements.
- Kollar (2016), Lemma2.6 and proof p.466, and Definition3.4 and its following paragraph p.468: local full published text re-read; https://content.algebraicgeometry.nl/2016-4/2016-4-022.pdf . The latter explicitly states finite generation for normal algebraic local schemes, including positive characteristic.
- Milne (2022), Theorem4.9 and Corollary4.10, printed p.87: https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/iAG2022.pdf . Full primary PDF opened; exact theorem and proof state faithful finite-dimensional regular subrepresentation and closed immersion for every affine algebraic group, without a reducedness hypothesis.

Final full revised-proof pass communicated to root after the tool clock2026-11-19T07:46:19Z, before integration-ready message2026-11-19T07:47:14Z.

### Record: agents/real_weil/C3_completion_algebraization_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent review of root C.3z

Reviewer: `/root/real_weil`, 19 November 2026; final full review at 07:44 UTC.
Reviewed file: `research/sections/C3_completion_algebraization.tex`.
Outcome: **passes after the explicit final-step scope repair recorded below**.

## Exact proof checks

The completion of the excellent normal local domain remains normal and
has regular puncture. A nonmaximal completed prime cannot contract to the
maximal ideal, because the closed fibre of completion is the residue
field. Regularity ascends along the regular completion map. The given
coefficient field and finitely many cotangent generators supply the
power-series presentation. The perfect-field formal Jacobian criterion
supplies the hypothesis of Artin's isolated-singularity algebraization.

Approximation of the algebraic model's finite equations modulo the
square of the maximal ideal gives an actual map to the original
henselian ring. Its completed map is surjective by the cotangent-space
criterion and successive polynomial approximation. Since both complete
domains have the same finite dimension, its prime kernel is zero. Thus
the actual map, and not merely a chosen jet, induces an isomorphism of
completions. Faithfully flat descent of regular maps proves that the
model map is regular. Base changing a proper regular alteration and
selecting a connected component meeting the generic fibre gives exactly
the proper regular integral alteration required in C.3w.

Every subsequent step of C.3w only needs the excellent henselian
coefficient-field ring, that alteration, proper formal existence, and
general henselian G-ring approximation. The formal-unit gerbe,
whole-family approximation, proper-curve rigidity and codimension-one
finite-flat norm arguments retain their hypotheses. No universal actual
family on a whole abelian local-Picard subgroup is inferred.

The first version's final invocation of C.3q needed a scope repair:
C.3q explicitly assumed an essentially-finite-type model for its local
Neron-Severi and full-kernel inputs. I flagged this, and then read the
entire revised step 5. The repair is correct. On every finite-type
coefficient algebra D, the henselized tensor pairs for R and its
completion have canonically identical completions
`lim_n (R/m^n) tensor_k D`. Elkik applies to their ideal complements.
The natural presheaf bijections survive etale sheafification, and
finite-type affine test objects give the represented group-scheme
isomorphism by Yoneda. Repeating for the algebraic model identifies
identity components and transfers the finite-generation statement for
the local Neron-Severi point group. A pointwise completion bijection
alone would not suffice, and is not what the revised proof uses.

For the finite-type kernel inside the reduced source identity
component, prime-to-p torsion injectivity excludes all such torsion
points. Chevalley's theorem then gives a unipotent reduced identity
component; divisibility lifts component torsion, so the component group
is p-primary. A p-power kills the whole reduced group. Its nilpotent
thickening is affine. In a faithful closed matrix representation, the
entries of the remaining multiplication matrix minus the identity lie
in the nilradical. A sufficiently large p-power annihilates the matrix
power. Thus a p-power kills the entire group scheme, including possible
mu_p factors. The subsequent faithfully flat descent of multiplication
and proper abelian image are exactly the valid argument of C.3q.
Point realization from C.3w now yields actual ordinary surjectivity.
There is no need to algebraize the pair (R,f) simultaneously.

## Primary sources freshly inspected

- Artin (1969), Theorem 3.8, printed p.32, including direct image
  inspection; proof and Theorem 3.9, pp.33–34.

### Record: agents/real_weil/C3ag_incidence_invariant_independent_REVIEW.md

# Independent audit of the N=6 incidence invariant candidate

**Final status: PASS on the full typed C.3ag proof and its auxiliary Hodge
input.** The initial audit below concerned incidence geometry and boundary
compatibility. Sections 7 and 8 record the subsequent full proof and fresh
primary-source audit, completed at 2026-11-19 09:11:36 UTC. Earlier qualified
stages are retained as check history, not as current unresolved gates.
The entire existing C.3ae proof was also read for the surrounding model
and obstruction.

Let S=W×C be smooth of dimension3, M=O_W(1) external K_C, and a=c1(M).
Three sections s0,s1,s2 have a transverse finite common zero scheme B.
Let H⊂P²×S be Σx_i s_i=0 and let p:H→S. Put h=c1(O_P²(1))|H.

## 1. Geometry: pass

Away from B, the kernel of O_U³→M_U is a rank2 bundle and H_U is its
P¹-bundle of lines. At b∈B the three ds_i form a basis of T*_bS after
trivializing M. At a point (x,b), the differential Σx_i ds_i is nonzero,
since x is a nonzero projective coordinate vector. Hence H is smooth
there as well. H_U is integral. Its complement P²×B has dimension2,
whereas H is a Cartier hypersurface of pure dimension4, so that complement
contains no component. Thus H is integral. The degree of B is M³=48.
The same equations show the strict-transform divisor and exceptional
zero section are transverse in the existing C.3ae construction.
The contraction, finite normalization, unique vertex, strict henselization,
and maximal-ideal pullback arguments are unchanged from that proof.

## 2. The integral CH1 decomposition needs no full Cayley trick

For every smooth parameter P, the complement of H_U×P in H×P has
codimension2. Localization therefore gives an isomorphism of CH¹ groups:
there are no codimension-one cycles supported in that complement.
The projective-bundle formula on H_U×P gives

    CH¹(H_U×P) = p* CH¹(U×P) ⊕ h p* CH⁰(U×P).

Removing B×P from S×P has codimension3, so localization likewise identifies
CH¹(U×P) with CH¹(S×P) and CH⁰(U×P) with CH⁰(S×P).
The displayed summands come from p* and h globally; consequently

    CH¹(H×P) = p* CH¹(S×P) ⊕ h p* CH⁰(S×P)

integrally. The argument is compatible with taking a parameter fibre.
This avoids any unverified application of a Cayley-trick decomposition in
higher codimensions. The exact localization and projective-bundle sources
opened afresh are Stacks Lemma42.19.3, Tag02RX, and Lemma42.36.2,
Tag02TX, including their proofs. H, S and their products here are smooth
finite-type schemes, within these sources' explicit scope.

## 3. Exact pushforwards and boundary invariance: pass

In P²×S, the divisor class of H is h+a and h³=0. By pushing its
intersection with h^j through the ambient trivial P²-bundle, one obtains

    p_*1=0, p_*h=1, p_*h²=a, p_*h³=0.

There is no exceptional-fibre correction: these are identities computed
through the global Cartier embedding. For γ=γ0+hγ1 in CH¹(H×P),

    p_*[(h−2a)(h+a)γ0] = 0,
    p_*[(h−2a)(h+a)hγ1] = −3a²γ1.

Writing τ=4[D_W]−H_W, with deg(τH_W)=0, the further pushforward of
τa²γ1 along W vanishes. Indeed a=H_W+k with k=c1(K_C), k²=0,
τH_W²=0 for dimension reasons, and deg(τH_W)=0 kills the remaining
term 2τH_W k. CH⁰(S×P) consists of constant integers on components.
Thus the invariant

    α ↦ pr_(C×P),* ( τ p_*[(h−2a)α] )

kills every lift ambiguity (h+a)CH¹(H×P) exactly in Pic(C×P), not merely
in its mixed component. Chow localization and the self-intersection
formula therefore define it on CH²(Y×P). All operations commute with
refined pullback to parameter points. A chosen closure before taking
fibres remains an allowed closure afterwards, so this is an actual-family
statement, with no henselized-parameter substitution.

## 4. Source and target formulae: pass

For b=b0+h b1+h²b2 obtained by restricting a fixed closure on Z×P,
the exact pushforward is

    p_*[(h−2a)b|H] = b0−a b1−2a²b2.

Since Pic⁰(W)=0, a divisor on W×C×P is a sum of a fixed divisor D'_W
from W and a line bundle λ from C×P (by the usual Picard/see-saw
argument). Thus

    pr_(C×P),*(τ a b1) = deg(τD'_W) · pr_C* K_C,
    pr_(C×P),*(τ a²b2) = 0.

The correction therefore has constant fibre class in Pic(C), so only
the mixed Hodge component of pr_*(τb0) needs to be killed by the new K3
argument. Finally for θ(λ) obtained from D_W and λ∈Pic⁰(C),

    ρ θ(λ) = deg(τD_W) λ = −9 λ.

The pullback of divisor classes is defined even though p is not flat at B;
no flat pullback of arbitrary cycles through p is used. This typing point
is necessary and is satisfied by the construction.

## 5. Remaining separate source/data check

The proposed Hodge step uses a very general rank2 Picard K3 so that only
its one-dimensional primitive Neron–Severi part contributes. If W is
specified using algebraically independent coefficients, the argument must
justify that this genericity realizes the required Hodge-group statement;
it must not silently assume every exceptional complex Hodge locus is
defined over Qbar. This is a source/genericity point for the separate
root/frobenius audit, not a flaw in the incidence geometry or boundary
calculation certified above.

Audit recorded at 2026-11-19 09:03:58 UTC.


## 6. Conditional Hodge representation argument and three-section rank

Assume the exact source input Hg(T_W)=SO(T_W), where T_W is the rational
transcendental K3 Hodge structure. Because H¹(C) is CM, the derived group
of Hg(T_W direct-sum H¹(C)) contains SO(T_W) acting trivially on H¹(C).
The standard SO representation is absolutely irreducible, so any rational
Hodge substructure of T_W tensor H¹(C) has the form T_W tensor U for a
rational subspace U of H¹(C). Stability under the Hodge circle makes U
a weight1 Hodge substructure. If U is nonzero, its (1,0) part is nonzero,
and T_W^(2,0) tensor U^(1,0) gives a nonzero (3,0) part. Thus this tensor
product has no nonzero level1 Hodge substructure. A rational Hodge class
in T_W tensor H¹(C) tensor H¹(P) would give a morphism
H¹(P)^*(-2)→T_W tensor H¹(C), whose image has level1; it is therefore
zero. The algebraic component under consideration consequently lies in
NS(W)_Q tensor H¹(C) tensor H¹(P). This argument is uniform in P and
requires no genericity condition chosen after P.

On a smooth quartic, the combined multiplication map
H¹(Ω¹_W)/C·H→H⁰(O_W(1))^* tensor H¹(Ω¹_W(1)) is injective: its dual is
the surjection sum_j w_j R₃→R₄ of the Jacobian quotient, which is
generated in degree1. Hence for the nonzero primitive NS class τ there
is a linear form w with μ(w)τ nonzero. The special triple s_i=w z_i
(i=0,1,2) makes the obstruction map on C·τ tensor C³ injective.
This proves that rank3 is a nonempty Zariski-open condition on the
space of triples of sections of M. The transverse finite-base-locus
condition is a different nonempty Zariski-open condition because M is
very ample. Their intersection is nonempty. The special triple proves
rank nonemptiness only and is not itself asserted to have a finite base
locus. This exact distinction is necessary.

These representation and rank deductions were independently checked.
The existence/specification of W with the assumed Hodge group remains
the separate primary-source input described in Section5.

## 7. Full typed proof, auxiliary Hodge input, and exact generic data: pass

Read in full:

- research/agents/cycles/dimension_six.tex, all six steps;
- research/agents/frobenius/C3_N6_Hodge_inputs.tex;
- research/agents/cycles/higher_local_surjectivity.tex, the inherited model
  and necessary Chern-class obstruction.

There are exactly 30 quartic coefficients after requiring the fixed line
D=V(w0,w1) to lie on W. The Hodge-generic complex choice of W is explicitly
separate from the algebraically independent 36 coefficients of the section
triple. This repairs the potential genericity ambiguity identified in the
initial audit: no claim that arbitrary coefficient-field embeddings preserve
the Hodge-generic surface is now made. The fixed field K0 contains the
quartic coefficients, the fixed divisor D, and Qbar. Both section conditions
are algebraic over K0. The rank-three open is nonempty by the special tuple
s_i=w z_i; its purpose is solely to prove rank nonemptiness. The independent
very-ample Bertini open gives the transverse reduced base scheme of length
48. Algebraic independence puts the chosen triple in both opens.

The auxiliary proof supplies NS(W)=ZH+ZD by the precise base-locus
Noether--Lefschetz theorem. Local Torelli realizes an open period domain
of dimension18 preserving H and D: H remains very ample, and Riemann--Roch
plus H.D=1 makes the deformed D an actual line. A nonscalar rational
endomorphism cannot have every period line as an eigenline; the nondegenerate
period quadric is irreducible and spans the ambient space. Its Hodge locus
is therefore proper. Avoiding countably many such loci and extra Picard
loci gives End_Hdg(T)=Q. The Hodge-locus theorem is being used over C only,
and the source itself warns against assuming a smaller field of definition.

Zarhin then gives Hg(T)=SO(T). The Fermat curve Jacobian is isogenous to
the cube of the CM elliptic curve with endomorphisms by Q(i). The derived
group of the joint Hodge group surjects onto SO(T) and is trivial on the
torus, so the joint group is SO(T) times that torus. The two opposite
character twists of the standard representation over Q(i) are absolutely
irreducible and exchanged by conjugation. Thus T tensor H1(E) is simple
over Q and has a nonzero (3,0) part. Semisimplicity excludes every level-one
sub-Hodge structure of its threefold direct sum. By the Poincare-duality
identification H1(P)^*(-2)=H1(P)(-1), the mixed class of every codimension2
cycle on W times C times P consequently has its W factor in NS(W).
This is uniform in P and does not require another genericity choice.

The model proof inherited from C.3ae does not require the three sections
to have no common zero. On F=0 the section ell G is not identically zero;
therefore F=ell G=0 still has codimension2 in the ambient base. The graph
open is dense in the Cartier hypersurface, and the remaining locus has
insufficient dimension to produce another component. Smoothness near V,
proper contraction, finite normalization, the unique vertex, and the
equality m_R O_Z=I_V are unchanged. The incidence calculation proves
smoothness of H at the base points and hence regularity and transversality
of the strict transform. All claimed punctures are regular.

The full necessary Chern obstruction on an actual fixed-product cycle
closure is correctly inherited, with rational Chern character used only
to deduce a zero class over C. Its projected summand receives no term
having a projective-space differential. The Hodge input reduces b0 to
the H and tau factors, the H factor is killed by the multiplication maps,
and the rank-three map kills the tau component. Section4 above checks
the two exact source corrections. The resulting mixed Picard component
is zero; the Jacobian homomorphism therefore has zero differential and
is zero in characteristic0. Compactification of the fixed parameter curve
preserves the chosen fibres. Thus rho kills the entire ordinary algebraic
source image, without any formal-family or henselized-parameter substitute.

The target divisor products are well typed under arbitrary pullback of
line bundles. The Poincare family makes them ordinarily algebraically
trivial. The exact identity rho theta=-9 is integral, and divisibility of
Pic0(C)(C) gives the asserted integral cokernel surjection. The specified
transcendental Abel point is nontorsion since the Abel closed immersion
and all torsion points are defined over Qbar. Hence the rational map also
fails to be surjective. No correction to the full typed proof is required.

## Material corrections to earlier material

1. **R9, 14:44–47:** a nonzero Griffiths class directly refutes A; failure of A′ for *any* smooth hyperplane section follows from finite-dimensional NS versus the infinite-dimensional quintic target. No Noether–Lefschetz assumption on the section is needed. “General” is used in Clemens's countable-exceptional-set sense, not for a specified Fermat quintic. C.1g now separately proves a complete infinite-order line counterexample on the specified geometric generic quintic. The original Annals1969 target remains not obtained; the alternative proof uses the correctly identified Griffiths1968 differential formula and explicitly expands the Ceresa–Collino cyclic geometry.
2. **Homological substitution, 14:45–46:** ordinary affine cohomology vanishes in high degrees, but quotienting cycles by homological equivalence does not preserve the right-exact localization argument used for algebraic equivalence. Arbitrary cohomological preimages need not be proved algebraic. This is a proof gap, not a refutation of the standard conjecture.
3. **Letter B:** do not identify its positivity assertion with modern Lefschetz standard conjecture B. `V×P1` gives a fixed-variety counterexample to B(X)⇒alg=num; it does not refute a statement with B assumed universally on all auxiliary varieties. The original cote14 scan46 explicitly says A and B imply semisimplicity; the opened English translation p258 instead says B also implies it. C.2d now records this source discrepancy and follows the original for the historical assertion.
4. **R10, 14:64:** “best denominator = card Ker u” is false. The least denominator is the group-scheme exponent. `[2]` on an abelian g-fold has exponent2 and order2^(2g), including in characteristic2. Uniqueness is in normalized divisor correspondences, not among actual divisors. The torsion quotient in 2.5 is essential.
5. **R10, 14:67:** the polarization sign depends on the Jacobian autoduality convention; the isogeny and rational inverse statements do not depend on that sign.
6. **R11:** cone dimension n+1, localization codimension r−1, and primitive exponents are checked. Global failure alone does not imply strict-local failure; root-owned C.3e supplies the missing finite-étale-stage argument and C.3f–g give actual strict-local counterexamples.
7. **Integral Question2:** isogeny to a Jacobian does not give an integral splitting. The exact criterion is direct summand of a product of Jacobians. For ppav over C, the minimal class criterion is equivalent. Recent Engel–de Gaay Fortman–Schreieder v3 supplies a negative result in dimension≥4; older “open” statements must be dated.
8. **R12, 14:126:** superscript `a` denotes the algebraically trivial subgroup of Chow, not the quotient by algebraic equivalence. Over Fpbar torsion plus divisibility kills products integrally. Spieß–Szamuely Th1.2 covers p-primary Albanese torsion; Th1.1 alone would not.
9. **R14 reading's finite-field parenthesis:** the established full CH0^0=Alb statement used here is over Fpbar, not an asserted equality over every finite-field model.
10. **R14/Lead E4:** rational family-converse equivalence is proved. The literal integral biconditional is false: on the same Ottem–Suzuki fourfold the family converse is true by diagonal decomposition, while ordinary AJ and Albanese equivalence differ. Under CH0 support on a surface, P for one-cycles is canonically the Walker Jacobian group; its ordinary AJ kernel is exactly the finite isogeny kernel.
11. **14:38:** a curve C inside A generates A through J(C); C(k)→A(k) is not itself generally surjective. The cohomological source is H^{2(n−i)+1}(X)(n−i).
12. **E.1:** saturation is not automatic for additive cones, even with the listed positivity/Galois/j requirements. It is automatic for all lattice points of a closed convex real cone with epsilon interior polar. The manuscript's unspecified meaning of “cone” must be stated.

## Approaches tried and what failed

For C.3 we separated the cycle-class diagram by the snake lemma, used projective bundle examples, tested the middle primitive map on `V×P1`, and used K3 Chow localization. The simpler products `V×P^r` fail to refute A for i≥2: their Griffiths strings are mapped isomorphically in that range. The codimension-two blow-up idea was then made iterable by embedding each blow-up as an incidence Cartier divisor in a projective line bundle with Tate motive. This supplies the complete universal A-surjectivity classification in C.1f. It still does not show injectivity on all higher Griffiths groups. The exact Chow formula was opened in Jiang2023 Theorem2.8(3), and the motive formula in Stacks Tag0FGQ. Attempts to obtain Manin1968 directly from three MathNet URLs and the torsor mirror failed; that original is not claimed opened.

For C.4 we searched primary literature on integral Fourier transforms, actual Jacobian summands, universal zero-cycles, and low-dimensional principally polarized varieties. The precise Theorem1.1 of Beckmann–de Gaay Fortman says IHC curves on A×Ahat implies IHC on A, not its converse without a unimodular line bundle. We therefore did not use IHC on all abelian threefolds to infer the stronger Question2 for arbitrary polarizations. Scavia–Suzuki's arbitrary-field theorem was opened and its crystalline clause retained.

For E.3 we read the exact definition of Albanese equivalence and its generation by abelian families up to integer, opened regular-homomorphism/Walker papers, and isolated the kernel-component issue. A torsion ℓ-adic cycle map by itself does not assign values to every nontorsion generator of the quotient. We repaired this for the forward implication by spreading one whole torsion relation, including all rational-equivalence data, then specializing to an algebraic closure of a finite field. The integral H1 comparison gives a prime-to-ℓ Albanese isogeny on the special fibre; finite-field torsion plus Roitman's ℓ-primary theorem kills the entire specialized relation. Bloch's exact specialization and functoriality theorems then prove E.3e. Existence of an algebraic representative in codimension2 is still insufficient by itself to identify its kernel with the manuscript's equivalence in the complex converse.

The further E.3j–l argument uses an actual diagonal decomposition, with all denominator clearing stated. Support on a curve kills the target-supported term on one-cycles; support on a surface factors it through Pic0. The source-divisor term factors through Albanese groups. Integral AJ functoriality gives a uniform exponent for the quotient AJ kernel. ACMV Theorem2.3 says the entire Walker kernel is divisible, so its quotient by N, being both divisible and of bounded exponent, vanishes. This proves the stated support cases without assuming Walker universality.

E.3m then proves that the general integral family converse itself is equivalent to P being the Walker Jacobian group: under that converse, one family covering the Walker Jacobian absorbs every other family by comparison of same-dimension isogeny covers; its remaining finite divisible kernel is zero. E.3n separately completes the previously proposed countability route. Zero-cycle representatives are moved to flat loci; Hilbert schemes, marked points, exact relative Albanese-zero conditions, and flat P1×X rational-equivalence witnesses give a countable union of constructible membership loci. Baire and the subgroup property yield a uniform exponent, and divisibility kills the family map. This identifies the integral and rational family conditions over C. E.3u subsequently refutes their universal truth using full morphic Abel–Jacobi; the exact equivalences remain valid.

No priority claim is made for the cone polarity, denominator observation, or generic rectangle argument. They are direct deductions; the literature searches and opened inputs are recorded rather than claiming novelty from failure to find an exact prior formulation.

## Finite computation scope

`check_cycles.py` checks matrices for six finite-dimensional models of the Griffiths summand, the cone formulae on a 625-point grid with multipliers1–12, smoothness of the Fermat quartic by four exact projective Jacobian-ideal calculations, the elliptic discriminant64, and the elementary [2] denominator comparison. It also checks seven formal blow-up stages and 49 pairs (n,i), plus a local universal Rees-ideal elimination. The infinite-dimensional statements and Chow nonvanishing are proved in prose, not inferred from the bounded computation. Python3.12.14, SymPy1.14.0; full output and exact runtime are in `output/check_cycles.json`.

## Decisive morphic distinction and explicit E.3 refutation (07:10 UTC)

The original Walker2007 paper distinguishes its full morphic Jacobian from the finite coniveau Jacobian now commonly called the Walker Jacobian. The full map is exactly ordinary Albanese on zero-cycles and functorial for every correspondence of the nonnegative dimension shift needed here. Hence it kills every generator of N. Its Jacobian construction uses a countable integral Lawson group; strict Hodge filtration makes a nonzero rational H1 cylinder produce nontorsion morphic image. These are E.3t, with all exact primary statements opened.

E.3u specifies a degree14 geometric generic hypersurface Y in split Q6, using65891 independent coefficients, and gamma equal to the difference of its intersections with two displayed maximal planes. Gamma has zero integral H4 class, but its Lawson s-image is nonzero rationally. The latter is the effective Friedlander argument: the actual published input is Theorem3.4, not the4.5 locator printed in Walker6.1. Nagel vanishing through10 covers the proof's required restriction degrees8/6 and relative degree9. Smooth dominant spread handles extra transcendental coefficients. Taking the fixed elliptic curve v²=u³−u and X=Y×E produces a codimension4 family with zero singular cylinder and injective Lawson cylinder. Its specified geometric generic point e survives nontorsion modulo N by spreading a hypothetical finite N relation and contradicting the countable torsion preimage.

Thus N⁴(X) is strictly smaller than ker Walker=A*H, and P⁴(X) has a nontorsion kernel over the finite Walker Jacobian. The formerly open equality <H*²>₀=A*H is false universally. This does not refute the separately conjectured comparison of the large one-motivic quotient with the full morphic Jacobian; that comparison is not needed for any conclusion above. No codimension2 counterexample or Beauville-conjecture counterexample is inferred.

Root and Frobenius independently read the full typed E.3s–u proofs and exact primary sources. Their targeted checks, the nonproper-CH0 wording repair, locator corrections, and failed source URLs are recorded. New files: one_motivic_quotient.tex, morphic_counterexample.tex, morphic_results.csv (3 rows), morphic_CHECK_EVENTS.tsv (17 events), morphic_references.bib; generation_references.bib and generation_CHECK_EVENTS.tsv retain the preceding source/failed-route audits. code/check_morphic_counterexample.py and output/check_morphic_counterexample.json give the finite algebraic certificate and exact runtime0.056826961001206655 seconds. code/compile_morphic.py and output/compile_morphic.json retain the successful four-pass standalone7-page build, without undefined citations or overfull boxes at that version, in3.2785024129989324 seconds. Parent performs the integrated master build and visual review.

## Local Picard refinements and literature scope

C.3i and C.3j were independently audited by root and frobenius against
Kollar's published actual-group sequences. The finite normalization of
the section ring is used because the target cone has a degree-two
section defect; the proof does not identify its cone contraction with
the blow-up of its maximal ideal. The fixed-scheme algebraic-equivalence
definition is distinguished from the local Picard functor's varying
etale neighbourhood convention.

C.3j proves that every ordinary algebraic-equivalence relation comes
from an abelian-subvariety image, by descending the whole family at a
single finite stage and extending its divisor to a smooth projective
compactification of the product. Kollar's unipotent-kernel theorem
then gives injectivity in dimension at least four. C.3k reaches
dimension three through a separate torsion-cover argument, including
the finite normalization/Hartogs calculation of all global divisor
units and the exact connectedness theorem after normal completion.
The final closed kernel is defined by the morphism of local Picard
functors; it does not presume a universal line bundle on an abelian
image of another Picard variety.

The new literature search opened Kollar2016 Theorem1.7, Definition3.3,
Section3.5 and Example4.2; Bhatt--deJong2014 Theorem0.1 and Examples1.31,
1.33--1.34; Wlodarczyk2005 Theorem1.0.2; Varbaro2009 Theorem1.6 and
Corollary1.12; Boutot1978 Introduction Theorem1(i)--(ii); and the exact
Stacks normalization, henselian, completion and connectedness lemmas.
Boutot's full ChapterII proof remains behind the publisher's subscription
wall, while the exact theorem statement in the publisher's introduction
was obtained and screenshot-checked. These standard source theorems
are cited inputs; no independent proof of resolution or local Picard
representability is claimed. No priority is claimed for the refinements.

No scan images were supplied. Statements about handwriting are limited
to the given transcriptions and their recorded uncertainty.

## Remaining gates and attempted approaches

**D.4:** A formal transport proof under a supplied compatible global
polarization does not prove that such algebraic polarizations exist and
specialize compatibly for all geometric motives. The slope expression
also fails before that question on the explicit even-weight example.
Milne's quotient positivity criterion now identifies the precise test
on additional endomorphisms. His 2002 Theorem 2.1 and Remark 3.7 give
actual CM and Lefschetz reduction cases. The 2026 van Hoften–Sempliner
preprint Theorem I, explicitly retained as a cited input, removes the
Hodge hypothesis in the abelian case and, through Milne 1999 Theorem
7.1, proves the abelian-type finite-field effectivity assertion. The
author's cofinal-field erratum was opened and retained. The general
specialization/positivity gate and effectivity outside the abelian-type
category have not been eliminated. D.4f identifies the latter exactly
with the full Tate cycle conjecture in the stated numerical-motive
framework: already every Frobenius-identity motive being effective
would force all Tate classes to be algebraic. D.4g extends the actual
polarization-preservation case from CM families to arbitrary relatively
polarized abelian families with exact faithful Tate-preserving
specialization functors, by the common generator-and-summand forms.
Its distribution conclusion retains the real-fibre identification and
slope-parity existence required by D.4a.

**E.4:** The literal identification with the attached integral theory is
refuted, not left as an open comparison: the proper sign terms in E.4b
and the Kummer contradiction in E.4d settle it. The corrected bounded
family is now constructed on every real-place-retaining finite-prime
open in every twist. E.4o proves that the twist-one localization cone
has the specified Artin–Verdier finite-coefficient comparison. E.4p
assembles all twists by keeping the proper rational correction and
using actual étale restrictions, with the separate explicit correction
in twist one. The complete rational manuscript table is preserved.

E.4l computes every twist-zero open group and identifies the actual
local support with the continuous dual of Z_p^× in degree three.
At the open over Z[1/2], degree two is Q_2/Z_2. E.4m identifies
RΓ(Q_p,Q_p/Z_p(n))[-2] as the extra negative-twist support. E.4n
proves full integral purity for n≥2 by Geisser2004 Proposition5.1
with its exact local cohomological-dimension bound; the historical
Bloch–Kato assumption is supplied by the opened Voevodsky2011 theorem.
All twists have purity after inverting the residue characteristic.
This explains the two refutations E.4j–k: naïve integral purity and
finite generation on opens cannot accompany all the desired finite
comparisons and rational values. The counterexamples and the corrected
complexes are compatible, not competing conjectures.

The remaining scope is explicit: these results do not identify every
open Weil–étale topos, every compact-support convention, or an
unspecified multiplicative theory on arbitrary arithmetic schemes.
Those are additional comparisons, not prerequisites left unfinished
for the stated family. The sign-deleted complexes E.4f still necessarily
lose the ordinary mod-two Kummer comparison. The named Morin, Chiu,
Beshenov, Geisser and Spitzweck sources were inspected in their actual
coefficient categories; no theorem from one category is silently
renamed as a theorem in another.

## Current-literature audit added after checkpoint 01

The full 25-page author PDF of van Hoften–Sempliner, The Hodge Conjecture
for CM abelian varieties, was opened and downloaded on 19 November 2026.
The Hexagon registry labels it 2610.00259v2, with v1 on 15 October and
the v2 submission on 18 October. Its Theorem I has no hypothesis beyond

checked the elementary surface-to-cycle reduction (Theorem 2.3.4) and
read the proof chain through Theorems 2.2.1 and 4.0.2 and Lemmas
4.2.7–4.2.10. The auxiliary field is enlarged to have nontrivial totally
real part, as required for compact Picard modular surfaces. The analytic
imports from the theta-correspondence literature have not been independently

primary preprint theorem; neither an announcement alone nor a statement
about all varieties is substituted for it. The parent's independent
