in CHECK_EVENTS.tsv.

**E.5:** Wibmer settles uniqueness after existence over an algebraically
closed field. It does not manufacture the manuscript's unspecified
integral model of rational motives in characteristic p. The norm torus
and the stronger pro-torus construction settle universal perfect-field
claims, including the cohomological-dimension-one pro-limit refinement,
not the undeclared motivic subcase. The structured crystal moduli are
explicit, but the phrase
continuous H1 has no defined object to which an equivalence can be proved.

## Ordinary local surjectivity: actual-family counterexample

C.3m uses the Fermat quartic C and V=P^m times C, with m=N-2.
Inside the total space of O(-1,-1) over P^m times P^2, the equation
F+t*ell*H defines a smooth neighborhood of V. The proper Segre
contraction gives an explicitly presented affine image W. The ring
R is the strict henselization of the normalization of this original
affine W at its unique vertex. The auxiliary neighborhood used in
the proof does not enter the definition of R. The principal function
f=w00+w11+w22 has regular punctured divisor Y.

The first-neighborhood class has three independent curve components.
Contraction with holomorphic one-forms is injective. Illusie's actual
line-bundle obstruction formula, checked against his theorem and
errata, then forces an abelian family that extends to that first
neighborhood to have zero classifying map to Pic^0(C). The square-zero
unit sequence makes all its individual first-neighborhood fibres
equal. Higher coherent cohomology vanishes, giving compatible formal
trivializations. Proper formal full faithfulness and finite reflexive
module descent over the faithfully flat completion prove that every
ordinarily algebraically trivial line bundle on X is actually trivial.

On Y, the projection from the strict transform to C pulls back the
actual Poincare family. A boundary-divisor and fibre-degree argument
proves this gives an injection of Pic^0(C). A point with one coordinate
transcendental over the algebraic numbers gives an explicit infinite-order
line bundle, so the counterexample survives rational coefficients.
This settles ordinary J1 nonsurjectivity itself. The earlier full-Picard
counterexample C.3i alone did not do so. Root and frobenius independently
audited all actual-family and formal-to-local passages before integration.

## General coefficient-field injectivity

C.3n removes the algebraizability restriction from the positive ordinary
injectivity assertion and also treats characteristic p after rationalization.
An ordinary curve family extends by Cartier closure directly along the
proper parameter curve. Normalizing its degree and one parameter fibre
classifies it by a map from the puncture to that curve's Jacobian. The
dual-Jacobian Poincare family then gives an actual abelian-parameter
family on the puncture, with no compactification or resolution of the
source. Boutot's original arbitrary-noetherian hypotheses give the source
local Picard scheme and the target functor's closed unit section. Their
closed kernel in the proper abelian image has no prime-to-characteristic
torsion and is therefore finite with p-primary point group. Both root
and frobenius independently checked the complete proof and the actual
primary page before integration. The coefficient/residue field is part
of the theorem's data, not an implicit mixed-characteristic convention.

## Typesetting status

The initial local compile failed because lualatex.fmt was absent; that
attempt is preserved in code/build_fragments.py and build/build_commands.out.
The parent subsequently repaired the shared TeX installation and reported
successful master builds and statement-register checks. The parent remains
responsible for integrating these fragments and the final visual inspection.
No separate uninspected final PDF is supplied by this agent.

## Exact degree-one boundary dimensions over C

C.3o completes the small-dimensional ordinary rational J1 cases.
For N1 both groups vanish. For N2 the target vanishes, so surjectivity
is automatic, but the strict henselization of the Fermat quartic cone
contains its Jacobian in ordinary Pic_alg and restriction to z2=0
kills it. For N3 the same cone is a divisor in a regular strict-local
threefold, whose puncture has Pic=0; surjectivity therefore fails.
Together with C.3m--n, injectivity always holds for N>=3 overC,
whereas surjectivity fails in every dimension N>=3. The failures
use an infinite-order actual line bundle and survive Q coefficients.
Over Fpbar, by contrast, the actual abelian-family construction makes
ordinary Pic_alg torsion. These characteristic distinctions are retained.

The same Fpbar torsion conclusion now holds for ordinary algebraically
trivial Chow classes in every codimension under the precise curve-family
convention in C.3o. A horizontal cycle closes over a smooth projective
parameter curve and remains flat there. A principal multiple of the
marked degree-zero divisor pulls back to a principal cycle relation,
and proper pushforward kills the corresponding finite multiple of the
fibre difference. Taking the excellent regular puncture itself as the
universally catenary base makes all these maps finite type in the needed
Chow formalism. No finite-type-over-k assumption on the puncture is hidden.

## Local torsion, completion, and the exact positive-characteristic gate

C.3p is finalized after full independent root and frobenius audits.
Restriction of finite etale covers is fully faithful in dimension at
least three and an equivalence in dimension at least four, for the
specified excellent normal strictly local ring with regular puncture.
For a regular divisor puncture, the invertible-order Picard torsion
groups are consequently isomorphic in dimension at least four. This
includes mixed characteristic. A separate argument proves invariance of
the coefficient-field local Picard functor under completion, by comparing
the identical completions of the two henselized tensor pairs and applying
Elkik's exact Picard corollary. It does not remove the henselization from
an arbitrary family over a parameter scheme.

C.3q, also independently audited by both agents, treats the normal
essentially-finite-type characteristic-p scope. Every proper abelian
subgroup of the target local Picard functor lifts to a proper source
abelian subgroup up to two isogenies whose composite is a power of p.
Prime-to-p torsion density, finite generation of the normal local
Neron–Severi group, and Kollár's unipotent restriction kernel give the
proof. It is a theorem about the specified local functors. The additional
point-realization argument on the fixed puncture times curves is supplied
separately by C.3w; no universal actual family on the whole subgroup is
inferred from the functor theorem itself.

## A complete all-characteristic smooth-cone case

C.3u closes that ordinary-family issue for a smooth projective cone
source in dimension at least four, assuming its full section ring is
generated in degree one. The divisor element is arbitrary, including
nonhomogeneous elements, and its puncture is assumed regular. Pullback
identifies the actual ordinary algebraically trivial Picard group of
the source with the point group of Pic0_red of its smooth projective
exceptional variety. Restriction to the divisor puncture is integrally
surjective in every characteristic, with zero kernel in characteristic
zero and finite p-primary kernel in characteristic p. Ordinary rational
J1 restriction is therefore an isomorphism in this scope.

The proof detects every ell-power torsion line bundle, for one prime ell
outside the torsion of NS(V)/Z[L], by extending it to the regular cone
resolution and twisting by the single exceptional divisor. Proper
henselian finite-etale comparison then identifies it with a pullback
from Pic0(V). Dense torsion in each ordinary abelian image, combined
with C.3p, proves target surjectivity. The source Poincare family is
already an actual product family. No splitting of the full local
Picard functor or general family-algebraization theorem is invoked.
The result is consistent with the C.3m counterexamples because their
sources are deformations of cones. C.3w subsequently closes the ordinary
point-realization step for the general algebraizable scope of C.3q.
The cone theorem remains an independent all-characteristic result.

## General algebraizable positive-characteristic degree-one surjectivity

C.3w is proved after a full independent root proof/source audit. Let R
be the strict henselization of a normal local scheme essentially of
finite type over an algebraically closed coefficient/residue field k,
with dimension at least three and regular puncture X. Every k-point
of a proper abelian subgroup of the local Picard scheme is an actual
ordinary algebraically trivial line-bundle class on X. This is a
point-realization theorem; it does not claim a universal line bundle
on the fixed product X times the whole abelian subgroup.

The construction fixes a regular alteration and works over a proper
parameter curve. Cartier extension on smooth henselized parameter
charts has only a constant exceptional-divisor lattice ambiguity,
whose first etale cohomology on the curve vanishes. Rigidifying at a
closed-fibre point leaves a formal-unit gerbe. A recursive Amitsur
calculation on each fixed affine cover proves the needed ordinary
etale cohomology vanishings for its band, so the family exists formally.
Proper formal existence and Artin approximation then supply an actual
family on the alteration. The approximation preserves its fibre
differences because the Picard kernel of a nilpotent proper immersion
is quasi-affine, and a proper connected curve maps constantly into it.
Compatible trivializations follow from finite-dimensional
Mittag–Leffler images of the global-function rings. Finally the norm
on the actual finite-flat locus extends uniquely across codimension
two in the regular target. Divisibility of the abelian point group
removes the alteration degree.

Combining this with C.3q proves **integral ordinary Picard restriction
surjectivity in characteristic p for every dimension N>=4** in this
algebraizable scope, for any nonzero local divisor element whose
puncture Y is regular. C.3n gives the p-primary torsion kernel, so the
ordinary rational J1 map is an isomorphism. The proof assumes neither
resolution of singularities in positive characteristic nor a general
henselian GAGA essential-surjectivity theorem. The remaining broader
local questions are outside these stated scopes: in particular the
arbitrary nonalgebraizable excellent-ring extension and higher
codimension. Characteristic-zero unrestricted ordinary surjectivity
is already refuted by C.3m, while the all-characteristic smooth-cone
case is settled by C.3u.

## Superseded local drafts

`C3_smooth_cone_draft.tex` and `C3_alteration_family_draft.tex` are now
comment-only pointers to the canonical final fragments, preserving their
filenames without presenting competing proved versions. The separate
smooth-cone draft source/bibliography notes are likewise pointers to
`SOURCE_AUDIT.md` and `references.bib`. In C.3w the initial stronger
affine-unipotent Picard-kernel argument was replaced by the exact
quasi-affine pullback theorem of Cheng–Ji–Larson–Olander, Proposition1.4;
quasi-affineness is precisely sufficient for the proper-curve rigidity
step. This substitution was independently checked by root. No numbered
proved assertion was dropped.


## Positive-characteristic ordinary degree-one boundary closure

C.3x, in `C3_positive_characteristic_boundary.tex`, supplies the full
dimension table for algebraizable normal strict local germs over an
algebraically closed coefficient/residue field of characteristic p.
Over Fpbar both rational ordinary J1 groups vanish. Over every larger
algebraically closed field, the universal injectivity/surjectivity
answers are: N1 yes/yes (zero groups); N2 no/yes; N3 yes/no; N>=4 yes/yes.
The last row uses C.3w and therefore retains its algebraizability scope.
The characteristic-zero table remains different.

The boundary failures are explicit in every positive characteristic,
including 2 and 3. Smooth plane elliptic cubics are chosen over Fp; a
point with transcendental x-coordinate gives a non-torsion Pic0 class
because every finite torsion subgroup is defined over Fpbar. The cone
resolution proves actual Pic0 injection after strict henselization.
A transverse linear section has three field punctures in dimension two;
the same cubic divisor in a regular local threefold yields dimension-three
non-surjectivity. Exact polynomial certificates and finite independent
samples are saved, and no finite check is used as a non-torsion proof.


## Multiplicative local divisor quotient in every characteristic

C.3y gives a fully specified strict local surface, regular off its closed
point, with ordinary Pic_alg=0 and Pic=k* direct sum (Z/4)^2. The explicit
projective blowup contraction uses twelve transverse triangle-quartic
points, including a separate coefficient in characteristic229.
The formal Picard group stabilizes at the exceptional triangle, and
proper GAGA plus Elkik gives actual line bundles on the strict henselization.
Actual curve families extend to a proper parameter and have constant
triangle gluing, so none of the multiplicative subgroup becomes ordinary
algebraically trivial. This refutes integral local B1 finiteness in every
characteristic and rational finiteness over positive-transcendence-degree
positive-characteristic fields. The Fpbar rational quotient is zero.
Root and cycles have completed independent full audits.

## Non-top higher local quotients and the excellent-ring extension

C.3aa, in `C3_segre_nori_quotient.tex`, proves that for every
`N>=4` and `2<=r<=N-2` there is a fully specified complex strict local
normal isolated cone germ with `B^r(X)_Q != 0`. The starting cycle is the
exact Nori–Nagel witness already proved in C.1i, with ambient parameter
`c=r`, section dimension `n=r+1`, and section degree `4r+2`. For
`V=P^m x T`, `m=N-r-2`, the ordinary cycle quotient is exactly
`B^r(T)_Q / H^(m+1) B^(r-m-1)(T)_Q`; its denominator is contained in
`H B^(r-1)(T)_Q`, so the chosen primitive cycle survives. C.3e then gives
an actual class on the strict henselian puncture. Nonzero rationalization
refutes the original assertion of finite integral groups. No infinite
rational dimension is asserted, and the top range `r=N-1`, `N>=3`, is
not settled by this construction. Root and cycles independently read
and passed the complete typed proof. The exact sign/index certificate
and its runtime are retained with the text.

Root C.3z, in `research/sections/C3_completion_algebraization.tex`, has
also passed this agent's complete independent audit, including its
revised final step. It removes the algebraizability restriction from
C.3w and the characteristic-p table C.3x for excellent henselian normal
local rings with an algebraically closed coefficient/residue field and
regular puncture. The earlier scope limitations in this chronological
report are superseded to precisely that extent. The proof constructs an
actual regular map from an algebraic model with the same completion,
pulls back a regular alteration, and verifies every subsequent family
argument. Its local Neron–Severi transfer uses an isomorphism of the full
henselian-tensor Picard functors, including identity components. Its
kernel argument kills the whole finite-type group scheme by a p-power,
including infinitesimal multiplicative factors. No mixed-characteristic
or higher-codimension extension is inferred. The separate review is
`C3_completion_algebraization_independent_REVIEW.md`.
## C.3ab: an integral positive theorem inside the remaining top range

The final file `C3_surface_cone_top.tex` is proved and independently
audited by root and frobenius, including the exact primary statements.
For a strict henselian cone over a smooth projective surface in
characteristic zero, the hypothesis
`H2(V,Omega1_{V/Q} tensor L^j)=0` for every positive j gives

`CH2(X)=CH0(V)/(L.CH1(V))` and `B2(X)=Z/g`,

where g is the positive gcd of the L-degrees of integral curves.
The hypothesis holds for every such surface over Qbar and, over C,
for all K3 and geometric-genus-zero surfaces. In particular P2 with
O(d) gives Z/d, and the Fermat quartic example of C.3f has B2=0 because
it contains a line. Its non-torsion CH2 class is ordinarily algebraically
trivial, so there is no conflict with that earlier counterexample.

The proof uses finite-level detection deduced from Morrow's pro cdh
theorem, an integral relative-K2 calculation justified by unique
divisibility, a direct henselian-DVR zero-cycle argument, and the exact
codimension-two Riemann–Roch multiplier minus1. Source and certificate
records distinguish every input. The former draft is superseded by a
comment-only pointer. Arbitrary complex surface cones, arbitrary
three-dimensional germs and all unrestricted higher top cases remain
open here; this subset result is not counted as full main-gate closure.


## C.3ad: rational top quotients of all smooth cones over Qbar

The final proof is `C3_rational_top_cones.tex`. For every smooth projective
V/Qbar of dimension d>=2 with the stated normal degree-one section ring,
it proves `CH^d(X)_Q = CH0(V)_Q / L.CH^(d-1)(V)_Q` and hence
`B^d(X)_Q=0` for the actual strict henselian puncture and ordinary
fixed-product algebraic equivalence. Loday's arbitrary-commutative-algebra
Hodge decomposition and the exact relative Adams shift avoid applying
smooth HKR to a nilpotent thickening. The only possible descent diagonal
is killed by Nakano vanishing because absolute and relative differentials
agree over Qbar. Root and frobenius completed full proof and exact primary
source audits. The symbolic certificate runtime is92.695311seconds; the
full Loday PDF and retrieval/hash manifest are retained. The old draft is
a comment-only supersession pointer. This is a restricted rational theorem:
no integral finiteness in dimension d>2 and no unrestricted local top-degree
closure is claimed or counted as main-gate movement.

The further research note `TOP_QUOTIENT_RESEARCH.md` is part of this
deliverable. It contains the audited explicit proper-curve K2-symbol lemma,
the elliptic absolute-form construction, and the precise remaining
finite-thickening-to-actual-product-family gate. It also records source
limitations and rejected inferences, including the BEK inverse-limit
K0 algebraization counterexample. These observations do not settle that
remaining gate.

The research note Section7 now proves a separate integral completion
descent lemma for K0 modulo actual proper-curve family differences.
Popescu approximation and a smooth-stage henselian retraction descend
an already existing completed family. Frobenius independently checked
the full qcqs scope and freshly opened every primary Stacks input.
This does not algebraize formal classes or identify arbitrary Chow
quotients with K0; those limits remain explicit. The earlier C3i exact
certificate was rerun once to add its required runtime0.020695seconds.

## C.3aj: all higher-codimension high-dimensional ordinary restriction maps

The final complete proof is `C3_high_range_surjectivity.tex`, with the
independently audited auxiliary input `../frobenius/C3_product_Hodge_inputs.tex`.
For every r>=2 and N>=2r+2, it specifies an excellent normal strictly
henselian complex local ring and regular punctured principal divisor
whose ordinary CH^r restriction cokernel surjects onto the nonzero uniquely
divisible group Pic0(C)(C)/Pic0(C)(Kbar). This is a genuine universal-range
counterexample and settles this high-dimensional surjectivity range.

The full proof has independent passes from root, Frobenius and cycles.
It separates the jointly Hodge-generic quartic surfaces from the algebraically
independent section coefficients. The boundary is controlled by weak
Lefschetz and a tensor-degree calculation; its values descend through
actual Hilbert families on the projective exceptional section. The ensuing
countable-field quotient avoids an unsupported exact-boundary cancellation.
The degree-r Chern obstruction kills every actual source-family difference,
whereas the target product of specified divisors evaluates to (-9)^(r-1).

The exact finite certificate and its stdout/JSON are in
`code/check_high_range_incidence.py`, `code/check_high_range_incidence.out`,
and `code/check_high_range_incidence_output.json`; runtime0.057480419seconds.
The full Grothendieck Hilbert exposé and its erratum were downloaded with
SHA256 and actual timings in `code/acquire_hilbert_sources.out`.
The former draft is a comment-only supersession pointer.

Additional independent reviews of the parent's dimension-six C.3ag and
cycles' regular-ambient C.3ah are retained as
`C3ag_incidence_invariant_independent_REVIEW.md` and
`C3ah_regular_ambient_independent_REVIEW.md`, with their separate event
ledgers. Both full typed proofs passed, including exact primary-source
inspection; they are not duplicate statement rows owned here.

## Search scope

D.1: searched the primary literature around rational Newton cocharacters,
the character-Q protorus, Langlands–Rapoport gerbes and real polarizations.
Kottwitz gives the correct rational source. Direct character duality shows
that this source has no nontrivial mu_2 map. No priority is claimed for
that elementary obstruction. Milne's corrected finite-field motives
paper supplies the comparison between abstract polarizations and the
standard-conjecture construction.

D.3: searched and checked Honda–Tate division-algebra invariants and
Milne's geometric-endomorphism formulas. The explicit family uses the
quadratic polynomial X^2-X+p and produces all denominator orders.
The relative-gerbe argument is written out, rather than inferred from
the existence of an unrelated division algebra.

E.4: opened the attached Lichtenbaum paper, Geisser's finite-field theory,
and the later Flach–Morin derived complex. Subsequent searches included
the phrases Weil-etale / totally positive, real places / 2-torsion,
Spec Z / Z(1), S-integers / Z(1), and open subschemes / number rings.
Primary results by Adrien Morin, Chiu and Beshenov were then opened.
They narrow the outstanding normalization question but were not treated
as interchangeable theories.

E.5: checked Kottwitz's sigma-conjugacy convention and the recent affine
torsor theorem of Wibmer. The latter removes the formerly common
countability qualification from algebraically closed uniqueness.

arbitrary noetherian local k-algebra with residue field k. Theorem1(i)
requires depth at least two for the finitely presented closed unit
section; (ii) additionally requires finite-dimensional H1 of the
puncture. The following sentence states that normal completion of
dimension at least three implies the hypotheses of(ii). There is
no finite-type-over-k condition on that page. Frobenius independently
rendered and checked the same page. The full ChapterII proof remains
unobtained; the theorem is a cited input, not independently reproved.

new proof by affine finite-presentation descent; it is not silently
ignored. The complete primary author notes Abelian Varieties dated

abelian-subgroup facts. This supplies the direct actual family
without compactifying the puncture and without a resolution theorem.

## C.3u: all-characteristic ordinary classes on a smooth cone

The fragment `C3_smooth_cone.tex` proves the all-characteristic
smooth-cone source theorem by a direct torsion calculation. It does not
assert a splitting of the full local Picard identity component, nor does
it identify arbitrary henselized-product parameter families with actual
line bundles on a fixed product. The Poincare family on the exceptional
smooth projective variety is an actual product family.

The source ring is the strict henselization at the vertex of the full
section ring of a smooth projective integral variety, with the full
section ring explicitly assumed generated in degree one. This makes
the standard total-line-bundle contraction the vertex blow-up and its
scheme-theoretic closed fibre the reduced exceptional variety. The
arbitrary divisor element need not be homogeneous. Regularity of its
puncture is retained.

Opened in this run on 19 November 2026:

- Stacks Project, Lemma 58.9.1, Tag 0A48: the entire statement and proof.
  This is proper finite-etale comparison over a henselian **local ring**,
  with the closed fibre; no flatness hypothesis is needed. The proof uses
  complete-ring algebraization and approximation for general henselian
  rings. In the application, only a section of a finite etale torsor is
  lifted. No Picard-family algebraization is inferred.
- Kleiman, *The Picard scheme*, arXiv:math/0504020v1, 1 April 2005,
  83 pages: Theorem 2.5(2), p.18; Exercise 4.3, p.27, with its full
  Answer 4.3, p.65; Theorem 5.4 and proof, pp.37–38; and Proposition
  5.10 and proof, pp.41–42. These supply the universal Poincare bundle
  when a rational section exists, properness of the Picard identity
  component for a normal projective variety, and the algebraic-equivalence
  point interpretation. The reduced identity component is used in
  positive characteristic. All locators refer to this author version.
- Cheng, Ji, Larson, and Olander, *Theorem of the Base*, author version
  dated 1 July 2021, 24 pages: Lemma 7.2 and Theorem 7.3 with proof,
  p.18. The latter gives Neron–Severi finite generation for smooth
  projective schemes over an algebraically closed field in every
  characteristic. The broader Theorem 7.4 was inspected but is not
  required here. The finite generation is applied to the quotient by
  the ample line-bundle class, not only to NS torsion itself.

Reproducible source acquisition is in `code/acquire_sources.py`; the
file sizes and SHA-256 hashes are in `code/smooth_cone_sources.out`.
No finite computation is used to infer the general theorem.

## C.3w: actual proper-curve families through a regular alteration

Final fragment: `C3_alteration_family.tex`. The source is explicitly the
strict henselization of a normal essentially-finite-type local model
over the algebraically closed coefficient/residue field. A normal
arbitrary excellent ring is not silently substituted. The proof realizes
points of proper abelian local-Picard subgroups by actual ordinary curve
families, and completes characteristic-p ordinary degree-one surjectivity
in C.3q's dimension-at-least-four algebraizable scope. It asserts no
universal actual family on the entire abelian subgroup.

New exact source checks in this run:

- de Jong (1996), *Smoothness, semi-stability and alterations*, published
  IHES 83, pp.51–93: full 44-page primary scan opened and downloaded;
  Theorem4.1 and Remark4.2, printed p.66, read. The alteration is proper
  and generically finite with regular quasiprojective source. It is
  applied to a finite-type model before strict henselization. The proof
  does not invoke an alteration theorem for a general nonalgebraizable
  complete excellent ring. Hash and size are in
  `code/alteration_sources.out`.

  additive quotients. It therefore proves ordinary etale cohomology
  vanishings and does not rely on an unproved inverse-limit comparison.
- Stacks Lemma21.11.1, Tag0CK0: exact gerbe-neutralization statement and

### Record: agents/real_weil/TOP_QUOTIENT_RESEARCH.md

# Residual top ordinary quotient: research record

**Updated 2026-11-19, after the certification of C.3ab and C.3ad.**

The unrestricted groups still at issue are ordinary
\(B^{N-1}(\operatorname{Spec}R\setminus\{\mathfrak m\})\), for \(N\ge3\).
Here ordinary algebraic equivalence always means differences of fibres on
actual fixed products with smooth parameter curves. An equality in a
henselized product or a compatible system of classes on infinitesimal
neighbourhoods is not an actual-product family.

## 1. Certified positive subset and its precise limit

The final, independently checked `C3_surface_cone_top.tex` proves, integrally,
for the stated smooth surface cones with
\(H^2(V,\Omega^1_{V/\mathbb Q}\otimes L^j)=0\) for all \(j>0\),
\[
 CH^2(X)=CH_0(V)/(L\,CH^1(V)),\qquad B^2(X)=\mathbb Z/g\mathbb Z.
\]
This includes every such cone over \(\overline{\mathbb Q}\), and over arbitrary
algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields when \(H^2(V,L^j)=0\) for
all \(j>0\). It is a restricted-family result. It does not settle the
unrestricted top question or establish finiteness for all complex surface
cones. Root and Frobenius independently checked the complete eight-step
argument and the exact primary sources. It is integrated after C.3aa.

The final `C3_rational_top_cones.tex` (C.3ad), independently audited by
root and Frobenius, additionally proves for every such smooth projective
cone over Qbar in every dimension d+1>=3 that
`CH^d(X)_Q = CH0(V)_Q / L.CH^(d-1)(V)_Q` and `B^d(X)_Q=0`.
This is rational in dimensions d>2 and remains a restricted smooth-cone
statement; it is not counted as main-gate movement.

## 2. An explicit proper-curve family for every field \(K_2\) symbol

**Auxiliary lemma, proved; independently checked by Frobenius. No Chow
consequence is inferred.** Let \(k\) be algebraically closed and let
\(a,b\in k^*\). There exist a smooth projective connected curve \(C/k\),
points \(P,Q\in C(k)\), and \(\xi\in K_2(C)\) such that
\[
                    P^*\xi-Q^*\xi=\{a,b\}\in K_2(k).
\]

If either input is 1 the symbol is zero and the claim is immediate. Otherwise
put \(c=(a-1)^2(b-1)^2/(ab)\ne0\), and consider in
\(\mathbb P^1_f\times\mathbb P^1_g\) the closure of
\[
                 (f-1)^2(g-1)^2=c f g.                 \tag{T.1}
\]
Choose a component through \((a,b)\) and normalize its reduced structure.
There are no vertical or horizontal components: substituting any fixed
value in either coordinate never makes the homogeneous equation identically
zero. Thus both coordinate maps on the normalization are nonconstant and
surjective. The only point of (T.1) on \(f=0\) is \((0,1)\), and the
derivative with respect to \(f\) there is \(-c\). Hence this is a smooth
point of the original curve, with one point \(Q\) on the normalization.
The normalization of the chosen integral component is a smooth proper
connected curve over the algebraically closed field. A point \(P\) over
\((a,b)\) exists even if that point of (T.1) is singular.

The boundary points of the curve have the following forms:
\[
 (f=0,g=1),\quad(f=\infty,g=1),\quad
 (g=0,f=1),\quad(g=\infty,f=1).
\]
Consequently every tame residue of \(\{f,g\}\in K_2(k(C))\) is 1.
The localization sequence for a smooth curve therefore lifts this symbol
to \(\xi\in K_2(C)\). The exact primary input is Quillen (1973), Section 7,
Proposition 3.2, equation (3.3), printed p.127, together with the filtered
limit over nonempty affine opens; the geometric Gersten theorem, Section 7,
Theorem 5.11, printed pp.133–134, gives the injectivity
\(K_2(\mathcal O_{C,x})\to K_2(k(C))\) needed to identify evaluations
with the following local expressions. These statements were directly
opened in the primary PDF, not inferred from a later survey.

Near \(Q\), the curve equation gives
\[
 f=u(g-1)^2,\qquad u=(f-1)^2/(cg)\in\mathcal O_{C,Q}^*.
\]
The Steinberg relation shows
\(\{g-1,g\}=\{g,-1\}\), which is killed by 2. Thus
\(\{f,g\}=\{u,g\}\) in the function field, hence also for its unique
local lift. Its specialization at \(Q\) is \(\{u(Q),1\}=0\).
At \(P\), both functions are units and specialization is \(\{a,b\}\).
This proves the lemma. The proof also applies in characteristic 2: the
derivative \(-c\) at \(Q\) remains nonzero and the same Steinberg identity
holds. The integral statement for field \(K_2\) uses the usual Matsumoto
presentation of \(K_2(k)\) by Steinberg symbols.

This prevents treating a bare \(K_2(k)\) summand in a prospective
normal-crossing calculation as automatically rigid under proper parameter
curves. It does **not** show that a family on an exceptional divisor lifts
to its regular resolution. It also does not assert that all higher Quillen
\(K_n(k)\) are generated by Milnor symbols. There is, however, a precise
further consequence: for n>=2, multiplying the constructed class by
constant classes \(\{a_3,\ldots,a_n\}\) realizes the image of every
Milnor symbol \(\{a_1,\ldots,a_n\}\) in Quillen K-theory as an actual
proper-curve evaluation difference. Additivity treats a finite sum.
Thus no Milnor-symbol term in a prospective top-weight exceptional-divisor
descent calculation is automatically rigid. The map from that exceptional
calculation to actual fixed-product families on the puncture is still
required; no such map is constructed by this observation alone.

## 3. The analogous absolute-differential spanning statement

**Auxiliary statement proved at the nilpotent-parameter level.** In
characteristic zero, every element of \(\Omega^1_{k/\mathbb Q}\) is a sum
of differences of evaluations of global absolute 1-forms on smooth proper
curves. It suffices to treat \(a\,db\), since these generate the module.
There is nothing to prove if \(b\) is algebraic over \(\mathbb Q\).
Take the fixed elliptic curve \(E:y^2=x^3-x\) over
\(\overline{\mathbb Q}\), with its regular form \(\omega=dx/y\), and
choose \(P=(b,y)\in E(k)\), where \(y^2=b^3-b\). Since \(b\) is
transcendental, \(y\ne0\). If \(Q\) is an algebraic point, then
\[
          P^*(a y\omega)-Q^*(a y\omega)=a\,db.
\]
The coefficient \(ay\) is a scalar multiplying the differential; no
differentiation of that scalar is implicit. The absolute form \(\omega\)
is the base extension of the algebraic invariant differential, and it
vanishes on pullback to an algebraic point because
\(\Omega^1_{\overline{\mathbb Q}/\mathbb Q}=0\).

For \(E_\epsilon=E\times\operatorname{Spec}k[\epsilon]/\epsilon^2\),
the calculation in C.3ab identifies
\(\mathcal K_2(E_\epsilon,E)=\Omega^1_{E/\mathbb Q}\).
The dimension-one descent spectral sequence has only columns 0 and 1,
so its edge map
\[
 K_2(E_\epsilon,E)\longrightarrow
 H^0(E,\mathcal K_2(E_\epsilon,E))
\]
is surjective. Consequently the preceding differences of forms really
are evaluations of actual relative \(K_2\) classes on the fixed proper
curve times the dual numbers. The same finite-order argument works for
each specified positive homogeneous degree of a split truncated polynomial
thickening. This is still a statement about finite thickenings.

## 4. The missing actual-family step

For a complex surface cone, the new part that C.3ab does not eliminate
comes from the absolute coefficient term in
\[
0\to\Omega^1_{k/\mathbb Q}\otimes\mathcal O_V
 \to\Omega^1_{V/\mathbb Q}\to\Omega^1_{V/k}\to0.
\]
The cohomological candidate is a quotient of
\(\Omega^1_{k/\mathbb Q}\otimes H^2(V,L^j)\); the relative term in
\(H^2(\Omega^1_{V/k}L^j)\) vanishes by Kodaira–Akizuki–Nakano.
The elliptic-curve construction explains why an assertion that all such
classes are constant on proper parameter curves would be false at the
differential level. It does not prove that the corresponding class on
\(V_a\times E\) is the restriction of a class on \(Z\times E\).

The two exact missing steps are: (i) construct the compatible classes or
objects on all exceptional thickenings, with the desired endpoint classes;
(ii) algebraize a family on the **actual** fixed product and, in the
henselian case, descend it from the completion. Finite-level injectivity of
\(K_0(Z)\to K_0(V_a)\), proved in C.3ab, alone implies neither step and
does not imply injectivity after quotienting by algebraic equivalence.

This is not an abstract precaution without a counterexample. Bloch,
Esnault, and Kerz (2014), *Deformation of algebraic cycle classes in
characteristic zero*, Appendix B, Proposition B.5, directly proves that
\(K_0(Y_{\mathbb C[[t]]})_\mathbb Q\to
(\varprojlim K_0(Y_{\mathbb C[t]/t^n}))\otimes\mathbb Q\)
is not surjective for a smooth projective even-dimensional \(Y/\mathbb Q\)
with positive geometric genus. Their Lemma B.7 nevertheless realizes the
specified finite-order differential contributions by actual moving point
sections. The counterexample assembles these with unbounded differential
complexity across orders. Both the complete statement and proof of
Proposition B.5 and Lemmas B.2, B.3, B.7 were read in the primary text.

The ample conormal bundle in the cone problem makes the relevant coherent
cohomology vanish in sufficiently high degree, unlike the trivial
\(\mathbb C[[t]]\) deformation in that counterexample. This is a possible
route to a stronger theorem, **not a proof** of a uniform algebraization
statement. Ordinary zero-cycle moving on a resolution and a proper-parameter
version of finite-order algebraization remain the next precise targets.

## 5. Literature examined and rejected in this scope

1. Kohrita (2024), *The algebraic part of motivic cohomology*, arXiv
   1512.02320v3, 10 April 2024, Theorem 1.1, Definition 2.3 and Proposition
   2.10: the general motivic equivalence uses smooth parameters and
   semiabelian varieties. It cannot silently replace actual proper-curve
   families on our non-finite-type puncture.
2. Kerz–Saito–Tamme (2019), *K-theory of non-Archimedean rings I*,
   Documenta Mathematica 24, 1365–1411, Section 1.1, p.1366: directly read
   the formulation of the \(K_0\)-algebraization problem and the warning
   referring to BEK Appendix B. Formal GAGA for coherent sheaves is not
   surjectivity on an inverse limit of Grothendieck groups.
3. Results of Lüders, Esnault–Wittenberg/Bloch, Saito–Sato and
   Binda–Krishna on relative zero-cycles over henselian **DVRs** do not
   directly apply to a resolution proper over the higher-dimensional
   local ring in this question. Lüders (2019), *Algebraization for
   zero-cycles and the p-adic cycle class map*, Mathematical Research
   Letters26(2),557-585, was subsequently opened in full at
   https://arxiv.org/pdf/1709.05622 (v3,28October2019). Theorem1.2,
   Proposition5.1 and its proof, and Corollary5.4, authorpp1-2,9-12,
   were read directly. The introduction and Section5 explicitly assume
   a smooth projective scheme over an excellent henselian DVR, despite
   the abstract's broader phrase “henselian local ring.” Its idelic
   proof may motivate a separately proved endpoint extension, but
   would still not give the one-dimension-higher proper-parameter
   family required here. The other listed works were search leads;
   no theorem from their unopened full text is claimed as an input.
4. A prospective normal-crossing exceptional divisor with spherical dual
   complex could produce a field \(K_n\) term in a descent computation.
   The resolution, exact differentials and actual-family survival must
   all be proved. The lemma in Section 2 defeats the simplest claimed
   rigidity for the \(K_2\) case. No top counterexample is asserted.

## 6. Additional local-cycle literature check

Dao and Kurano, *Hochster's theta pairing and numerical equivalence*,
full primary author PDF at
https://www.isc.meiji.ac.jp/~kurano/ronbun/kurano32.pdf, was opened.
Remark6.4, authorpp20-21, proves that its actual-product notion of
algebraic equivalence implies numerical equivalence. Its local numerical
Chow groups and Conjecture3.2 are not the ordinary quotient sought here;
the numerical factorization supplies no missing ordinary-family theorem.
Only these exact portions, not the entire paper, were read in this pass.

Ceresa and Collino (1983), *Some remarks on algebraic equivalence of cycles*,
Pacific Journal of Mathematics105(2),285-290, was also opened in the primary
9-page scan. The introduction and Theorem1.1 with its proof, pp285-287,
concern exceptional lines and Abel-Jacobi on projective nodal threefolds.
Their statement does not assert survival after localization at the node,
and no such local consequence is inferred. The result is therefore not
used as a counterexample to the top local quotient.

## 7. Descent of an already existing actual K0-family from completion

**Auxiliary reduction proved at the K0 level; this does not construct the
missing completed family.** Let (R,m,k) be an excellent henselian local
ring with a specified coefficient field k equal to its residue field.
Let Z be a quasi-compact, quasi-separated R-scheme, and write
\(\widehat Z=Z\times_R\widehat R\). For either scheme W, define
\(A_{K_0}(W)\) to be the subgroup of K0 of perfect complexes generated by
\[
 i_{t_1}^*\beta-i_{t_0}^*\beta,
 \qquad \beta\in K_0(W\times_k T),
\]
where T is a smooth projective connected k-curve and t0,t1 are k-points.
All fibre pullbacks are derived pullbacks of perfect complexes. Then
\[
 K_0(Z)/A_{K_0}(Z)\longrightarrow
 K_0(\widehat Z)/A_{K_0}(\widehat Z)
\]
is injective, integrally.

Indeed the completion map R→Rhat is regular by excellence. Popescu's
theorem writes Rhat as a filtered colimit of smooth finitely presented
R-algebras A_i (Stacks, Theorem16.12.1, Tag07GC). Suppose the image of
alpha in K0(Zhat) is a finite sum of the indicated fibre differences.
Keep the finitely many curves and points fixed over k. Each of the
systems Z_(A_i) and Z_(A_i)×_kT has quasi-compact, quasi-separated terms
and affine transition maps. The category of perfect complexes commutes
with such inverse limits (Stacks, Lemma36.29.3, Tag09RF; its exact
hypotheses are Situation36.29.1, Tag09RC). Thus the finitely many
perfect complexes representing all beta descend to one A_i. Increasing
i further also descends the equality in K0: by the presentation of K0
of a triangulated category, that equality uses only finitely many
isomorphisms and distinguished-triangle relations. Morphisms and their
cones descend by the same limit theorem. Consequently the desired
finite sum of fibre differences equals the pullback of alpha already
in K0(Z_(A_i)).

The structure map A_i→Rhat induces an R-algebra map A_i→k. Since A_i is
smooth over R and (R,m) is a henselian pair, this map lifts to an
R-algebra map A_i→R (Stacks, Lemma15.13.3, Tag0H74). Pull back every
perfect complex and every descended relation along this one map.
The resulting objects lie on the actual products Z×_kT, and their
fibre differences sum to alpha in K0(Z). This proves the assertion.
The maps to R preserve the fixed coefficient field because they are
R-algebra maps; no extension or change of parameter fields occurs.

The argument concerns **actual** objects over the completion. It does
not turn a compatible sequence of K0 classes on exceptional thickenings
into an object or class over Zhat×T, and makes no claim of K0 continuity
for that inverse limit. It also does not assert, without a separately
verified Riemann–Roch comparison, that the analogous Chow quotient for
an arbitrary non-finite-type regular scheme equals this K0 quotient.
For the top problem, this isolates the harder step as the construction
of an actual completed family, rather than its subsequent descent to
the henselian base. The exact Stacks statements and relevant proofs
were opened on19November2026; this reduction is not counted as a solved
case of the top integral finiteness problem.

The auxiliary proper-curve symbol lemma was independently checked by
Frobenius on 19 November 2026. The root session supplied the elliptic-form
spanning construction; this record checks its exact nilpotent \(K\)-theory
scope. These checks do not change the status of the unrestricted C.3 top
quotient, and this record is not counted as main-gate movement.
